# Review backlog

Should-fix findings left unapplied by the adversarial review rounds of 2026-09-13. Every must-fix from the same rounds was applied and is in the history; these are quality and hardening items, confirmed by a reviewer but not required to pass the gates. Generated from the workflow outputs of the implementation session; file and line references are as of the commit each round reviewed and may have drifted.


Total items: 964. Generated 2026-09-13; feature 003, 004 and 005 sections appended 2026-09-16, the handoff rounds the same day. Feature 006 spec review appended 2026-09-17.


## Pre-test diagnostics (items 1, 3, 4, 8)


### 4-check-interference (11)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:628** - Unreported deviation from the item text. The item's correction (1) says to use "_finding_lines per group (reaching group['result']['finding'])", but the implementation calls `_finding_lines(group["result"])`. I verified the implementation is the correct one -- `check_interference_group` returns a `CheckResult` (reviewer/src/swreview/checks/result.py:50-65: check/status/severity/observed/requirement/inputs/calculation/coverage_limits/recommended_action), so there is no `finding` key and the item's parenthetical would have raised KeyError. The implementer's report nonetheless says "Deviations from the item text: none in behavior", so the wrong parenthetical is now unflagged and a later reader could "fix" the code back into a crash.
  - Fix: Record the deviation explicitly for the orchestrator: the item's `group['result']['finding']` is factually wrong; `group['result']` IS the finding-shaped CheckResult dict. No code change.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:883** - DRY violation against Principle V ("repeated logic across checks, parsers, or scripts MUST be extracted once it appears twice with the same intent"). `INTERFERENCE_FIXTURE = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "golden" / "fixtures" / "bracket-assy-interference"` is a verbatim copy of `FIXTURE_DIR` at reviewer/tests/unit/test_checks_interference.py:38-40. test_cli.py already imports fixtures across test modules (`from tests.unit.test_tools_checks_fastener import joint_package`, line 32), so the duplication is also inconsistent with the file's own convention. If the golden fixture is ever renamed or moved, two files must change and only one is obvious.
  - Fix: Import the existing constant (`from tests.unit.test_checks_interference import FIXTURE_DIR`) or move it to tests/support/, and keep the new docstring on the local alias.
- **specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/cli.md:18** - Contract drift: the row still reads ``swreview check fit\|stack\|fastener\|alignment`` and does not list the new `interference` subcommand, so the CLI contract no longer describes the CLI. Not a scope violation by this item -- the orchestrator restricted spec edits to files the item names, and item 4 names none -- but the drift is real and nothing in the test suite catches it (no test reads contracts/cli.md).
  - Fix: Route to the item that owns contracts/cli.md (rank 9): add `interference` to the check row, noting `--package <dir>`, `--json`, and that it is read-only (never writes exceptions.json).
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:957** - The read-only guarantee is asserted only for `exceptions.json`. The item's sentence is broader ("it must stay read-only"); `interference_case` also calls `load_package(directory)`, and nothing pins that the command leaves the rest of the package directory (package.json, case.json, answer key) untouched.
  - Fix: In test_check_interference_leaves_the_exceptions_file_byte_for_byte, snapshot every file under the copied directory (path -> sha256 or read_bytes) before the run and compare the whole mapping afterwards, instead of one file.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:613** - No test covers the empty/boundary package: a package with zero interferences and no exceptions.json beside it. I confirmed the code path is safe (ExceptionStore.load() treats an absent file as "none yet", reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:221-229, and the text builder degrades to "0 interference condition(s)" / "0 exception(s) after refresh" / "unresolved coverage: 0 item(s)"), but Principle III asks for boundary-condition tests and this is the shape every freshly dumped package will have on test day -- the very scenario the item's "why" is written for.
  - Fix: Add one test over a minimal package (e.g. the existing joint_package fixture directory, which has no exceptions.json) asserting exit 0 and the three zero-count lines.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:627** - Undisclosed deviation from the item text. The item says to use "_finding_lines per group (reaching group['result']['finding'])"; the code passes `group["result"]` directly. The code is right and the item text is wrong - `check_interference_group` returns a `CheckResult` (src/swreview/checks/result.py:50), which IS the finding-shaped record, so `group['result']['finding']` would KeyError exactly the way the item warned `_emit_check` would. But the implementer's report says "Deviations from the item text: none in behavior" and lists only two unrelated notes, so the next reader has no record that the plan's parenthetical is wrong.
  - Fix: Record the deviation in the handoff: "item says group['result']['finding']; CheckResult is itself the finding record, so _finding_lines(group['result']) is the correct call and the item's parenthetical is an error." No code change.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:883** - DRY: INTERFERENCE_FIXTURE duplicates, character for character, the path expression already defined as FIXTURE_DIR at tests/unit/test_checks_interference.py:38-40 (`Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "golden" / "fixtures" / "bracket-assy-interference"`). Constitution Principle V: "repeated logic across checks, parsers, or scripts MUST be extracted once it appears twice with the same intent", and CLAUDE.md says to flag repetition aggressively. test_cli.py already imports fixtures across test modules (lines 32-33, `from tests.unit.test_tools_checks_fastener import joint_package`), so the convention for sharing exists. I left this in should_fix rather than must_fix because a path constant is data, not logic, and the duplicate is inert.
  - Fix: `from tests.unit.test_checks_interference import FIXTURE_DIR as INTERFERENCE_FIXTURE` and keep the explanatory docstring as a comment, or drop the local constant entirely.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:897** - test_check_interference_json_is_the_library_case asserts `body == interference_case(interference_package_dir)` against a command whose body is one call to interference_case - a delegation identity that cannot fail while the wrapper compiles, and that pins none of the numbers the item's "why" cares about ("the unverified interference volume unit"). It is what the verification field asked for, and the content is genuinely pinned elsewhere (tests/golden/test_golden/bracket-assy-interference.yml lines 49-53 carry the mm3 volumes through the inline-kwargs branch, with the directory/inline equivalence proved by test_checks_interference.py:414-431), but nothing in this module says so, so a future reader may mistake it for content coverage.
  - Fix: Either add the cross-reference to the test's docstring ("content is pinned by tests/golden/test_golden/bracket-assy-interference.yml; this asserts only that the CLI emits the library case verbatim"), or add one substantive assertion here, e.g. `assert "largest overlap volume 4.1 mm3" in body["groups"][0]["result"]["observed"]`, so a unit regression fails at the CLI too.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:1005** - No coverage of the empty case: a package with zero interferences (every dump before the console `interference` command has merged rows) prints the bare header `0 interference condition(s)` / `0 exception(s) after refresh` / `unresolved coverage: 0 item(s)`. That is the correct, loud output, but it is the state most real invocations will hit first and no test fixes it.
  - Fix: Reuse an existing zero-interference package (e.g. the joint_package_dir fixture already in this module) and assert exit 0 plus the three zero-count lines.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:631** - Cosmetic inconsistency in the text block separation: a blank line is emitted before each group (line 620) and before the exceptions header (line 628), but not before `unresolved coverage:`, so the unresolved-coverage block runs straight on from the exception list. Principle I makes unresolved coverage the thing that must stay visible; it is the one block with no visual break.
  - Fix: `lines.append("")` before the `unresolved coverage:` line, and extend test_check_interference_human_output_lists_exceptions_and_unresolved_coverage if you want the separation asserted.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:883** - Stale gate line in the handoff report, not in the code: the report quotes `Passed! - Failed: 0, Passed: 419` for `dotnet test extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release`. On the tree as it stands now that command reports `Failed: 0, Passed: 495, Skipped: 0, Total: 495` (other items in the shared tree added C# tests). Still green, and this item touches no C#, but the quoted line does not describe the tree it was reported against.
  - Fix: Re-quote the C# gate from a run on the current tree, or state that the C# gates were run before the other items landed.

### 1-attach-only (8)

- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Console/Program.cs:68** - The started-session log line asserts '--allow-start was given and nothing was running.' The second clause is not established: `started` only means `Marshal.GetActiveObject` threw, which the whole item argues does NOT mean nothing was running. With `--allow-start` set, an elevation mismatch now starts a second seat AND writes a false cause into extract.log - reintroducing, one layer up, the exact mis-statement the item was raised to delete from `TryGetRunning`'s comment. The `DescribeAttachFailure()` machinery that knows the truth is not consulted on this path.
  - Fix: Reword to what is known, e.g. 'Started a new SOLIDWORKS session: --allow-start was given and no instance was visible to COM from this process.' Better: on the allowStart path, log `DescribeAttachFailure()` as a warning line before creating the seat, so an engineer who finds an unexpected second seat in extract.log can see the elevated one it failed to attach to. No new test needed beyond an assertion on the wording if one is added.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/CommandLineOptionsTests.cs:305** - `AllowStart_IsAcceptedByEveryCommandThatAttaches` cannot fail for any change to Program.cs. Its comment claims 'Program.cs calls SwAttach.Connect from all five commands, so all five must accept the flag; an option missing from one command's list is a usage error at run time', but the test builds its own local `InterferenceOptions`/`CaptureOptions`/`ServeOptions` copies and feeds them to `CommandLine.Parse` - it never touches `Program.KnownOptions` or `Program.*OptionNames`. Deleting `KnownOptions(...)` from one command leaves the suite green. It also covers only 3 of the 5 commands: `resolve` is absent entirely (no `ResolveOptions` array exists) and `dump` is only covered incidentally by the neighbouring facts. Separately, the four test-local arrays are now verbatim copies of Program.cs's five arrays plus 'allow-start' - Principle V DRY: the five-way repetition the implementer's deviation #2 claims to have removed has moved into the test file.
  - Fix: Make `Program.KnownOptions` (or the five `*OptionNames` fields) `internal` and add `[assembly: InternalsVisibleTo("SwReview.Extractor.Tests")]` to the Console project, then drive the theory from the real arrays: `[InlineData("dump")] [InlineData("resolve")] [InlineData("interference")] [InlineData("capture")] [InlineData("serve")]`, parsing with `Program.KnownOptions(<the real array>)`. That deletes the four duplicated arrays and makes the test able to fail. If exposing internals is judged too invasive, at minimum add `ResolveOptions`, include `dump` and `resolve` in the theory, and rewrite the comment so it does not claim wiring coverage it does not have.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Sw/SwSession.cs:169** - `FileLoadErrors.Describe` is wired only into the `document == null` failure message. OpenDoc6 sets the `warnings` out-param almost exclusively on a SUCCESSFUL open, so the entire `DescribeWarnings` half of the new, well-tested decoder is dead in production: a document that opens carrying `swFileLoadWarning_MissingExternalReferences` or `swFileLoadWarning_ModelOutOfDate` means the dump describes an incomplete or stale model, and that fact is currently dropped with no log line and no gap. Principle I: 'Unsupported, missing, truncated, and failed checks MUST remain visible in the report as unresolved coverage, never silently dropped.' The implementer flagged this honestly as deviation #4 and out of scope, which I agree with for THIS item - the item's 'printing' target was the one failure message.
  - Fix: Do not widen this item. Raise a follow-up item: surface a non-zero `warnings` from a successful `OpenDoc6` as a `GapCollector` gap (the decode function it needs already exists and is fully tested), which needs a gap sink plumbed into `SwSession.Attach`/`OpenReadOnly`.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Sw/SwSession.cs:163** - The decode is applied only when OpenDoc6 returns null. On a SUCCESSFUL open the warnings int is read into a local and then discarded: a document that opens carrying swFileLoadWarning_ModelOutOfDate (0x2000) or swFileLoadWarning_MissingExternalReferences (0x100000) is dumped with no record anywhere that the IR describes a stale or incomplete model. Principle I: "Unsupported, missing, truncated, and failed checks MUST remain visible in the report as unresolved coverage, never silently dropped." The item says "Decode OpenDoc6's error/warning ints" without restricting to the failure branch. The implementer flagged this honestly as a follow-up; the loss is pre-existing rather than introduced here, which is why this is should_fix and not must_fix.
  - Fix: At minimum, when warnings != 0 on a successful open, surface FileLoadErrors.DescribeWarnings(warnings) rather than dropping it - e.g. expose it on SwSession as a read-only property the console host logs, so the wording stays testable and no new sink is invented. A gap entry is the right long-term home but GapCollector.cs belongs to another in-flight item, so keep it out of this change.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Console/AttachFailure.cs:116** - The unreadable branch states an inference as fact, and can state a wrong one. It is reached whenever EITHER the session id or the integrity level is null, but the text says "cannot read its Windows session or token, which itself means it runs elevated or as another user." For SwProcessFacts(pid, sessionId: null, integrityLevel: "medium") - covered by Describe_SessionUnreadable_IsTreatedAsUnreadableNotAsAMatch at AttachFailureTests.cs:104 - the token WAS readable and read the same integrity level as ours, so "runs elevated" is contradicted by a fact already in hand. Principle I forbids inferring a favourable/definite value from a missing input. The existing test only asserts Contains("cannot read"), so it passes on the wrong sentence.
  - Fix: Split the branch: name only the fact that was actually unreadable, and assert elevation only when IntegrityLevel is the null one ("this process cannot open its token, which happens exactly when it runs elevated or as another user"); for an unreadable session id with a matching integrity level, say the session could not be read and stop. Strengthen the two tests to assert the specific clause rather than the shared substring.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Console/SwAttach.cs:106** - Process.GetProcessesByName can itself throw (Win32Exception/InvalidOperationException on a machine with broken performance counters or a restricted token), and Process.GetCurrentProcess() at line 101 is never disposed. DescribeAttachFailure has no guard, so a secondary failure while GATHERING the diagnosis replaces the item's whole deliverable - the cause sentence - with an unrelated exception message on the one path that matters most. Every other read in this file is individually guarded (SessionIdOf, IntegrityLevelOf), so the omission is inconsistent with its own style.
  - Fix: Wrap the enumeration in try/catch for Win32Exception and InvalidOperationException and fall back to AttachFailure.Describe with an empty process list plus a note that the process list could not be read, so the engineer always gets a message naming the ProgID and --allow-start. Put `self` in a using. Add an AttachFailureTests case for the empty-list-because-unreadable wording.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Sw/SwSession.cs:144** - The item dictates this message's wording verbatim ('not open in SOLIDWORKS and not found on disk from this process') and the item's verification prescribes the method: "Extract the pure pieces and test those." The FileLoadErrors decode next to it was extracted and has 20 tests; this string was left inline in a class the implementer correctly notes no test touches, so the one piece of wording the item specifies letter-for-letter is verified by reading only. It is a pure function of a path.
  - Fix: Move it to a one-line pure helper beside FileLoadErrors (e.g. FileLoadErrors.DescribeMissingDocument(string path)) and add two tests: one asserting the mandated clause appears verbatim, one asserting the mapped-drive/EPDM/UNC guidance is present. SwSession then calls the helper, and the contract sentence is regression-guarded at near-zero cost.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/AttachFailureTests.cs:46** - Describe_NoSolidWorksProcess_SaysSoWithoutGuessing asserts DoesNotContain("session") and DoesNotContain("integrity") over the ENTIRE message, including the fixed prologue and the --allow-start advice that are not under test. It passes today only because that boilerplate happens to avoid both words; any later rewording of the --allow-start sentence that mentions a session breaks a test whose subject is the diagnosis clause. Separately, SwProcessFacts_KeepsWhatItWasGiven at line 152 asserts only that a three-property constructor assigns its three properties - no behaviour, no failure mode.
  - Fix: Have Diagnose's output be assertable on its own (make it internal + InternalsVisibleTo, or have Describe take the diagnosis as a seam) and scope the negative assertions to that clause. Delete SwProcessFacts_KeepsWhatItWasGiven - the constructor is already exercised by every other test in the file.

### 3-typename-census (9)

- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/HoleDumper.cs:131** - All three sites record the pass only AFTER the walk loop completes (HoleDumper.cs:131, MateDumper.cs:70, ComponentTreeDumper.cs:383). The `gate.Call("GetTypeName2", ...)` and `gate.Call("GetNextFeature", ...)` calls inside the loops are not wrapped in `TryStep`, so a COM failure mid-walk propagates to `RunPhase`, which records a phase gap and discards every sighting collected so far. That is precisely the moment the engineer most needs to know which type names were reached before the tree read died — the census exists to make exactly this class of failure diagnosable (Principle I: unsupported coverage stays visible).
  - Fix: Wrap each walk in try/finally and call `AddPass` in the `finally`, so a partial pass is still censused. Add a unit test at the census level is not needed; a single comment plus the finally at all three sites is the whole change.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PackageWriterTests.cs:117** - `Build_FeatureTypesNoDumperRead_BecomeOneUnsupportedGapPerDocument` asserts `Assert.Single(package.Gaps, g => g.EntityKind == "feature")` but `FakeSources.SeeFeatureTypes` (PackageWriterTests.cs:433) is hard-wired to a single `HousingPath` pass, so the "per document" half of the name is never exercised. The emit loop in `PackageWriter.AddSkippedFeatureTypes` (PackageWriter.cs:289-300) — one `Add` per `DocumentTypeNames`, each with its own `scope.DocumentId(document.DocumentPath)` — is only covered for n=1. A bug that emitted the first document's id on every gap would pass every test in the suite.
  - Fix: Let `SeeFeatureTypes` take the document path (or add a second setter for the assembly document) and add a test asserting two gaps, each with the entity_id of its own document and only its own type names in the reason.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/PackageWriter.cs:297** - The gap reason names the document with `Path.GetFileName(document.DocumentPath)` only. Two distinct files with the same name in different folders (routine in a vault with revised/copied parts) produce two gap lines that read identically in `swreview validate` output, distinguishable only by the opaque `doc:<12 hex>` entity_id. Principle VI requires a finding an engineer can reproduce; here they must cross-reference the hash against package.documents by hand.
  - Fix: Either include the full path in the reason, or keep the file name and append the parent folder (e.g. `bracket-assy\\housing.SLDPRT`). One-line change; the existing `Assert.Contains("housing.SLDPRT", ...)` assertions still hold.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/GapCollectorTests.cs:229** - `TypeNames_IsOneCensusEveryHolderOfTheCollectorSharesAndStartsEmpty` claims in its name to prove that every holder of the collector shares one census, but the body constructs one `GapCollector` and calls it directly — nothing in it demonstrates sharing. The property it actually pins (starts empty; a censused name is not itself a gap) is real and worth keeping; the name over-claims and will mislead the next reader into thinking sharing is covered.
  - Fix: Rename to what it tests (e.g. `TypeNames_StartsEmptyAndACensusedNameIsNotItselfAGap`), or make it earn the name by passing the same collector to two holders and asserting both see the other's pass.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/PackageWriter.cs:298** - The gap reason ends "SOLIDWORKS reported these GetTypeName2 names in the feature tree and no dumper reads them." - a present-tense causal claim about the code. It is false whenever the dump aborted: `aborted` is set by a `CircuitOpenError` in any earlier phase (PackageWriter.cs:140-180), the mate and hole phases are then skipped, and `AddSkippedFeatureTypes` still runs at line 188. The root assembly's pattern-walk pass has already censused `MateGroup` as unconsumed, so the gap will state that no dumper reads MateGroup (or HoleWzd, for a part root where the hole phase never ran). On test day that sentence points the engineer at a non-existent type-name constant bug - the precise misdiagnosis this item was written to prevent.
  - Fix: Either skip the census gap when the dump aborted (pass the `aborted` flag into `AddSkippedFeatureTypes`, and let the abort gap carry the coverage loss), or reword to the claim the data actually supports: "...and no dumper that ran in this dump read them." Add a PackageWriterTests case where a phase raises `CircuitOpenError` and assert whichever behaviour is chosen.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/TypeNameCensusTests.cs:194** - `Unconsumed_IsStableAcrossCalls` asserts `census.Unconsumed().Single().Describe()` equals itself across two calls, with a census holding exactly one name and no count ties. `Unconsumed()` is a pure function over a `Dictionary` + `OrderByDescending/ThenBy`, so with one entry there is no ordering left to be unstable - the assertion cannot fail for any implementation, including a broken one. Tie-break determinism is already covered properly by `Unconsumed_OrdersByCountThenName` (line 179), so this test adds no signal.
  - Fix: Either delete it, or give it teeth: seed at least two names with equal counts plus a third, and assert the full ordered `Names.Select(n => n.TypeName)` sequence is identical across two separate `Unconsumed()` calls (and that a call does not mutate the census - e.g. calling it twice still yields the same counts).
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PackageWriterTests.cs:118** - `Build_FeatureTypesNoDumperRead_BecomeOneUnsupportedGapPerDocument` names the per-document rule but only ever drives one document: `FakeSources.SeeFeatureTypes` appends to a single `_holePass` list that is always recorded against `HousingPath` (line 539). Every assertion in the file is `Assert.Single(package.Gaps, g => g.EntityKind == "feature")`, so the `foreach` in `AddSkippedFeatureTypes` and the per-document `scope.DocumentId(document.DocumentPath)` mapping are never exercised with more than one entry - an implementation that emitted one merged gap, or that reused the first document's id for every gap, would keep the suite green. The item's headline requirement is 'one gap per document'.
  - Fix: Let the fake record passes for two documents (e.g. `SeeFeatureTypes(path, ...)` recording `HousingPath` and `ScrewPath`), then assert two `entity_kind == "feature"` gaps, with distinct `EntityId`s matching each document's id and each reason naming its own file name and only its own type names.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/TypeNameCensus.cs:91** - The comment at line 87 claims the census is "Keyed the way DocumentIds hashes a path", but `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` only folds case, while `DocumentIds.Hash` also trims and replaces '/' with '\\' before lowering. Two spellings of one file that differ by separator or surrounding whitespace therefore become two `DocumentEntry` rows but one document id, and `AddSkippedFeatureTypes` emits two gaps carrying the *same* `entity_id` with split counts. `AddPass_TheSameFileSpeltDifferently_IsOneDocument` only tests the casing half, so the comment's claim is asserted only where it happens to hold.
  - Fix: Either normalize the key exactly as DocumentIds does (`path.Trim().Replace('/', '\\')` before the ignore-case lookup, keeping the first-seen spelling for display) and extend the test with a '/'-separated and a whitespace-padded spelling, or narrow the comment to what the code does ('case-insensitive, matching how SOLIDWORKS varies case') so it stops asserting agreement it does not have.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/PackageWriter.cs:293** - The gap pairs `entity_kind = "feature"` with `entity_id = scope.DocumentId(...)`, i.e. a `doc:` id. `Gap.EntityKind` is documented as "The IR entity type the gap is about" (Ir/DrawingSheet.cs:90) and every other gap in the extractor either carries a null id or an id of the kind it names (`"component"` + `cmp:`, `"hole"` + `hol:`). On the Python side `tools/context.py:126 entity_kind("doc:...")` answers "document", directly contradicting the gap's own label, and `swreview validate` prints the pair verbatim as `unsupported feature doc:0a1b...`. The schema does not catch it (`entity_kind` is a free string), so nothing else will.
  - Fix: Use `entity_kind = "document"` - the reason text already says these are feature type names, so no information is lost - or keep "feature" and set `entity_id` to null. Update the `g.EntityKind == "feature"` filters in PackageWriterTests accordingly.

### 8-pattern-names (7)

- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PatternFeatureTypesTests.cs:36** - Command (1) omits `moLocalLPattern_c`, which the item's "what" text explicitly names as part of the evidence set: "string extraction from ... sldasmu.dll shows internal feature classes moLocalLPattern_c, moLocalChainPattern_c, moDerivedCirPattern_c and moMirrorCompFeat_c". The recorded array searches the other three plus the negatives, so the anchor name — the one already-trusted entry that validates the whole mo<X>_c -> "<X>" inference — is the one name the recorded command does not cover.
  - Fix: Add 'moLocalLPattern_c' to the name array in the command at line 36 and add its output row (I reproduced it: `moLocalLPattern_c ascii=3 utf16=0`).
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PatternFeatureTypesTests.cs:60** - The strongest single piece of evidence in the file — that the LocalChainPattern hit sits in a contiguous UTF-16 run reproducing swFeatureNameID_e 103..113 in order — is asserted in prose only, with no recorded command that regenerates it. Every other claim in the block is backed by a pasteable command. I verified the claim is true (the run reads `CurvePattern SketchPattern APattern TablePattern DimPattern LocalLPattern LocalCirPattern LocalCurvePattern LocalSketchPattern LocalChainPattern GroundPlane`), but an auditor cannot re-derive it from what is checked in, which is the failure mode the item was trying to avoid by rejecting a hand-transcribed table.
  - Fix: Add the one-line command that prints the run, e.g. `powershell -NoProfile -Command "$b=[IO.File]::ReadAllBytes('...sldapiu.dll'); $u=[Text.Encoding]::Unicode.GetString($b); ([regex]::Match($u,'.{0,160}LocalChainPattern.{0,60}').Value -replace '[^\x20-\x7E]','|')"` above the quoted run.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PatternFeatureTypesTests.cs:128** - Reading the private `PatternFeatureTypes` field by reflection is a convention this test project does not otherwise use — no other source file under SwReview.Extractor.Tests/ references System.Reflection or BindingFlags — and it trades Principle V's "explicit beats clever" for keeping the production surface unchanged. It is also a weaker pin than it looks: it verifies the contents of a field, not that ReadPatternMembership consults that field, so a future change that inlines or bypasses the set leaves the test green. The tradeoff was disclosed and is defensible (widening to internal needs an InternalsVisibleTo in the csproj, which is outside this item's scope, and ComponentTreeDumper has no COM-free test seam today), so "do nothing" is a reasonable answer here.
  - Fix: Either accept as-is and add one sentence to the class doc noting that the pin covers the constant only, not its use at ReadPatternMembership; or, if the csproj is opened for another reason, make the field `internal static readonly`, add `[assembly: InternalsVisibleTo("SwReview.Extractor.Tests")]`, and drop the reflection helper.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/ComponentTreeDumper.cs:57** - The TablePattern justification comment states the component patterns are "the Local* family 108-112 plus the Derived* pair and MirrorComponent 116". The file's own cited evidence refutes "the Derived* pair" under both readings: swFeatureNameID_e contains only swFmDerivedLPattern=28 (no swFmDerivedCirPattern at all, as the test file itself notes), and an exhaustive ASCII scan of sldasmu.dll for mo*(Pattern|Mirror)*_c returns moDerivedLPattern_c(2), moDerivedCirPattern_c(2), moDerivedCurvePattern_c(2), moDerivedSketchPattern_c(2), moDerivedTablePattern_c(2), moDerivedAPattern_c(2), moDerivedHolePattern_c(1) — a family of at least seven, not a pair. Since the set carries only DerivedLPattern/DerivedCirPattern, the pattern-driven ("derived") component patterns built from a part's curve, sketch or table pattern are plausibly the same silent-miss this item exists to fix, and the comment currently asserts they do not exist. This is the file's evidence trail, which the implementer's own deviation 1 argues must stay true.
  - Fix: Correct the sentence to what the evidence shows (e.g. "the Local* family 108-112, the Derived* family — of which only DerivedLPattern and DerivedCirPattern are in this set — and MirrorComponent 116"). Do NOT add the other Derived* names: the item's "what" authorizes exactly two additions. Report the gap to the orchestrator as a candidate follow-up item, and note in the comment that the rank 3 census is what would surface DerivedCurvePattern / DerivedSketchPattern / DerivedTablePattern on a real model.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PatternFeatureTypesTests.cs:61** - The provenance comment claims the contiguous UTF-16 run in sldapiu.dll "follows swFeatureNameID_e exactly - \"CurvePattern SketchPattern APattern TablePattern DimPattern ...\", i.e. ids 103..113 in order". The run reproduces verbatim, but it is contradicted by the enum the same file prints three paragraphs later: id 105 is swFmFillPattern, while the run has APattern. "Exactly" is refuted by the file's own two commands; a reader auditing the evidence hits the mismatch and loses confidence in the rest. (Checked further: the run also stops at GroundPlane 113 then continues SMGusset/Belt/StrctSys*, so it does not track the enum past 113 either — 114/115/116 are absent.)
  - Fix: Downgrade the claim to what is true: the run is positionally aligned with ids 103..113 except position 105, which the API table spells APattern where swFeatureNameID_e spells swFmFillPattern, and the run does not continue in enum order past GroundPlane 113. The load-bearing part of the evidence — LocalChainPattern sitting at the 112 position — survives that correction intact.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PatternFeatureTypesTests.cs:119** - Test name PatternFeatureTypes_IsExactlyTheSetTheProvenanceSupports overclaims relative to the file's own findings. The provenance does not support "ChainPatternFeat" at all — moChainPatternFeat_c ascii=0 in sldasmu.dll and 0 boundary hits in sldapiu.dll (I also confirmed 0 raw UTF-16 occurrences of the substring anywhere in sldapiu.dll) — and does not support "DerivedCirPattern" from the enum. Those names are kept deliberately, as the body text honestly explains, so the set is "provenance plus names kept because removing a possibly-live name regresses grouping", not "the set the provenance supports". Under Constitution I (evidence before conclusions) the assertion name is the first thing a reader takes as the claim.
  - Fix: Rename to something that states the actual guarantee, e.g. PatternFeatureTypes_MatchesThePinnedSet or PatternFeatureTypes_IsThePinnedSetWithRecordedProvenance, and leave the per-name reasoning where it already is.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PatternFeatureTypesTests.cs:36** - Provenance command (1) checks six class names and omits moLocalLPattern_c, which the item's "what" names as part of the citable extraction ("string extraction from ... sldasmu.dll shows internal feature classes moLocalLPattern_c, moLocalChainPattern_c, moDerivedCirPattern_c and moMirrorCompFeat_c"). The recorded evidence therefore does not cover the one name in the set that the pilot fixture will actually exercise, so the mo<X>_c -> "<X>" correspondence the comment relies on is never demonstrated for a name known to work.
  - Fix: Add 'moLocalLPattern_c' (and, cheaply, the rest of the Local* family) to the name list in command (1) and record the counts — moLocalLPattern_c is present with ascii=3, so the paste-and-compare stays green and the "Local* family" claim becomes self-verifying.

## Feature 002 spec package review


### spec, plan, contracts, tasks (44)

- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The generated Codex profile deliberately enables the CLI's shell tool. That hands the model generic command execution on the workstation (read-only filesystem, but arbitrary processes), which the constitution bans outright, and the plan's Constitution Check marks that same gate PASS with an empty Complexity Tracking table. Nothing in the feature discloses or justifies the exception.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F2 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The per-launch tool-service secret is written in cleartext into a file inside the run folder, and the process that can read that folder is exactly the sandboxed CLI the secret is meant to constrain. That contradicts the plan's own rule and defeats FR-019/FR-022: with the secret plus the pipe name (also in the same file, `--bridge-pipe <pipe>`), the general-chat model can talk to the in-process dispatcher directly and issue any command the dispatcher accepts — including `interference`, which mcp-toolset.md deliberately withholds from general chat.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F3 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/pane-host-messages.md** - Saving settings restarts the backend process unconditionally, which kills any chat session it is hosting: the SSE stream drops, the in-flight review turn dies, and the session on disk is left without an ended time — exactly what FR-008 and US2 Acceptance 3 forbid. Nothing in the contract or tasks defines the interaction between a settings save and a running turn.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F9 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/settings.schema.json** - The quickstart's primary US1 validation and T044 both require selecting the `fake` provider in the Settings UI and pressing Review, but the settings contract cannot store `fake`, and T026 tests the schema round trip. The demonstration path the plan relies on (deliver US2 and US1 'with the fake provider so the pane is demonstrable without keys') is therefore blocked by its own contract.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F10 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - The 16000 token ceiling is copied from an Anthropic-specific constraint that no longer applies, and no event, coverage bucket, or task covers the failure mode it creates. On a reasoning model, reasoning tokens count against `max_output_tokens`, so a turn can end with `status: incomplete` / `incomplete_details.reason = max_output_tokens` — a truncated answer that is not an exception and not a tool error. `turn.ended` has no reason for it (`end | max_steps | error | stopped`), so the runner would report a normal end and the report would show a review that simply stopped, violating Principle I's rule that truncated work stays visible.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F12 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/data-model.md** - FR-014 requires effort to be mapped to each provider's own control and the mapping recorded, but no artifact states the mapping table, and the four inherited levels include `xhigh`, which research itself flags as model-dependent for OpenAI and which has no counterpart in Gemini's thinking level. T017/T019 assert only that 'effort mapping is recorded', so a mapping that silently collapses xhigh to high, or that sends a value the provider rejects mid-run, would pass the tests.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F13 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/mcp-toolset.md** - The general-chat path gets a second implementation of the call choke point with nothing specifying that it reuses the first. RecordedTool is where argument validation, step recording, the `{"error": ...}`-to-error-result conversion, and the never-raise-past-the-runner rule all live; the MCP server needs the same four behaviours against a different sink. T059 simply says 'implement', so the two paths can drift — and a divergence here means a failed tool call in general chat is dropped rather than surfaced, which FR-022/SC-003 forbid.
  - Fix: see the finding id constitution:F14 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/settings.schema.json** - The settings schema forbids the `fake` provider, but quickstart Scenario 1 and task T044 require saving `fake` in Settings, and T026 validates the written settings file against this schema. The two cannot both hold.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F02 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/chat-api.md** - The stop endpoint and its corresponding `turn.ended` reason have no producer: no FR requires a stop control, no page-host message carries one, and no task builds one.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F08 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/data-model.md** - The data-model event table disagrees with the JSON Schema it describes, so whichever an implementer reads first produces events the other rejects.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F09 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The generated Codex profile hands the general-chat model a shell, which the constitution's technical constraints prohibit, while the plan's Constitution Check records that gate as PASS with an empty Complexity Tracking table. The Gemini profile denies shell, so the two modes also enforce different boundaries for the same FR.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F11 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - The terminal's working directory is "the run folder", but the only task that creates a run folder is part of the review flow, so a Terminal-first session has no defined working directory and no task defines how one is chosen or created.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F13 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - An acceptance scenario requires re-evaluating findings after an evidence answer, and no task implements or tests that behaviour.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F16 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - Two named edge cases have no implementing task and, for the retry case, no data field to carry what the spec promises.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F17 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - Five of the nine measurable outcomes have no task that measures them, and SC-004 names a "guard log" artifact that no contract, data-model entry, or task defines.
  - Fix: see the finding id consistency:F18 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The per-launch bridge secret and the bridge pipe name are written in cleartext into files under `<run_dir>/.swreview-cli/`, which is the sandboxed CLI's own working directory and is readable by it. The restriction profile grants the CLI read access to the run folder on purpose (Codex `[features] shell_tool = true` "for reading the run folder"; the Gemini policy allows `read_file` and `list_directory` at priority 50). The general-chat model can therefore read the credential that authenticates to the in-process SOLIDWORKS dispatcher. Because the dispatcher's vocabulary is `ping|capture|measure|interference` and authorization is a single shared secret, anything holding that secret can issue `interference` — the exact command mcp-toolset.md says is excluded "by construction" (FR-025, FR-022). The exclusion lives only in the MCP server layer, not at the trust boundary. `chat-api.md` line 16-17 compounds it: `POST /sessions` carries `bridge: {pipe, secret}` and `GET /sessions/{chat_id}` is specified to withhold only `token`, so the bridge secret is echoed back to the WebView2 page as part of session state.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-001 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The Gemini restriction rests on an environment variable the contract itself will not name ("or the documented equivalent for a settings directory override"), and nothing in the task list proves the restriction is actually in force. If the override name is wrong, Gemini loads the engineer's `~/.gemini/settings.json` and `~/.gemini/policies/`, our deny-all policy is absent, and the CLI runs with its full core toolset (`write_file`, `run_shell_command`, `web_fetch`) against the customer's machine — a silent fail-open with no detection. The specified first-launch verification only compares the *MCP tool listing* to the allowlist, so it detects a missing `swreview` server but not the presence of unrestricted core tools. Across both CLIs, the sole check that a write is refused is a manual quickstart sentence; there is no automated or scripted negative test anywhere in tasks.md.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-004 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/chat-api.md** - The SSE contract cannot be implemented by the page as specified, and the obvious workaround leaks the token. The browser `EventSource` API cannot set an `Authorization` header and cannot set `Last-Event-ID` (the browser sends it only on its own automatic reconnect), yet chat-api.md requires the header on every request and T041 requires "SSE client with `Last-Event-ID` reconnect". The implementer's shortest path is `?token=...` in the URL, which puts the per-launch bearer token into the server's access log, WebView2 history and any crash dump. Separately, the page is served from a virtual host name while the backend is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` — a different origin — and no CORS/`Origin` policy is defined anywhere in the package, so cross-origin `fetch`/`EventSource` behaviour is unspecified; a permissive `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` added later would let any page in any browser on the workstation probe the port.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-005 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - The automated scan that is supposed to prove SC-006 ("API keys never appear in any file under the run folder or the log folder … an automated scan confirms it") reads the keys to search for from its own environment, but on the workstation the key lives DPAPI-protected in `%APPDATA%\SwReview\settings.json` and reaches only the backend child process's environment. When the engineer runs `uv run swreview audit-secrets <run> <logs>` from a separate shell, that shell has no key, so the scan has nothing to look for and reports "none" regardless of what is in the files. The check passes vacuously exactly in the scenario it exists to cover.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-006 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - SC-004 makes "zero mutating SOLIDWORKS calls … verified by the guard log" the acceptance evidence for the read-only boundary, but no guard log exists and no task creates one. `SwGate.Call` performs `ReadOnlyGuard.Assert` and then executes; it writes nothing anywhere. T047 adds a tool-service request log (per-request, at the dispatcher level), which records commands, not the interop members underneath them — so it cannot distinguish an allowed getter from a refused writer, and cannot substantiate a 30-minute general-chat claim. The claim is currently unverifiable. Compounding it, `ReadOnlyGuard` is a denylist of ten member names plus four prefixes, documented as needing manual extension "the moment a phase touches an API family that can write", and feature 002 adds no task to re-review it even though it adds two new untrusted callers driving a live `ISldWorks` in-process.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-007 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/README.md** - Two gaps in the tool-service transport's authentication. (a) The `secret` is optional in the protocol and the version stays 1.0, while the console host is specified to ignore it — so a client has no way to tell whether the host it is talking to enforces authentication, and the README's rationale is stated backwards ("clients that omit the field simply fail on the in-process host and still work against the console host" describes the harmless direction; the risky direction is a client that believes it is authenticated talking to a host that never checks). `ping` reports `protocol: "1.0"` either way. (b) No access control is specified for the named pipe itself. T047 creates `swreview-<guid>` with no `PipeSecurity`; .NET Framework 4.8 has no `PipeOptions.CurrentUserOnly`, so the pipe is created with the process token's default DACL and nothing in the design states who may connect, states that a server-side identity check (`NamedPipeServerStream.GetImpersonationUserName`) is performed, or prevents another process from creating the same pipe name first and harvesting the secret a client sends it.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-009 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/chat-api.md** - `run_dir` is accepted as a caller-supplied path at three boundaries with no constraint that it lie under the configured `run_root` and no normalization against traversal or UNC paths. `POST /sessions` takes any `run_dir` and the server then writes `session.json`, `report.md` and `events.jsonl` into it — anyone who reaches the loopback port with the token (including an injected page script, SEC-002) can point a session at an arbitrary directory. `folder.open` and `report.open` take a payload-supplied path/id and ShellExecute it, so a page message can launch an arbitrary file or directory (including a UNC path) with the engineer's credentials; `report.open` is specified as "Open `report.md` in the default app", which is a shell-association launch of a path the caller chose.
  - Fix: see the finding id security:SEC-010 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/chat-api.md** - With the chosen stack (`starlette` + `sse-starlette` + `uvicorn`, plan.md Primary Dependencies) the contract is violated by default and the port is not discoverable. uvicorn's default logging configuration sends the access logger to stdout, so access log lines will interleave with the handshake line the add-in parses. Separately, `--port 0` binds an ephemeral port whose number is only knowable after startup from the bound socket; `uvicorn.run(port=0)` gives the caller no way to print it, and uvicorn's `fd=` option is not usable on Windows.
  - Fix: see the finding id feasibility:F05 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The Gemini half of the contract asserts six configuration facts that research never established, and the contract itself hedges on the most load-bearing one. `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` as a settings-directory override, `mcp.allowed`, `security.disableYoloMode`, `general.defaultApprovalMode`, `context.fileName`, the per-server `trust` key, and a `toolName = "*"` wildcard deny rule appear nowhere in research R4. If the home override is wrong, the generated files are never read and Gemini runs under the engineer's own global settings — the exact failure FR-021 exists to prevent, and it would fail open (unrestricted), not closed.
  - Fix: see the finding id feasibility:F08 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - The locator is specified to resolve npm shims, but the launch path has no provision for executing one. A ConPTY child must be started with `CreateProcess` plus `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_PSEUDOCONSOLE` (so `System.Diagnostics.Process` cannot be used), and `CreateProcess` cannot execute a `.cmd`/`.bat` at all — it requires `cmd.exe /c "<shim>"` — while a `.ps1` requires `powershell.exe -File`. Consequently the Gemini CLI (`gemini.cmd`) would fail to start, and T050's job-object assignment has to be done by hand against the raw process handle rather than through the managed Process class.
  - Fix: see the finding id feasibility:F11 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/research.md** - The marshalling design has no timeout and no story for a blocked application thread. The pipe reader thread hands each request to the SOLIDWORKS thread and must wait for the result, but that thread is routinely blocked by modal dialogs, rebuilds, and long API calls. With no bound on the wait, a single modal dialog hangs every review tool call and every general-chat tool call indefinitely, and the Python client's own timeout fires while the C# side still holds the request — producing exactly the kind of unaccounted-for state Principle I forbids. `Control.BeginInvoke` also throws if the control's handle is not created or has been destroyed during unload.
  - Fix: see the finding id feasibility:F15 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/pane-host-messages.md** - Every chunk of pseudo-console output becomes one `PostWebMessageAsJson` call, and those calls must be made on the SOLIDWORKS application thread. A CLI that repaints a spinner or streams a long answer produces hundreds of small writes per second; each one posts a window message into SOLIDWORKS' pump and allocates a base64 string. Nothing in the contract or the tasks bounds the rate, so the realistic outcome is a visibly sluggish SOLIDWORKS UI during general chat — and the 50 ms keystroke-echo goal has no headroom left.
  - Fix: see the finding id feasibility:F16 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/data-model.md** - The data model and the event schema disagree on two event bodies, and T024 asserts that every event the runner emits validates against the schema — so an implementation written from the table will fail the test it is paired with. `session.started` is documented with four fields but the schema requires a fifth, `effort_mapping`, and lists it in `required` with `additionalProperties: false`. `turn.ended` is documented with three reasons but the schema allows four; the missing one, `stopped`, is the reason the `POST /sessions/{chat_id}/stop` route must produce.
  - Fix: see the finding id feasibility:F17 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - No task removes the hard-coded Claude model id from product code, and eight feature-001 tests assert that id. FR-026 ("No Claude or Anthropic path MAY exist in product code, configuration, or documentation") cannot be satisfied by the task list as written, and the assertions will fail once the default becomes `gpt-5.6`.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F2 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/plan.md** - The stated mitigation for the top provider risk is unverifiable by the test it names. A `respx`-recorded exchange replays a response we author ourselves, so it can never show that OpenAI's strict-schema validator accepts the generated `SourceRef`/nullable schemas — the exact thing research flags as unverified. Every test in T017 will pass while the real call 400s.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F3 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - T038 implements the process contract the whole pane depends on — first stdout line is exactly `{port, token}`, nothing else on stdout, bind 127.0.0.1 only, `--fail-bridge N` — with no preceding test task. T036 uses Starlette's in-process test client, which never exercises `__main__`, and the only test of the handshake (T033) runs against a *stub script* that prints the line by construction, so both sides are tested against assumptions and neither against the real program.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F4 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/data-model.md** - The two normative descriptions of the event stream disagree, so T024 ("every event the runner and tools can emit validates against `contracts/chat-events.schema.json`") and T035/T014 cannot both be satisfied: a `session.started` event built from data-model.md is invalid under the schema, and a `/stop` producing `turn.ended` is valid under the schema but not under data-model.md.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F5 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - Four test tasks are ordered after the code they cover, and one behavior has no test at all, which breaks the file's own rule ("Test tasks precede implementation in every phase") and constitution Principle III. Written after the fact these tests are calibrated to the implementation's actual output rather than to the contract, which is exactly how a schema or message mismatch survives.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F8 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/tasks.md** - The only tests for either WebView2 page are string scans of the source file, which is close to tautological: a scan passes if a message name appears in a comment and cannot fail for a page that sends nothing, sends the right name with the wrong payload, or mishandles a reply. Meanwhile the page behaviors with real failure modes — `Last-Event-ID` reconnect, the 500-event memory cap, input disabled while a turn runs, error-card actions — have no test at all, so spec Edge Case "Very long sessions" and FR-008's "next action" are unverified.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F9 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - Four named failure modes have no test task anywhere in tasks.md, so the spec's Edge Cases section is not a verified contract: (a) the document closed mid-review, (b) a tool call naming a tool that does not exist, (c) a model id the provider no longer offers, (d) a persistent reference that no longer resolves offering the component's full path.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F10 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - Nothing in the data model, the contracts, or tasks.md can express the link this edge case requires, so the task list cannot be executed as written. `ReviewSession` gains only `provider_info` (T016), no chat-api endpoint takes a predecessor id, and no test covers the retry path or the requirement that the aborted session still carries an ended time.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F11 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - The WebView2-missing fallback has neither an implementation task nor a test, yet it is the failure mode that takes down both tabs at once. T043 builds the tab host assuming WebView2 initializes; nothing shows the download link or the plain-text run-folder fallback the spec promises.
  - Fix: see the finding id tests:F12 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/data-model.md** - The ChatSession state machine is incomplete: `ended` is terminal, but FR-006 and US1 Acceptance Scenario 5 require a follow-up message on a *completed* session to run in that same session. There is also no state or transition for `POST /stop` or for the failure-then-Retry path. T035 tests "transitions from data-model section 3", so the incomplete machine would be locked in by test and follow-ups would be rejected.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F2 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/quickstart.md** - Every workstation scenario and SC-007 are anchored on "the bracket fixture from feature 001", which does not exist in the repository and is not created by any task. `benchmarks/native/` is empty and `benchmarks/packages/` contains only `cover-blind-tap`. Nothing is gitignored, so this is genuinely absent, and the three SOLIDWORKS tasks (T044, T051, T063) cannot be run as written.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F5 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - SC-004 promises "Zero mutating SOLIDWORKS calls ... verified by the guard log", but no guard log exists anywhere in the codebase or in feature 001, and no task in tasks.md creates or asserts one. Worse, the existing guard is a denylist that throws rather than records, so even if a log were added it could only prove that none of ten named members were called — not that zero mutating calls occurred. The success criterion is unverifiable as stated.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F6 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/data-model.md** - The event catalogue is stated three times and the three copies disagree. data-model §2 says `session.started` carries `session_id, package_id, provider, model` while the schema *requires* `effort_mapping` as well; data-model says `turn.ended` reason is `end | max_steps | error` while the schema enum also contains `stopped` (which `POST /stop` produces). FR-013 lists only 8 of the 13 event types. An implementer following data-model.md will emit events that fail the schema T024 validates against.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F8 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md** - The bridge secret — the sole authentication on the in-process tool service — is written in cleartext into `<run_dir>/.swreview-cli/codex.toml` and `<run_dir>/.swreview-cli/gemini/settings.json`, inside the very directory the sandboxed CLI is launched in and explicitly permitted to read. The general-chat model can read its own profile, recover the secret and the pipe name, and address the tool service outside the MCP allowlist, which defeats FR-022's "MUST record every call in a chat log" and SC-003's "100% of tool calls are recorded". The contract's own claim that the secret is "never on the command line" understates the exposure rather than removing it.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F9 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - The Retry-after-provider-failure behaviour — "starts a new session that references the previous one" — has no functional requirement, no field anywhere in data-model.md or the contracts to hold the reference, and no endpoint or task that implements it. T041 renders a Retry button on error cards with nothing behind it.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F11 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/spec.md** - Three of the nine success criteria are timing thresholds, and nothing in tasks.md or quickstart.md measures any of them. The quickstart scenarios list expected behaviours but no stopwatch, no instrumentation, and no recorded number; the workstation tasks (T044, T051, T063) say only "record results". These criteria cannot be shown met or unmet at the end of the feature.
  - Fix: see the finding id spec-quality:F14 in the review; the fix agent applied only must-fix and local should-fix items

## Feature 002 Phases 1-2 (provider layer)


### schema-protocol (16)

- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_provider_schema.py:96** - `enumerate_tools()` wraps `from swreview.tools import registry` in `except ModuleNotFoundError` and falls back to `FALLBACK_TOOL_FUNCTIONS` / `FALLBACK_BRIDGE_TOOL_FUNCTIONS` (lines 55-91), a hand-maintained second copy of the registration list. T013 has landed, `registry.py` imports cleanly and the fallback is now dead code - but it is a live silent-failure trap: any future `ModuleNotFoundError` anywhere in registry's import graph (a renamed module, a dropped dependency) makes 29 parametrized tests silently re-target a stale hardcoded list and still report green. It is also the DRY violation the constitution's Principle V calls out - the tool list now exists in two places.
  - Fix: Import `swreview.tools.registry` unconditionally at module top and delete `enumerate_tools`, `FALLBACK_TOOL_FUNCTIONS` and `FALLBACK_BRIDGE_TOOL_FUNCTIONS`. The agent-tools.md golden (line 201) already keeps the registry honest against the contract.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_provider_schema.py:370** - `test_strictify_makes_a_nested_optional_field_nullable_too` asserts the exact behaviour that produces must_fix #1 - it pins `scope.properties.component_ids.type == ["array","null"]` as correct. Nothing in the suite closes the loop: there is no test that an arguments object which conforms to a strictified schema is actually accepted by `RecordedTool`/`validate_call`. That missing round trip is the coverage gap that let the defect through, and it is exactly the class of test the constitution calls non-negotiable.
  - Fix: Add a per-tool test that builds the maximal strict-conforming instance from `strictify(canonical_schema(fn))` (every property present, optionals set to null, required scalars given a valid value) and asserts `RecordedTool.call(...)` does not come back with an argument-validation `is_error`. Then update this test to whatever the corrected `strictify` emits.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/fake.py:187** - `error = str(result.payload.get("error")) if result.is_error else None` fabricates the literal string `"None"` for the contract's `tool.finished.error` field whenever a `ProviderTool` returns `is_error=True` with a payload that has no `error` key. The schema declares `error` as `["string","null"]`, so `"None"` validates and the pane would show an error whose message is the word None. `RecordedTool` happens to always set the key, so this is latent - but the fake is the thing every runner, server and pane test drives, and an invented message in an error path is the opposite of "a missing input must stay unknown".
  - Fix: `raw = result.payload.get("error"); error = str(raw) if raw is not None else (summarize_result(result.payload) if result.is_error else None)` - or assert the invariant explicitly. Add a test with a `FakeTool(is_error=True, payload={"detail": "..."})`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_provider_schema.py:106** - T006's sentence is absolute: "none contains an object with zero properties". The implementation carves out `NO_ARGUMENT_TOOLS` (`get_package_summary`, `list_gaps`, `get_review_checklist`) and exempts the root parameters object (line 341). The exemption is substantively right - a tool that takes nothing has no other strict-mode form, and `{"type":"object","properties":{},"required":[],"additionalProperties":false}` is accepted - but it is a literal departure from the task text that has not been surfaced as a deviation.
  - Fix: Keep the exemption (it is correct) and record it explicitly in the task's deviation note, so the narrowing of a stated constraint is a decision on the record rather than a detail buried in a test docstring.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/schema.py:113** - `parse_docstring` returns only the text before the FIRST section header, so any `Returns:`, `Note:` or `Examples:` prose - whether it sits before or after `Args:` - is silently dropped from the description the model actually reads, with no error. No tool has such a section today (verified across all 29), so this is latent, but the module raises loudly for an undocumented parameter and says nothing at all for discarded prose - an inconsistent contract for the same failure class.
  - Fix: Either append the non-Args sections to the description (they are prose the model benefits from), or raise for a section the parser does not consume, so a future docstring cannot lose text without anyone noticing.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/session.py:112** - `ToolContext.session` became `ReviewSession | None` (for general chat), but `mark_coverage` (line 112), `request_evidence` (line 73), `record_drawing_finding` (line 179) and `get_review_checklist` (line 205) all dereference it unguarded, and `ToolRegistry.functions_for` still hands the full curated list - session tools included - to a sessionless context. A general-chat call to `mark_coverage` therefore surfaces as `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'coverage'` converted to a tool error, rather than a result that says there is no review session in this chat. This most likely belongs to T059, but it lands in a file T005a edited.
  - Fix: Either filter the four session-writing tools out of `functions_for` when `context.session is None`, or add one shared guard (`context.require_session("mark_coverage")`) that returns the standard `{"error": ...}` shape. Cover it with a test that builds a sessionless context and calls each of the four.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/__init__.py:270** - `available()` returns only the adapters that happen to have been imported already, so before the first `get()` call it can legitimately return `()`. The docstring says so, but the name reads like "the providers this build supports" and any caller that validates a user's `--provider` against it would reject a perfectly legal value depending on import order - an order-dependent failure that no test would reproduce.
  - Fix: Rename to `registered()`, or have `available()` return `tuple(ProviderName)` and keep the import-state question behind a separate `registered()`. Either way state in the docstring which one a CLI choice should be validated against.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/schema.py:338** - `gemini_adapt`'s docstring claims "only `additionalProperties` and `$defs` come out; everything else ... goes across as standard JSON Schema", but it leaves in the constraint-only `anyOf` branches that `strictify` is careful to drop (`SourceRef`'s `{"required": ["sheet"]}` alternatives) and the pydantic `title` keys. The installed `google-genai` types `parameters_json_schema` as `Optional[Any]` with no client-side validation (types.py:4814), so whether the Gemini backend accepts a bare-constraint `anyOf` branch is untested and unverifiable from the SDK source - the claim rests on nothing.
  - Fix: Either assert the claim in a test against a real Gemini `FunctionDeclaration` round trip (T017/T018's live test is the natural home), or drop the constraint-only branches and `title` in `gemini_adapt` too - reusing `_drop_constraint_only_branches` costs one line and removes the open question. Soften the docstring to what is actually known either way.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/fake.py:187** - `error = str(result.payload.get("error")) if result.is_error else None` emits the literal string "None" in the `tool.finished` body when a ProviderTool returns is_error=True with a payload that has no `error` key. It validates against chat-events.schema.json (string is allowed), so the contract test cannot catch it, and no test covers that payload shape. Today's RecordedTool always sets `error` (registry.py:439), but T059's MCP path and any other ProviderTool implementation are not bound by that.
  - Fix: Use an explicit fallback, e.g. `payload.get("error")` with `summarize_result(payload)` (or a fixed "the tool reported a failure" string) when the key is absent, and add a test case with `FakeTool(payload={"detail": "..."}, is_error=True)` asserting the event carries a meaningful error string.
- **reviewer/tests/support/contracts.py:64** - `contract_validator` passes `format_checker=Draft202012Validator.FORMAT_CHECKER`, which reads as 'formats are enforced', but `rfc3339-validator` is not installed in the venv, so `date-time` is silently NOT checked (I verified: `FORMAT_CHECKER.check('not-a-timestamp','date-time')` passes; `uuid` is checked). Every `format: date-time` field in chat-events and review-session — event `at`, session.ended `ended_at`, disposition `at`, `answered_at` — is validated as a plain string, so T008's and T010's contract assertions are weaker than they look.
  - Fix: Add `rfc3339-validator` (or the `jsonschema[format-nongpl]` extra) to the dev dependencies in pyproject.toml, plus a one-line guard test asserting `contract_validator(...)` rejects `{"at": "not-a-timestamp"}`, so the assurance is real rather than assumed.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/fake.py:45** - `_DELTA = re.compile(r"\S+\s*")` drops leading whitespace: for `text="  Checking\nthe joints"` the deltas rejoin to "Checking\nthe joints", so the stream the pane renders differs from `TurnResult.text` and from the `text.done` body. The invariant is only spot-checked on one whitespace-free string (test_text_only_turn_streams_deltas_then_one_done), so nothing pins it.
  - Fix: Split with a pattern that keeps every character (e.g. `re.findall(r"\s*\S+\s*", text)` or `re.split` keeping separators) and add a parametrized test over several texts (leading spaces, embedded newline, trailing space, single token, unicode) asserting `''.join(deltas) == turn.text == text.done body`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_provider_protocol.py:322** - `test_stub_satisfies_the_provider_protocol` (and test_fake_provider.py:105) assert `isinstance(x, AgentProvider)`. For a runtime_checkable Protocol, isinstance only checks that the attribute names exist and are callable — it does not check signatures. Both tests would still pass if `run()` lost `on_event` or renamed `max_steps`, which is exactly the drift the protocol exists to prevent.
  - Fix: Add a signature assertion alongside the isinstance check, e.g. compare `inspect.signature(FakeProvider.run).parameters` against `inspect.signature(AgentProvider.run).parameters` (names and keyword-only-ness), for both the stub and FakeProvider.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_provider_schema.py:324** - The strict-mode assertions cover additionalProperties, required, nullability, $defs and $refs, but nothing asserts the absence of the keywords OpenAI rejects outright under `strict: true` for all models — allOf, not, if/then/else, dependentRequired, dependentSchemas, patternProperties. I verified none is present today, but pydantic can emit `allOf` when a field carries a $ref plus metadata, so a future model change (a Field(description=...) on SourceRef, an Annotated constraint) could reintroduce one and the only signal would be a 400 in T018a's live call. Research risk 4 designates T006 as the local evidence for exactly this.
  - Fix: Add one parametrized test over ALL_TOOL_FUNCTIONS walking `strictify(canonical_schema(fn))` and asserting no node contains any key in a named UNSUPPORTED_KEYWORDS frozenset, with the OpenAI doc reference in the docstring (fine-tuned-model-only restrictions such as minLength/minItems deliberately excluded, since they are supported on gpt-5.x).
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:382** - Nested model fields reach the model with no description at all: every property of InterferenceSettings, CoverageScope and SourceRef comes out as a bare name plus a pydantic-generated `title` (verified on check_fit.bore_dimension_ref: document_id/sheet/view/annotation/persist_ref/page/bbox all lack `description`). T006's test_every_parameter_carries_its_docstring_description only walks top-level parameters, so the gap is invisible. The docstring prose on mark_coverage/bridge_interference partly compensates, but `pairs`, `positions`, `ignore_hidden` and `fastener_folder_treatment` are exactly the fields where a guess silently becomes recorded engineering data.
  - Fix: Add `Field(description=...)` to InterferenceSettings, CoverageScope and SourceRef fields, and extend the T006 test to require a description on every property of every object subschema (not just the root's), so the requirement is enforced rather than assumed. Note this touches models shared with feature 001 output — check the golden baselines stay byte-identical (descriptions live in schemas, not in serialized sessions, so they should).
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_tools_session.py:137** - In test_mark_coverage_refuses_a_scope_it_cannot_read, `with pytest.raises(ValidationError): CoverageScope(widgets=[])` asserts pydantic's own extra="forbid" behaviour, not any code in this repo — it is tautological and would pass even if mark_coverage were deleted. The behaviour that actually replaced the old error path (a model-invented scope key becomes an error ToolCallResult plus `failed` coverage rather than a raise) is only asserted in T012's registry tests, and the docstring here says so.
  - Fix: Either drop those two lines and keep only the unknown-ids assertion (which does exercise the tool), or make the case real by going through `ToolRegistry().build(context)` and asserting `tools['mark_coverage'].call({... 'scope': {'widgets': []} ...}).is_error is True` plus a `tool.mark_coverage` entry in `context.session.coverage.failed`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/schema.py:233** - `_make_nullable` adds `null` to every non-required property, and schema.py's own docstring records the other half of the bargain: RecordedTool must drop a null for a parameter whose annotation does not admit one, so the default applies. For `scope: CoverageScope` that means a model sending `{"component_ids": null, "pairs": null, "configuration": null, "positions": null, "document_ids": null}` re-creates precisely the silent empty-scope outcome T005a set out to eliminate — it validates, and mark_coverage records an empty scope with no error. No test in T005a/T006 covers an all-null scope.
  - Fix: Decide explicitly and pin it with a test: either have mark_coverage reject a scope with no field set (`if scope == CoverageScope(): return error_result(...)`, matching 'never write a default for engineering data'), or add a test asserting the all-null scope is accepted deliberately. Either way, add the case to test_tools_session.py so the choice is visible.

### registry-runner (17)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/runner.py:391** - `ReviewRun.start` writes defaults into the `session.started` body when `provider_info` is missing: `"provider": mapping.provider if mapping is not None else ""` and `effort_mapping` falling back to `{}` (line 396). Both are contract-invalid - the schema pins `provider` to the enum `[openai, gemini, fake]` and requires `effort_mapping.{requested,provider_param,provider_value}` - so the branch writes a silently malformed event into `events.jsonl` rather than failing. It is also error handling for a scenario `start_review` makes impossible, which the constitution's Principle V rules out.
  - Fix: Drop the conditionals and read `self.session.provider_info` directly; if the invariant needs stating, `raise ValueError("a review run must record its provider before the first turn")` when it is None. Same shape at line 444, where `session.ended` emits `"ended_at": None` if `ended_at` were null, against a schema that requires a date-time string.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/report/session.py:38** - `ProviderInfo.provider` is typed `str`, but `review-session.schema.json` constrains it to `["openai", "gemini", "fake"]`.
