Pydantic will happily accept `ProviderInfo(provider="anthropic", ...)` and `save_session` will write a `session.json` that fails its own contract, with nothing in the write path validating against the schema. `tests/unit/test_session.py` covers the equivalent hole for `key_source` (`test_provider_info_rejects_an_unknown_key_source`) but has no counterpart for `provider` - because there is nothing to assert.
  - Fix: Type it `ProviderName` (already imported alongside `EffortMapping` from `swreview.agent.providers`) or a local `Literal["openai", "gemini", "fake"]`, and add the mirror of the `key_source` rejection test.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/runner.py:359** - data-model.md section 3 rule 4 reads "the cumulative step count is recorded separately **on the session**"; the implementation keeps it on the in-memory run (`self.total_steps`, incremented at line 502) and the runner docstring restates it as "on the run". Nothing persists it, so a `session.json` reloaded after a pane restart cannot tell how much budget the session has already spent. `len(session.steps)` is a near-equivalent but counts recorded calls, not provider-reported steps, and diverges the moment an adapter reports a step it did not record.
  - Fix: Either add an optional `cumulative_steps` (or fold it into `Timing`) on `ReviewSession` and write it on each finalize, or - if the field is genuinely not wanted - amend data-model rule 4 to say "on the run" so the artifact and the spec agree. My recommendation is the session field: it is one integer, it survives a restart, and the resume rule exists precisely for the restart case.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/registry.py:413** - `ToolDispatch.by_name` is a `@property` that rebuilds the whole name dict on every access. `ToolDispatch.call` hits it once through `get()` and, on the unknown-name path, a second time for `sorted(self.by_name)` in the error message - so every dispatched tool call rebuilds a ~21-entry dict, twice for hallucinated names. `ToolDispatch` is a frozen dataclass over an immutable tuple, so the mapping is invariant for its whole life.
  - Fix: Build it once: `functools.cached_property`, or compute it in `ToolRegistry.dispatch` and store it as a field. Small, but this is the per-call hot path for every provider turn.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/registry.py:388** - The trace summary regressed for list-returning tools. Feature 001's `summarize` rendered a list as `f"{len(payload)} items: " + json.dumps(...)`, so the count survived the 200-character truncation; `summarize_result` (agent/providers/__init__.py) has no list branch, and because `_as_object` wraps lists as `{"result": [...]}` first, a long `list_components` result now records `{"result":[{"id":"cmp:0001",...` truncated at 200 chars with the count gone. `InvestigationStep.result_summary` is the trace line an engineer reads in `session.json`, and "how many did it get back" was its most useful fact.
  - Fix: Restore the count in `summarize_result` for the wrapped-list case - e.g. when the payload is exactly `{RESULT_KEY: [...]}`, emit `f"{len(items)} items: " + json.dumps(items, ...)` before truncating. Keeps the one shared summarizer (the reason it was moved to `providers`) and restores the feature 001 trace.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/registry.py:154** - `RESULT_KEY` wrapping changes the payload the model actually receives for every list-returning query tool (`list_components`, `list_mates`, `list_holes`, `list_fasteners`, `list_interferences`, `list_gaps`, `find_components`, `find_dimensions`): feature 001 handed the model a bare JSON array, it now gets `{"result": [...]}`. The change is necessary (Gemini's `function_response` takes an object) and is documented in the module docstring, but `specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/agent-tools.md` still documents the bare-array return shapes, and T064's doc-update sentence only covers the schema-generation prose and the `mark_coverage`/`bridge_interference` rows - it does not mention the result envelope.
  - Fix: Add the result-envelope rule to the T064 doc edit (one sentence in agent-tools.md: a non-object tool result is delivered as `{"result": <value>}`), and consider asserting it once in `test_registry_provider_neutral.py` against the contract text so the two cannot drift.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_runner_provider.py:707** - Two bridge tests from the deleted `test_agent_runner.py` have no replacement: `test_the_pipe_name_reaches_the_factory` (a non-default `pipe_name` actually reaches `bridge_factory`) and `test_the_default_factory_builds_a_bridge_client_without_connecting` (the default factory is `BridgeClient` and constructing it does not connect). The new `test_bridge_true_wires_a_client_into_the_context_and_the_tools` only asserts the default pipe name `"swreview"` via an injected factory, so `pipe_name` is now a parameter nothing exercises and the production default factory is untested. T014 says "replace the anthropic-based fake", not "drop coverage", and the project's stated preference is too many tests over too few.
  - Fix: Port both cases: one asserting `start_review(..., bridge=True, pipe_name="custom", bridge_factory=f)` passes `"custom"` through, and one asserting `start_review(..., bridge=True)` with no factory builds a `BridgeClient` without opening the pipe.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/runner.py:173** - `finalize_session` does `review = context.session` and immediately dereferences `review.coverage` / `review.evidence_requests`, but T013 made `ToolContext.session` optional (`ReviewSession | None`). A sessionless context - the general-chat shape the same task introduced - reaches here as a bare `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'coverage'` rather than a named error. `ReviewRun.session` (line 368) already has the explicit guard; the module-level function does not.
  - Fix: Add the same explicit check at the top: `review = context.session; if review is None: raise ValueError("finalize_session needs a context with a review session")`. Three lines, and it keeps the type checker honest now that `session` is optional.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/report/session.py:38** - `ProviderInfo.provider` is typed `str`, but the contract (review-session.schema.json `$defs/ProviderInfo`) constrains it to `enum: ["openai", "gemini", "fake"]`. Nothing on the Python side rejects any other value, so a session.json that fails its own contract can be written without a single test failing. test_session.py:289 proves `key_source` is rejected for a bad value but there is no equivalent for `provider` - the one field where an enum actually exists in the schema.
  - Fix: Type it `provider: ProviderName` (it is a StrEnum, so it serializes to the bare value and the golden/contract output is unchanged), and add a `ProviderInfo(provider="anthropic", ...)` -> ValidationError case next to the existing key_source test.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/runner.py:391** - Two defensive fallbacks in the event emission produce events that violate chat-events.schema.json. `"provider": mapping.provider if mapping is not None else ""` emits `""`, which fails the `session.started` body's `provider` enum; and at runner.py:444 `"ended_at": ... else None` emits null, which fails `session.ended`'s `"ended_at": {"type": "string"}`. Both are unreachable through `start_review` (it always sets provider_info; finalize always sets ended_at), so no test covers them - the fallback silently trades a loud failure for a contract-invalid line in events.jsonl that the pane will choke on.
  - Fix: Drop the fallbacks and read the values off the session directly, or raise/assert if they are absent. `explicit over clever`: if the state is impossible, say so rather than writing an invalid event. If the branch is kept, it needs a test that the emitted event still validates.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_runner_provider.py:707** - The port from test_agent_runner.py lost two bridge cases. (1) `test_the_default_factory_builds_a_bridge_client_without_connecting` is gone - every new test injects `bridge_factory`, so `factory = bridge_factory if bridge_factory is not None else BridgeClient` (runner.py) is now entirely untested; breaking the default wiring would ship green. (2) `test_the_pipe_name_reaches_the_factory` used a custom `pipe_name="review-42"`; the new test only asserts the default `"swreview"`, so a runner that ignored the caller's pipe_name would still pass.
  - Fix: Restore both: one test passing `pipe_name="review-42"` with an injected factory asserting the name reaches it, and one with `bridge=True` and no factory asserting `isinstance(run.context.bridge, BridgeClient)` and `pipe_name == "swreview"` (the old test proved BridgeClient does not connect on construction, so it stays offline).
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/registry.py:262** - `_admits_null` (the recursive anyOf/oneOf walk) and the nullable-keeps-its-null half of `_prepared` are currently unobservable: no tool in the registry has a parameter whose annotation admits None and whose default is not None (verified by grep over tools/*.py), so dropping a null and keeping it produce identical results for every tool that exists. The one test aimed at this (test_registry_provider_neutral.py:139) passes `parent_id: None` where the default is also None, so it cannot distinguish the branches - forcing `_admits_null` to return False unconditionally would break nothing. That is ~16 lines of recursion with no test that can fail.
  - Fix: Either pin it directly - a small test that builds a `RecordedTool` over a synthetic `ToolSpec` with `flag: bool | None = True` and asserts a null arrives as `None` rather than `True`, and that a non-nullable `bool = True` gets the default - or, if no real tool will ever need it, delete the recursion and drop nulls for anything without an explicit `"null"` in `type`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_runner_provider.py:541** - Resume rule (d) asserts the `max_steps` closeout item exists (`len(closeout) == 1`) only before the resumed turn. After `continue_session` - which finalizes a second time - the test checks steps, total_steps and turn.ended reasons but never re-checks the closeout item. So the interaction of rule (d) with rule (a) is unpinned in both directions: a finalize that withdrew the item, or one that duplicated it, both stay green here. (The sibling case at line ~455 covers the same mechanism for a `mark_coverage` item, so the mechanism is not wholly untested - but the max_steps item is the one this test exists for.)
  - Fix: Add the two assertions after `run.continue_session("Keep going.")`: the closeout list is still length 1 and its reason still names `max_steps reached (2 tool calls)`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_registry_provider_neutral.py:299** - The FR-026 guard exempts all of `agent/providers/__init__.py` from the `(?i)anthropic|claude` scan, justified by `RETIRED_PROVIDERS` needing the bare names in order to reject them. But the exemption is wider than the justification: that file also carries prose - `"Feature 002 removed the Anthropic SDK entirely (FR-026)"` and the error text `f"provider {text!r} was removed with the Anthropic SDK in feature 002"` - which is a vendor string under reviewer/src/ that the product constraint forbids and that `MODEL_OR_PACKAGE` (`claude-|anthropic[.\-/]|@anthropic`) deliberately permits. It is the one hole the scan leaves. Separately, the scan only walks `*.py`; non-Python files under src/ (checklist_v1.yaml, prompts/system_v1.md, bridge/PROTOCOL.md) are unscanned - clean today, but nothing keeps them so.
  - Fix: Reword the two prose mentions in providers/__init__.py to avoid the vendor name (e.g. "the retired provider SDK"), keeping only `RETIRED_PROVIDERS = frozenset({"anthropic", "claude"})`, then narrow `RETIREMENT_GUARD` to permit just that one literal line. While there, widen `source_files()` to every text file under src/ (T068 will want this anyway).
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/registry.py:431** - `RecordedTool.call` guards a non-Mapping `arguments` and converts it to an error result, but `ToolDispatch.call`'s unknown-name path does not: it goes straight to `record_call(..., arguments=arguments, ...)` which does `to_jsonable_python(dict(arguments))` and raises TypeError for a list or string - inside the layer whose documented invariant is "nothing raises. Ever." The asymmetry is unreachable in practice today (openai_provider raises MalformedArgumentsError for non-object arguments before dispatch, gemini gets a dict from the SDK), which is also why neither the guard at registry.py:302 nor this gap has a test.
  - Fix: Move the non-Mapping coercion above the name lookup so both paths share it, and add one test passing `[1, 2]` as arguments to both a known and an unknown tool name, asserting an error result rather than a raise.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_report.py:117** - The shared `build_session` fixture still writes `"model": "claude-opus-5"` (same at test_session.py:74, and in test_scorecard.py / test_timing.py). The FR-026 scan is deliberately scoped to reviewer/src/, so this is not a violation - but these are precisely the two files T016 just touched, and the new tests now render a `ProviderInfo(provider="openai", model="gpt-test")` header directly above a `- Model: claude-opus-5` line in the same report. It reads as an oversight and will be re-flagged at T068.
  - Fix: Swap the fixture model literal to a neutral `"gpt-test"` (or the provider-neutral default T023 lands). One-line change per file, no assertion depends on the value.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_registry_provider_neutral.py:99** - `test_the_built_names_are_the_registered_groups_in_order` derives `expected` from `REGISTRATIONS` - the same constant `TOOL_FUNCTIONS` is derived from - so it can only catch a `build` that reorders or drops entries, not a wrong tool surface. Mild tautology. It is not a real hole (test_provider_schema.py:202 holds the golden against contracts/agent-tools.md, which is the assertion that has teeth), so this is a note rather than a defect.
  - Fix: Either leave it and add a one-line comment pointing at the agent-tools.md golden as the real guard, or fold the ordering claim into that golden test so one test owns the tool surface.

### adapters-settings (14)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:309** - `TurnResult.text` is inconsistent between the two adapters for the same provider-neutral contract. OpenAI accumulates every round's text and returns the join (openai_provider.py:250-252, 362). Gemini returns only the last round's `round_.text` (line 312) and hard-codes `text=""` on the `max_steps` return (line 309), so anything the model said before its tool calls, or in the round the budget cut short, is dropped from the turn result the runner records.
  - Fix: Accumulate round texts in `run` the way openai_provider does and return the join on every exit path, including `max_steps`. Add a two-round test where both rounds emit text and assert `result.text` holds both.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:267** - `text.done` cardinality differs between adapters: Gemini emits one per model round (line 267-268), OpenAI one per turn carrying the whole turn's text (openai_provider.py:361-365). FR-013 requires a uniform event stream that both the pane and the CLI consume; a consumer that treats `text.done` as "the turn's answer is complete" will be wrong on one of the two providers, and the difference is pinned by tests on both sides rather than reconciled.
  - Fix: Pick one semantic, write it into contracts/chat-events.schema.json's description (or data-model section 2), and make both adapters follow it. One `text.done` per turn is the reading that matches `turn.ended` and the existing OpenAI tests.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:208** - `max_output_tokens` defaults to `None` and is then omitted from `GenerateContentConfig` (line 388), so `OUTPUT_CEILINGS[ProviderName.GEMINI]` in agent/settings.py:97 is dead unless a caller remembers to pass it, and the `MAX_TOKENS` -> "truncated" path depends on the model's own default rather than on a budget we own - the settings module's own docstring (settings.py:100-108) says the opposite ("Both are *our* budget for one turn ... which it can only do if the ceiling is ours to set"). The OpenAI adapter defaults from `output_ceiling` (openai_provider.py:182-186) and rejects a non-positive value (line 187-188); Gemini does neither. tests/unit/test_gemini_provider.py:275-287 pins the omission as intended behaviour.
  - Fix: Default to `output_ceiling(ProviderName.GEMINI, model)` when `max_output_tokens is None`, and validate `> 0`, exactly as openai_provider.py:182-188 does. Update the test to assert the settings ceiling is sent when nothing is passed.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:349** - `_stream` catches only `errors.APIError`. google-genai raises transport failures (httpx connect/read/timeout errors) unwrapped from `_api_client`, so a dropped connection or DNS failure escapes `run` with no `error` event, no `retryable` classification and no redaction - the pane gets an unclassified crash instead of the retry card FR-013 and FR-028 assume. The OpenAI adapter has no such hole because `openai.OpenAIError` covers `APIConnectionError` (openai_provider.py:323, and the mapping at 412).
  - Fix: Widen the except to cover the transport exceptions the installed client can raise (`httpx.HTTPError`/`httpx2` equivalents alongside `errors.APIError`) and map them to a retryable `GeminiProviderError` through `_report`. Add a test whose stub `generate_content_stream` raises a connect error.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/settings.py:389** - `redact(text, secrets)` and `RedactingFilter(secrets)` take `Iterable[str | SecretStr | None]`. A bare `str` satisfies that annotation at runtime, so `redact(msg, settings.api_key.get_secret_value())` - an easy and silent mistake - iterates the key's characters and masks every occurrence of every character in the text, producing unreadable output while looking like it worked. `openai_provider._redact` (line 406) gets it right by wrapping in a list, but nothing enforces it at the boundary; "explicit over clever" argues for rejecting the ambiguous input.
  - Fix: Reject a bare `str`/`SecretStr` argument in `_secret_values` with a `TypeError` naming the mistake ("pass a sequence of secrets, not one secret"), and add a test for it.
- **reviewer/tests/conftest.py:26** - T018a gives OpenAI a key-gated proof that its strictified schemas survive the real API, on the stated grounds that "a respx exchange replays a body we authored and cannot retire this risk". Gemini has exactly the same risk and no such test: `LIVE_KEYS` is `("OPENAI_API_KEY",)` with a TODO-style docstring ("Gemini's key names join this tuple with its own live test"), and every Gemini assertion runs against a hand-rolled `StubClient` (tests/unit/test_gemini_provider.py:97-116) that never serializes a request. `gemini_adapt` output under `parameters_json_schema`, `thinking_level`/`thinking_budget` acceptance per model, and the content role are all unproved against the API - and the role defect above is precisely what that gap let through.
  - Fix: Add `tests/live/test_gemini_live_schemas.py` mirroring the OpenAI one (post each tool group's `gemini_adapt`ed declarations plus one function-response round trip, fail on 400, skip on 401/429/5xx) and add `GOOGLE_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY` to `LIVE_KEYS`. Note that `LIVE_KEYS` is currently an `any()` check, so once it holds both providers' names an OpenAI-only workstation would try to run the Gemini live test - gate each live module on its own key.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_gemini_provider.py:355** - No Gemini test scripts a round that contains BOTH text and a function call - every tool-call test uses a call-only first round (test_a_function_call_runs_through_the_tool_and_is_answered_with_its_id, test_parallel_calls_in_one_round..., test_max_steps_is_a_per_turn_budget) and every text test is single-round. That is precisely the shape where the text.done/TurnResult.text defect above is visible, and it is the common real shape for Gemini (the model narrates before calling a tool). The suite passes while the turn text is being lost.
  - Fix: Add a test scripting [text_part("Checking the bracket. "), call_part(...)] then [text_part("It interferes."), finish_reason=STOP] and assert exactly one `text.done` whose text is the concatenation, and `result.text == "Checking the bracket. It interferes."`.
- **reviewer/tests/conftest.py:38** - `live` tests are gated on `any(os.environ.get(name) for name in LIVE_KEYS)`, i.e. ANY provider key un-skips EVERY live test. Today LIVE_KEYS holds only OPENAI_API_KEY so it is inert, but the docstring at line 28 states "Gemini's key names join this tuple with its own live test (feature 002, Phase 2)". The moment they do, a workstation with only GOOGLE_API_KEY set runs tests/live/test_openai_live_schemas.py, whose module-scoped `client` fixture does `os.environ["OPENAI_API_KEY"]` and raises KeyError - a red suite instead of a skip, which is exactly what the skip convention exists to prevent.
  - Fix: Gate per test rather than globally: give each live test its own required key (e.g. a `requires_key("OPENAI_API_KEY")` marker or a fixture that calls `pytest.skip` when its key is absent) so a Gemini-only or OpenAI-only environment skips the other provider's live test instead of erroring.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/settings.py:151** - `output_ceiling` consults `MODEL_OUTPUT_CEILINGS` before `OUTPUT_CEILINGS`, and the docstring says the model is consulted first "so a model with a smaller output window can be given its own number". `MODEL_OUTPUT_CEILINGS` is empty and no test ever populates it, so the model-first precedence - the entire reason the table exists - is unproven: swapping the two lookups, or dropping the per-model table altogether, leaves the whole suite green. The one test that touches it (test_output_ceiling_falls_back_to_the_provider_budget_for_an_unlisted_model) only exercises the fallback.
  - Fix: Add a test that monkeypatches a per-model entry (e.g. `monkeypatch.setitem(settings.MODEL_OUTPUT_CEILINGS, "gpt-5-nano", 4096)`) and asserts `output_ceiling("openai", "gpt-5-nano") == 4096` while the provider budget still applies to other models.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/openai_provider.py:473** - `_encode_assistant`'s fallback branch - rebuilding `function_call` input items from the neutral `tool_calls` list when the message carries no `openai` key - is untested. Its stated purpose is resuming a session on a provider it did not start on (module docstring, line 445-449), and every test either replays an adapter-written history (which takes the `HISTORY_KEY` branch) or a text-only assistant message. A mistake here (a missing `call_id`, `arguments` left as a dict instead of a JSON string) produces a 400 on the first resumed request, and nothing in the suite would catch it.
  - Fix: Add a respx test whose `messages` contain an assistant message with `tool_calls` and no `openai` key plus the matching `role: tool` message, and assert the encoded `input` carries `{"type": "function_call", "call_id": ..., "name": ..., "arguments": "<json string>"}` followed by the `function_call_output`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:310** - The truncation check is asymmetric with the OpenAI adapter. `_end_reason(round_.finish_reason)` is reached only when the round made no calls, so a round that comes back with `finish_reason=MAX_TOKENS` AND a function_call runs that call and continues the loop, and the turn can end `end` rather than `truncated`. The OpenAI adapter deliberately does the opposite (openai_provider.py:263-267: the ceiling is checked before dispatch because "running those calls would spend the session on a request we already know is incomplete"), and the runner turns `truncated` into unresolved coverage - so the same cut-short turn is recorded as finished on Gemini and unresolved on OpenAI. Untested in either direction on Gemini.
  - Fix: Check `round_.finish_reason == MAX_TOKENS` before dispatching the round's calls and return `truncated` without running them (mirroring `_hit_ceiling`), and add a test scripting a chunk with a function_call and `finish_reason=MAX_TOKENS` that asserts `reason == "truncated"`, `steps == 0` and no `tool.started`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/openai_provider.py:286** - `max_steps` means something different in each adapter. OpenAI returns `max_steps` the moment `steps >= max_steps` after running the round's calls, so with max_steps=1 the model never sees the result of the call it just made (test_max_steps_is_a_per_turn_budget asserts `route.call_count == 1`). Gemini instead runs the calls, loops, and issues one more request so the model can summarize, only ending `max_steps` if that next round asks for more calls. Same flag, same budget, observably different turns - and the runner records unresolved coverage in one case and a finished turn in the other.
  - Fix: Pick one semantic and state it on `AgentProvider.run`'s docstring in providers/__init__.py (the Gemini behaviour - spend the budget, then let the model answer once - wastes less), then make both adapters match and pin it with the same test in both suites.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/openai_provider.py:322** - Three error paths in the OpenAI adapter have no test at all: the `response.failed` stream event (`_failed`, line 425, which produces error_class `ResponseFailedError` and reads `response.error.message`), the `final is None` guard producing `IncompleteStreamError` (line 326), and both constructor `ValueError`s (a non-positive ceiling, and neither api_key nor client, lines 188-190). `response.failed` is a routine Responses-API outcome (a mid-generation model failure), and the `response.error` attribute access is exactly the kind of shape a stub never validates.
  - Fix: Add respx exchanges for a `response.failed` event (with and without an `error` object) and for a stream that ends with neither `response.completed` nor `response.incomplete`, asserting the error_class, `retryable`, and that the `error` event is emitted; add two one-line pytest.raises tests for the constructor guards.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:452** - `_request` documents that "A model that sends no `id` gets no id back (`""` here, omitted on the response part): inventing one would pair a response to a call the model never made", and `_response_part` (line 471) guards on `if call_id`. Neither branch is tested - every `call_part` helper in the suite passes an explicit id. The documented compatibility behaviour for pre-3.5 models is unproven, and `GeminiRequestError` for a 403 (`code in (401, 403)`, line 526) is likewise only exercised at 401.
  - Fix: Add a test with `types.FunctionCall(name=..., args=...)` and no `id`, asserting the tool is still called and the emitted `function_response` part has `id is None`; add 403 to the error-mapping parametrize list.

### cli-gates (13)

- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_events_schema.py:14** - The module docstring states: "the assertion is one-way, so wiring a producer for one of them does not break this test." Line 221 is `assert emitted == SCHEMA_EVENT_TYPES - NOT_YET_EMITTED` - strict set equality. The first commit that wires a `finding`, `coverage`, `evidence.requested` or `disposition` producer into the runner will break this test in exactly the way the docstring promises it will not, and the next agent will read the docstring and be misled about why.
  - Fix: Either make the assertion match the prose - `assert emitted >= SCHEMA_EVENT_TYPES - NOT_YET_EMITTED` plus `assert emitted <= SCHEMA_EVENT_TYPES` - or delete the "one-way" sentence and say plainly that NOT_YET_EMITTED must be shrunk in the same commit that wires a producer.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_events_schema.py:58** - `NOT_YET_EMITTED` excuses `finding`, `evidence.requested`, `disposition` and `coverage` on the grounds that they "belong to the US1 chat server". FR-013 requires the *provider layer* to emit "finding recorded, evidence request opened, coverage changed" as part of the uniform event stream, and this module's own point 2 argues `events.jsonl` is what the pane replays after a crash. If those four are pushed by the chat server over SSE only, they never reach `events.jsonl` and a post-crash replay loses every finding and evidence card. I found no task between T035 and T041 that names the emission explicitly (T041 only consumes them on the page), so the allow-list currently legitimizes a gap that nobody owns.
  - Fix: Confirm with the plan author whether the runner (T015) or the chat server (T037) emits these four. If the runner, the emission belongs in Phase 2 and NOT_YET_EMITTED should be empty. If the chat server, add a one-line comment naming the task id that will shrink the set, so the exemption is traceable rather than open-ended.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/benchmark/runner.py:45** - Three problems in one function. (a) Layering: `from swreview.cli import provider_factory` makes the benchmark library depend on the CLI, which already imports `benchmark.runner` at module scope - a deliberate circular edge that only survives because the import is deferred to call time, and that drags typer and the fake provider into any library-only consumer. (b) DRY: `_default_review_fn` (lines 32-56) is a line-for-line duplicate of `cli._review_fn` (cli.py:837-855) - same `ProviderSettings.from_env`, same `run_review(provider=provider_factory(settings), model=, effort=, key_source=)`. CLAUDE.md asks for aggressive DRY flagging. (c) Coverage: grep across `reviewer/` shows `_default_review_fn` is referenced only by its own definition and docstring - every CLI path injects `review_fn`, and no test exercises it, so it is untested code whose only job is to duplicate tested code.
  - Fix: Delete `_default_review_fn` and make `review_fn` a required argument of `run_benchmark` (every real caller already passes one), or keep one default that is literally `from swreview.cli import _review_fn as _default_review_fn` with a test that calls `run_benchmark` without `review_fn` and asserts it reaches `provider_factory`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:170** - `provider_factory`'s two real branches are never executed by any test. `tests/unit/test_cli.py` monkeypatches the whole function for every review test; the only two tests that use the real factory are `--provider fake` (line 388, the FAKE branch) and the no-key case (line 413), which raises inside `OpenAIProvider.__init__` before any client is built. So `genai.Client(**settings.client_kwargs())`, the `redact=` wiring, the `max_output_tokens=output_ceiling(...)` argument and the OpenAI `adapter(model=..., **client_kwargs())` spread are all unasserted. I exercised all three by hand and they construct correctly today, but a renamed SDK kwarg or a dropped `redact=` would surface only on the workstation - and a dropped `redact=` is silent (the Gemini adapter falls back to `_verbatim`).
  - Fix: Add two tests that monkeypatch `openai.OpenAI` and `google.genai.Client` with recording stubs and assert the exact kwargs `provider_factory` passes, including that the Gemini adapter receives a non-default `redact` that masks the configured key and the per-provider `max_output_tokens`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:208** - `class Effort(StrEnum)` (line 133) restates `providers.EffortLevel = Literal["low", "medium", "high", "xhigh"]` value for value, and line 208 bridges them with `effort_level: EffortLevel = effort.value  # type: ignore[assignment]`. The `type: ignore` is precisely what would hide the drift if either list changes - add a fifth level to `EffortLevel` and nothing fails; add one to `Effort` and the ignore swallows it. This is a DRY violation on a small, exactly-duplicated enumeration, and it is masked rather than surfaced.
  - Fix: Build the Typer choice from the single source: `Effort = StrEnum("Effort", {level: level for level in get_args(EffortLevel)})`, or keep the class and replace the ignore with `typing.cast(EffortLevel, effort.value)` plus an assertion (in a test) that `{m.value for m in Effort} == set(get_args(EffortLevel))`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/context.py:202** - T023 says the default must be taken "from `agent/settings.py` per provider at every use (`new_session`, both `build_context` overloads, `context_for`, ...)". `new_session` instead hardcodes one provider: `default_model(DEFAULT_PROVIDER)`, i.e. always `gpt-5.6`. It has no provider parameter, so the per-provider part of that sentence is not implemented. Every caller that omits `model` records OpenAI's model id regardless of what is running: `cli._context_for` (cli.py:537, used by all four `check` subcommands), `tools/golden.py:49`, and any future general-chat or MCP context that builds a session. Today `start_review` always passes `chosen_model`, so no review is mis-stamped - but the guard is a convention, not the type.
  - Fix: Either give `new_session`/`build_context`/`context_for` a `provider: ProviderName` parameter alongside `model` and resolve `default_model(provider)`, or make `model` required and let the two or three callers that genuinely have no model pass `default_model(DEFAULT_PROVIDER)` explicitly at the call site, so the OpenAI assumption is visible where it is made rather than buried in a shared helper.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:173** - The missing-key path is correct (exit 1, one stderr line, verified) but the message is the adapter's internal wording: `error: ValueError: an OpenAI adapter needs either an api_key or a ready-made client`. It names neither `OPENAI_API_KEY` nor the settings pane, so the engineer whose key expired gets no route to the fix. The same will be true for Gemini once a keyless Gemini run reaches the SDK.
  - Fix: In `provider_factory`, check `settings.api_key is None` for OPENAI and GEMINI before constructing the adapter and raise a `ValueError` naming the provider, the environment variable it looked at (`OPENAI_API_KEY`, or `GOOGLE_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`), and that the pane's settings file takes precedence - with `key_source` in the text. Assert the variable name appears in stderr in `test_review_on_openai_without_a_key_exits_1`.
- **reviewer/tests/support/contracts.py:20** - Two near-identical loaders now coexist: `load_contract` (line 20, hardcoded to the feature 001 directory) and `load_any_contract` (line 33, searches `CONTRACT_DIRS`). `load_any_contract` is a strict superset for every name 001 holds, so the older one is duplication kept only to avoid touching callers. Separately, `contract_registry()` (line 40) re-reads and re-parses every `*.schema.json` in both feature directories on every call, and `contract_validator` calls it each time - `test_events_schema.py` alone builds it once per test plus once per parametrize case.
  - Fix: Make `load_contract` an alias of `load_any_contract` (or delete it and update the callers), and wrap `contract_registry` in `functools.lru_cache` so the registry is built once per session. Both are pure reads of committed files, so caching is safe.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_events_schema.py:14** - The module docstring states that the NOT_YET_EMITTED assertion 'is one-way, so wiring a producer for one of them does not break this test', but line 221 asserts strict equality: `assert emitted == SCHEMA_EVENT_TYPES - NOT_YET_EMITTED`. Wiring the US1 chat server's `finding`/`evidence.requested`/`disposition`/`coverage` producers will fail this test, contradicting the documented contract and sending a future agent hunting a non-bug.
  - Fix: Keep the strict equality (it is the stronger assertion and matches the 'too many tests than too few' preference) and correct the docstring to say the set is exact and NOT_YET_EMITTED must be shrunk as producers are wired. Only weaken to `SCHEMA_EVENT_TYPES - emitted <= NOT_YET_EMITTED` if the one-way behaviour is actually wanted.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:1283** - `test_benchmark_run_passes_the_provider_and_model_to_every_package` runs against the `benchmark_set` fixture (test_cli.py:1219), which is documented as 'A one-package benchmark set'. With one package, `[provider.settings.provider for provider in built] == [ProviderName.FAKE]` cannot verify the property both `cli._review_fn` and `benchmark/runner.py`'s new docstring claim is the point: a fresh adapter per package, because adapters carry per-turn state (step indices, a client). A regression that hoisted provider_factory out of the per-package loop would still pass, and the test's own name overclaims.
  - Fix: Add a two-package fixture (the same tmp_package_dir referenced under two package_ids) and assert `len(built) == 2`, that the two RecordingProvider objects are distinct (`built[0] is not built[1]`), and that both carry the same resolved settings - or rename the test to what it actually proves.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:457** - `test_review_with_an_unknown_provider_is_a_usage_error` asserts only `result.exit_code == 2` and its docstring invokes FR-026, but Typer's `ProviderName` enum validation rejects 'anthropic' before `providers.get` is ever reached, so the test proves Typer's enum conversion works and says nothing about the RETIRED_PROVIDERS guard at agent/providers/__init__.py:248. It is also the weakest assertion in the rewritten file - exit code only, no message.
  - Fix: Assert the usage error names the accepted spellings (`'openai' in result.stderr + result.stdout` and `'gemini'`), so the test fails if the enum ever silently gains a member; and leave the retirement-guard assertion where it belongs, in test_registry_provider_neutral.py, rather than implying it here.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:170** - `provider_factory`'s gemini branch (the `genai.Client(**settings.client_kwargs())` construction, including the enterprise kwargs) is never executed by any test. Two tests use the real factory - fake (test_cli.py:388) and the openai missing-key path (test_cli.py:414) - but the gemini branch, which is the only place `output_ceiling(...)` and the `redact` lambda are wired into an adapter, has no coverage. A wrong kwarg name there surfaces only at runtime on a real Gemini run.
  - Fix: Add a test that monkeypatches `google.genai.Client` (or `swreview.agent.providers.get`) and calls `cli.provider_factory(ProviderSettings(provider=gemini, model=..., api_key=...))` directly, asserting the client kwargs, the model, `max_output_tokens == output_ceiling('gemini', model)`, and that the injected `redact` masks the key.
- **reviewer/tests/support/contracts.py:59** - `contract_validator` calls `contract_registry()` on every invocation, which re-globs both contract directories and re-parses every *.schema.json from disk each time (test_events_schema.py alone calls it 8+ times, and the parametrized turn-reason test calls it once per case). Separately, `load_contract` and `load_any_contract` are now near-duplicate readers differing only in the directory they search.
  - Fix: Wrap `contract_registry` in `functools.cache` (the files are read-only fixtures for the life of a test session) and make `load_contract` delegate to `load_any_contract`, or drop `load_contract` in favour of the one loader once its remaining callers are updated. Low urgency - the suite runs in 13s - but it is the kind of duplication the DRY preference asks to be flagged.

## Feature 002 Phases 3-4 (settings and review chat)


### chat-backend (15)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:955** - `worker.shutdown(wait=False)` does not stop `concurrent.futures.thread._python_exit`, which joins every non-daemon pool thread at interpreter exit. A shutdown signal that arrives while a turn is blocked in a provider HTTP call finalizes the session (good) and then hangs the process on that join until the call returns. Worse, when it does return the stranded worker runs `_play`'s `else`/`finally`: a second `finalize()` writes another `session.ended` and re-saves `session.json` *after* the ones shutdown wrote. test_chat_server.py:1080 shows the shape - it has to defer `provider_control.release()` until after its assertions precisely because the stranded worker would rewrite the files.
  - Fix: Mark the chat's executor threads as ones the process need not wait for, or set a per-chat `shutting_down` flag that `_play` checks before finalizing/settling so a stranded turn writes nothing after shutdown, and give `shutdown()` a bounded join (e.g. `worker.shutdown(wait=True, cancel_futures=True)` with a timeout guard) rather than an unbounded implicit one at exit.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:725** - `disposition` calls `_require_idle` and then `await run_in_threadpool(record_disposition, ...)`. The chat's state stays non-RUNNING across that await, so a concurrent `POST /messages` passes `_require_idle`, sets RUNNING and submits a turn on the chat worker while `record_disposition` is still mutating `run.session` and writing `session.json` + `report.md` from a different thread. The contract's Concurrency clause promises the server serializes per chat; the message and evidence endpoints do (no await between check and submit), this one does not.
  - Fix: Run `record_disposition` on the chat's own single-threaded executor (submit and await the future via `anyio.to_thread`/a wrapper around `_submit`) so it serializes with turns, or hold the chat in a short-lived `RUNNING`-equivalent guard across the await. Add a test that posts a disposition and a message concurrently and asserts one of them is 409.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:837** - `PACKAGE_ERRORS` includes `ValueError`, `KeyError`, `LookupError` and `OSError`, and wraps everything `start_review` raises as `InvalidPackage` with the message "<run_dir> is not a readable evidence package". `start_review` also calls `provider.effort_mapping(effort)`, which raises `EffortNotSupportedError(ValueError)` (openai_provider.py:206) - the deliberately named fail-fast error of data-model s1 ("fails fast with a named error; it never silently downgrades"). The pane is then told the package is unreadable when the real cause is that the chosen model cannot express the requested effort, and FR-008 requires the error's real class plus a next action.
  - Fix: Catch the provider-side errors before the package ones: wrap only the package load in `PACKAGE_ERRORS` (or re-raise `EffortNotSupportedError` and the adapter's own error classes as `ProviderFailed`/a dedicated `InvalidEffort` `ChatError` carrying the real class name). Add a test posting an effort the fake adapter cannot map and asserting the error_class is not `InvalidPackage`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:598** - `development` only changes what `GET /health` lists. A release build (no `--dev`) advertises `providers: ["openai", "gemini"]` and then still accepts `POST /sessions {provider: "fake"}` and runs the scripted script, writing `Dry run...` text into a real run folder. FR-027's stated purpose is "so a shipped configuration can never produce fabricated findings"; today only the C# settings loader enforces that, and the component that actually produces the findings does not.
  - Fix: Make `_settings`/`create_session` refuse `ProviderName.FAKE` with `UnknownProvider` (400) when `self.development` is False, so `/health` and `/sessions` agree. Same for `GET /models?provider=fake`. Add tests for both on a non-development app.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:965** - `run.close()` - which releases the live SOLIDWORKS bridge named pipe - is only reached from `_finalize_on_exit`, and that method returns early for any chat where `chat.finished` is True. So the ordinary paths (turn ends, `/stop`, a failed chat) never close the bridge: a US3 session's pipe handle is held for the life of the backend process. FR-019's "MUST stop both when the add-in unloads" is satisfied only because the process dies.
  - Fix: Close the run when the chat reaches a terminal state: call `chat.run.close()` from `_play`'s terminal branches (or from a small `_close_run(chat)` invoked wherever the chat settles ENDED/FAILED), guarded so a follow-up that reopens the session re-acquires a client. Assert in a test that the injected bridge factory's client saw `close()` after the session ended.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/sessions.py:345** - `record_disposition` emits `{"finding_id": ..., "disposition": disposition.model_dump(...) if disposition else None}`. `chat-events.schema.json` requires `disposition` to be a `Disposition` object (`required: [finding_id, disposition]`, `$ref`), so the `None` branch would write a line that fails the contract the events-schema test enforces. It is unreachable today (`set_disposition` always sets one), which makes it a defensive branch that can only ever produce an invalid event.
  - Fix: Drop the conditional and emit `finding.disposition.model_dump(mode="json")` directly, or assert `finding.disposition is not None` first so a future regression fails loudly instead of writing a schema-invalid event.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:1170** - `_scan_file` opens every file as UTF-8 with `errors="replace"`. A key written into a UTF-16LE file - the default encoding for `>` / `Out-File` redirection in Windows PowerShell 5.1, which is this project's primary shell - decodes to interleaved replacement characters, so neither the verbatim env value nor the `sk-`/`AIza` detectors match. `audit-secrets` reports "none" over a file that does contain the key. That is exactly the false green the command's own docstring says it exists to prevent, and T029 pins the loud-rather-than-green behaviour.
  - Fix: Sniff a BOM (or try UTF-8, then UTF-16LE/BE) per file, or scan the raw bytes for both the UTF-8 and UTF-16LE encodings of each secret and each detector pattern. Add a test that plants a key in a UTF-16LE file and asserts exit 1.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:623** - `bridge.secret` from the `POST /sessions` body is stored on the chat and then never used: `_start_review` passes only `bridge.get("pipe")` (line 832) and `bridge/client.py` has no notion of a secret at all, so the named-pipe handshake is unauthenticated despite the contract carrying the field. It is also absent from the redaction set - `ChatServer.secrets` is built from `KEY_VARIABLES` only (line 587/104) - so an error or log line that ever quotes the request body would carry it in the clear. `public()` correctly withholds it, so nothing leaks today, but the guarantee is accidental rather than enforced.
  - Fix: Decide one way: either have the bridge client present the secret on connect (and add a contract line saying how), or remove `secret` from the `POST /sessions` body in `chat-api.md`. Either way, add the supplied bridge secret to the per-chat redaction set the moment it is accepted so `self.redact` masks it, and add a test asserting an error body built from a request carrying a bridge secret does not contain it.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:958** - `_finalize_on_exit` finalizes a mid-flight chat but does nothing to stop the turn, so the stranded worker can rewrite what shutdown just wrote. It emits `turn.ended {reason: "error"}`, finalizes, and calls `chat.run.close()` -- which only closes the bridge client (runner.py:472) -- then `shutdown()` calls `worker.shutdown(wait=False)`, which does not cancel work in progress. ThreadPoolExecutor threads are non-daemon and joined at interpreter exit, so the process lingers until the provider call returns, `_play` then runs to completion and appends a second `turn.ended`/`session.ended` and re-saves `session.json` after the stream's terminator. Closing the bridge out from under a running turn also turns its next bridge tool call into a closed-pipe error. test_chat_server.py:1080 acknowledges this in a comment ("letting it run any earlier would rewrite the files just asserted on") rather than preventing it.
  - Fix: Set `chat.stop_requested.set()` at the top of `_finalize_on_exit` so the stranded turn stops at its next tool boundary, and mark the chat closed (e.g. a `ChatSession.closed` flag checked at the top of `_play`'s `else`/`except TurnStopped` branches) so a turn that finishes after shutdown does not emit a second `session.ended` or re-save the session. Then have test_chat_server.py:1080 release the gate BEFORE its assertions and assert the file is unchanged -- that is the version that actually pins the behaviour.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:1081** - The set of provider key environment variables is written out four times, and two of the copies have a correctness consequence. `cli.py:1081 KEY_ENV_VARS`, `chat/server.py:104 KEY_VARIABLES`, `tests/conftest.py:26 LIVE_KEYS` and `tests/unit/test_cli_audit_secrets.py:48` all spell `("OPENAI_API_KEY", "GOOGLE_API_KEY", "GEMINI_API_KEY")`, while `agent/settings.py:330-332` hardcodes the same names a fifth time inside `_key_from_env`. If a fourth variable is ever supported, `audit-secrets` silently stops scanning for it and the backend silently stops redacting it -- a DRY violation that degrades into a secret leak, not a style issue.
  - Fix: Put one `KEY_ENV_VARS` in `agent/settings.py` beside `_key_from_env` (which should read from it), and import it in `cli.py` and `chat/server.py`. Leave the copies in `tests/conftest.py` and `test_cli_audit_secrets.py` as they are -- the latter already carries a comment saying the duplication is deliberate so a rename has to be made twice, which is a defensible test-side choice.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_server.py:60** - The three chat test modules each re-declare the same helpers rather than sharing them: `EVIDENCE_ARGUMENTS` and `DRAWING_FINDING_ARGUMENTS` appear verbatim in test_chat_server.py:60-73, test_chat_sessions.py:51-62 and (the evidence one) test_events_schema.py; `call`/`turn` appear in test_chat_server.py:76-83 and test_chat_sessions.py:65-72; `wait_until` in test_chat_server.py:88 and test_chat_main.py:151; `events_of`/`start_session` in test_chat_server.py:210/240 and test_chat_main.py:172/187. The tool-argument dicts are the risky pair -- when a tool signature changes, three separate literals have to be edited and a missed one fails as an opaque validation error in an unrelated module.
  - Fix: Move the scripted-turn helpers and the tool-argument fixtures into `reviewer/tests/support/` (a `scripts.py` beside the existing `contracts.py` and `packages.py`), and import them from the three modules. Keep `wait_until` per-module if the timeouts genuinely differ; the argument dicts and `call`/`turn` should be single-sourced.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_server.py:1162** - `test_a_turn_with_no_tool_call_left_to_make_finishes_normally` does not test what its docstring claims. The docstring says "The stop flag is read at a tool boundary, so a turn that reaches none just ends", but the test never calls `POST /stop` -- it only asserts that an ordinary tool-free turn ends with reason `end`. The behaviour the docstring describes (Stop pressed on a turn that will reach no further tool boundary, so the 202 is honoured but `turn.ended` carries `end` rather than `stopped`) is the one real edge of FR-030's "next tool boundary" wording and is untested.
  - Fix: Hold the gate, `POST /stop`, release, and assert the documented outcome for a tool-free turn: 202 from `/stop`, the chat settles in `ended`, `session.json` has a non-null `ended_at`, and record what `turn.ended.reason` actually is. If the contract wants `stopped` even in that case, `_play` needs to consult `chat.stop_requested` after a normally-completed turn; if `end` is acceptable, say so in the test so the next reader is not misled.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:821** - The `bridge.secret` the contract has the pane send is accepted and then dropped. `_start_review` reads `bridge = chat.bridge or {}` and forwards only `str(bridge.get("pipe") ...)`; the secret is stored on `ChatSession.bridge` (correctly never echoed, per the tested `public()`) and otherwise unused, so the named pipe is currently opened unauthenticated. T049 is the task that wires `secret` through `bridge/client.py`, so this is deferred rather than wrong -- but there is no marker in the code saying so, and `fake_chat_script`'s bridge probes already exercise the path.
  - Fix: Add a one-line comment at server.py:821 naming T049 as the task that will forward `bridge['secret']` to `BridgeClient`, so the dropped field is visibly deferred rather than looking like an oversight to whoever picks that task up.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_sessions.py:496** - `assert isinstance(UUID(str(run_with_a_finding.session.session_id)), UUID)` is a tautology -- `UUID(str(x))` returns a `UUID` or raises, so the assertion can never be false in a way the preceding line has not already caught. It is the only non-load-bearing assertion I found in the module.
  - Fix: Delete the line, or replace it with the assertion the test's name implies is missing: that the emitted `disposition` body's `finding_id` matches a finding actually on `run.session` (the earlier `test_a_disposition_is_written_to_...` already does this, so deleting is the honest option).
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_server.py:480** - Three contract-adjacent cases have no test. (a) `POST /sessions` with an unknown `provider` -- `_settings` maps the `ValueError` from `from_env` to a 400 `UnknownProvider`, but only the `/models` route is tested for that (test at line ~455). (b) `POST .../disposition` while a turn is running returns 409 `TurnRunning` via `_require_idle`; T036's "disposition 200/409" is satisfied by the `IllegalTransition` case, but the concurrency refusal on this route is unexercised. (c) `--dev` is untested through either entry point -- `test_a_development_build_also_lists_the_scripted_provider` constructs the app directly, so neither the Typer `--dev` flag nor `__main__`'s `--dev` is proven to reach `create_app(development=True)`.
  - Fix: Add three short cases: `start_session(..., provider="acme-llm")` asserting 400/`UnknownProvider`; a gated turn plus a disposition POST asserting 409/`TurnRunning`; and one subprocess case in test_chat_main.py launching with `--dev` and asserting `GET /health` lists `fake`.

### settings-backend (12)

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:201** - AnyTurnRunning() swallows every exception from IBackendClient.IsTurnRunning and treats "cannot ask" as "no turn running". A single slow or failed GET /sessions/{chat_id} (30 s HttpWebRequest timeout in BackendClient) therefore lets settings.save restart the backend under a live turn — and because of the must_fix above that restart is a TerminateProcess, so the turn dies without finalization. The comment justifies the choice for a backend that is down, but a transient failure is indistinguishable from a dead one here.
  - Fix: Distinguish BackendUnavailable (endpoint null / connection refused) from every other failure: treat only the former as "no turn", and answer anything else with error {error_class: "TurnRunning"} (or a distinct class) so the save is retried rather than silently destructive. Add a PageMessageTests case whose FakeBackend throws a transient exception from IsTurnRunning.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:449** - Report() appends the raw `failure` (Exception.ToString(), i.e. message plus every inner exception and stack) to %LOCALAPPDATA%\SwReview\logs\addin.log with no redaction. BackendProcess wraps failures as `new BackendStartException(Redaction.Redact(...), processId, failure)` keeping the *unredacted* original as InnerException, so ToString() re-prints whatever the redacted message just masked. This is the only log path in the add-in that is not redacted, against FR-015's "MUST be redacted from any error text".
  - Fix: Route Report through Redaction.Redact(failure, secrets) using the resolved key (the same list ReviewHost.Redact builds), and add a test that an exception carrying the key reaches addin.log masked.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:983** - Half-configured Gemini Enterprise input is discarded silently: Enterprise() returns null when project or location is blank, the save succeeds, and the page is answered `settings.saved` as if everything was stored. app.js:964 does the same on its side. An engineer who fills in Project and tabs past Location presses Save, sees "Saved.", and watches the field blank itself with no explanation.
  - Fix: Refuse the save with error {error_class: "InvalidSettings"} naming the missing field (the Python side already raises exactly that error for the half-set environment case, settings.py:360), and keep the page from clearing the field it refused. Add the case to PageMessageTests beside the other InvalidSettings rows.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ProcessLifetimeTests.cs:146** - T050's sentence "two hosts in one process get distinct pipe names and ports" is only half covered, and the covered half is tautological: both stub backends are told which port and token to print by the test itself, so Assert.NotEqual(a.Port, b.Port) proves nothing about the system. Pipe names are not tested at all (the class docstring at line 30 acknowledges the deferral to T047/US3).
  - Fix: Either narrow the assertion to what it really proves (two live BackendProcess instances keep independent endpoints and process ids) and leave a named TODO referencing T047 for the pipe-name half, or drive the real `--port 0` binding in the T037a subprocess test. Make sure the pipe-name clause is actually honoured when T047 lands rather than left behind a checked-off T050.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Settings/UserSettings.cs:234** - A settings file that cannot be read or carries an unsupported version yields Defaults() in memory; ReviewHost then clones *those defaults* on the next settings.save (ReviewHost.cs:434) and overwrites the file. The stored api_key_protected, terminal_cli, python and run_root of a v2 file (or of a file that was momentarily locked) are destroyed by an ordinary Save, with only the earlier `status` banner as warning. PageMessageTests.SettingsSavePreservesFieldsThePageDoesNotOwn covers the healthy path only.
  - Fix: Record on SettingsLoadResult whether the file was *unusable* as opposed to merely missing/normalized, and have ReviewHost refuse settings.save in that state with an error naming the file (or back the original up before overwriting). Add a PageMessageTests case for the v2 file.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/BackendLocator.cs:98** - FindOnPath will happily return a .cmd or .bat launcher, and FromOverride accepts one too, but ChildProcess.StartSuspended passes the path as CreateProcess' lpApplicationName — which cannot execute batch files (it requires cmd.exe as the application). A workstation whose `uv` or `swreview` on PATH is a shim gets ERROR_BAD_EXE_FORMAT surfaced as "the backend could not be started: ...", pointing at Python rather than at the shim, which is exactly the failure mode the locator's own docstring says it exists to prevent.
  - Fix: Either restrict the search to .exe, or detect .cmd/.bat in BackendProcess and launch them through %COMSPEC% /c with correct quoting. A unit test over BuildCommandLine plus a .cmd stub would pin it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/BackendProcess.cs:296** - When the first stdout line is not a handshake, the line is never written anywhere — not even to the redacted log. The reply and the log both say only "the backend's first stdout line was not the {port, token} handshake", so the engineer has no evidence of what the backend actually printed. The stated reason (it might contain the key) does not apply to the log, which is written through Redaction.Redact with the key registered as a secret, exactly as every stderr line already is.
  - Fix: Write the offending line to the log via the existing redacted Write() (keeping it out of the exception message and out of the page), and assert in BackendProcessTests.AnUnparseableFirstLineIsReportedWithoutEchoingIt that the log contains the line with the key masked.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/BackendClient.cs:180** - StartOptions never passes `--dev`, which reviewer/src/swreview/chat/__main__.py:151 defines and server.health (server.py:598) uses to list `fake` among the providers (FR-027). A development-build pane therefore offers and saves `fake` while the backend it launched reports a provider set that excludes it — the two halves of the FR-027 affordance disagree, and any future consumer of /health's provider list (or a diagnostic) will be misled.
  - Fix: Pass `--dev` when ReviewHostOptions.BuildMode is Development (thread the mode into BackendClient), and assert the flag in BackendProcessTests.TheAllowedOriginAndRunRootAreOnTheCommandLine alongside --allow-origin/--run-root.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/BackendProcessTests.cs:89** - Assert.Contains("3", failure.Message) is near-vacuous in both the timeout test (line 89) and the early-exit test (line 110): the message always embeds stub.LogPath, a temp path containing a 32-hex GUID, so a "3" is present with ~87% probability regardless of whether the timeout seconds or the exit code were ever formatted in. The clause of T033 being checked ("times out with a clear error", "reports its exit code") is effectively unasserted.
  - Fix: Assert the surrounding phrase instead: Contains("within 3", ...) / Contains("3 seconds", ...) for the timeout, and Contains("exited with code 3", ...) for the early exit.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/UserSettingsTests.cs:190** - T026 asks for the key to be "protected with DPAPI CurrentUser". The tests prove only round-trip and tamper-detection: an implementation using DataProtectionScope.LocalMachine (readable by every account on the box) or one passing no entropy would pass every assertion here, including SavedFileNeverContainsTheKeyInTheClear. The entropy binding at UserSettings.cs:292/322 is likewise unasserted.
  - Fix: Add a test that a blob protected with different entropy (ProtectedData.Protect(plain, otherEntropy, CurrentUser), base64'd into ApiKeyProtected) resolves to Key==null with a non-null Error - that pins the application-specific entropy. The CurrentUser-vs-LocalMachine scope is not reliably observable in-process (CryptUnprotectData ignores the flag), so note it in the test doc comment rather than pretending to test it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/BackendProcess.cs:560** - DefaultHealthProbe is the code that actually runs in production, and every test injects options.HealthProbe instead, so it has no coverage at all. Untested properties that matter: the token goes in the Authorization header and never in the URL (chat-api.md), request.Proxy = null so a corporate proxy is not consulted for 127.0.0.1, a non-200 counts as unhealthy, and a connection refusal is retried rather than thrown.
  - Fix: Add a BackendProcessTests case using a System.Net.HttpListener bound to http://127.0.0.1:<free port>/ : assert the received request path is /health with no query string, that the Authorization header is "Bearer <token>", and that a 503 response makes WaitForHealth keep polling until it times out.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:455** - Save() catches TurnRunningException and BackendStartException around Backend.Restart and degrades gracefully, but any other exception from Restart (e.g. Win32Exception from CreateProcess, ObjectDisposedException from a disposed supervisor) escapes into Receive's generic catch. The settings file has already been written and _settings already swapped, yet the page gets only error{HostError} and never settings.saved, so the pane keeps rendering the previous settings while the store and the backend hold the new ones. PageMessageTests exercises only the TurnRunning and BackendStart paths.
  - Fix: Catch Exception around the Restart call (redacted into a status error) so settings.saved is always sent once the file is written, and add a PageMessageTests case whose FakeBackend.Restart throws an arbitrary exception, asserting settings.saved is still replied and a status{stage:"error"} explains the backend did not come back.

### host-page-control (13)

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.css:103** - `.field { display: block; }` is an author-origin declaration, so it beats the user-agent `[hidden] { display: none }` rule regardless of specificity. `#enterprise` is `<fieldset id="enterprise" class="field">` and `renderProviders()`/the provider `change` handler hide it with `ui.enterprise.hidden = currentProvider() !== 'gemini'` (app.js:876, 1102). The Gemini Enterprise project/location fieldset is therefore permanently visible for every provider, and the page's only defence for `hidden` elsewhere (`.banner`, `.panel`, `.details`) is the accident that those rules happen not to set `display`.
  - Fix: Add `[hidden] { display: none !important; }` near the top of app.css (and to the Terminal page's CSS when T060 lands), so the `hidden` property the scripts use is authoritative regardless of what a class rule sets.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:421** - `Save` pushes an unsolicited `models` message (`Send("models", null, ...)`) before an `UnknownModel` refusal. `contracts/pane-host-messages.md`'s "Host → review page (unsolicited)" table is exhaustive and lists only `status`, `document.changed` and `backend.stopped`; `models` appears only as the *reply* to `models.list`. The page relies on the undocumented push (app.js:136-141 `case 'models'` with a `payload.provider` guard the reply shape does not carry). ReviewPageContractTests only passes it because its HostToPage set is harvested from backticked spans in the reply column.
  - Fix: Either add a `models` row to the unsolicited table in `contracts/pane-host-messages.md` (documenting the `{provider, models}` payload) or drop the push and let the page call `models.list` itself when it sees `UnknownModel`. Pick one before T060 copies the pattern into the Terminal page.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:344** - `scheduleReconnect` never gives up. After a `settings.save` the host restarts the backend (new port, new token); the page learns the new endpoint through `requestInit(false)` but `state.chatId` still names a chat that died with the old child, and the open stream is never closed. The reconnect loop then re-issues `GET /sessions/<dead chat>/events`, gets a 404, throws, and reschedules forever at the 15 s ceiling, repainting "…Reconnecting…" indefinitely with no way out but a pane reload. The same loop runs if the backend exits without a `backend.stopped` message.
  - Fix: Treat a 4xx on the events stream as terminal (404/401 in particular): stop the loop, show an error card with Retry/Open Settings/View log, and clear `state.chatId`. Also `closeStream()` and clear the chat in `saveSettings`'s success path, since the chat is known to be gone once the backend is restarted.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:145** - FR-008 requires every error to be shown with its class and a next action. Several paths show only the bare message on the banner or the card status line, dropping `error_class` and offering nothing: `handleUnsolicited`'s `case 'error'` (`showBanner(asError(payload).message)` - `asError` computes `errorClass` and it is discarded), the `report.open`/`folder.open`/`log.open` catches (app.js:1092, 1097, 1102) and `requestInit`'s catch (app.js:1030). Only `startReview`, `sendFollowUp` and `stopTurn` build a proper `render.errorCard`.
  - Fix: Route these through `render.errorCard({error_class, message, retryable})` (or a banner variant that prints the class and offers Open Settings / View log), so the three next actions FR-008 names are present for host-side failures too - `NotFound` on report.open and `BackendUnavailable` on init are exactly the cases where "View log" / "Open Settings" is the engineer's next move.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageContractTests.cs:110** - T042 asks that "every message type it sends **and handles**" be checked against the contract. Rule 1 covers what the page sends, but rule 3 - the only check over handled types - filters literals through `DottedType` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z0-9_]+)+$`), so every non-dotted type the page handles is invisible to the scan: `case 'models'`, `case 'error'`, `case 'status'` in `handleUnsolicited`, and the `'init'`/`'ok'`/`'settings'`/`'settings.saved'` replies it consumes. A typo in `case 'modles'` would pass all four tests.
  - Fix: Add a rule that harvests the `case '<type>':` labels out of `handleUnsolicited`/`onChatEvent` (a `case\s*'([a-z][a-z0-9_.]*)'` scan is enough given the page's shape) and asserts each is in `Contract.HostToPage` or `Contract.ChatEvents`, so the "handles" half of the task sentence is actually enforced.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:330** - `onFrame` calls `onChatEvent(event)` inside the `ReadableStream` reader's promise chain with no guard. A throw from any renderer - e.g. `entry.card.parentNode.replaceChild(...)` at app.js:485 and 520 when the card was removed by an intervening `resetTranscript()`, or a malformed body from a future event type - propagates out of `read()` into `openStream`'s `.catch`, which tears down a perfectly healthy live stream and drops into the reconnect backoff. One unrenderable event costs the rest of the turn's streaming.
  - Fix: Wrap the per-event dispatch: `try { onChatEvent(event); } catch (error) { appendCard(render.errorCard({error_class: 'RenderError', message: String(error && error.message || error), retryable: false})); }`, and null-guard the two `parentNode.replaceChild` calls (fall back to `appendCard`).
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageContractTests.cs:246** - T041 names two rules the static scan is perfectly able to check and does not: the stream must be read with `fetch`+`ReadableStream` and "never with `EventSource`", and "the token never appears in a URL". Today the only thing standing behind either is a prose comment in app.js (lines 18-23); a future edit that switches to `new EventSource(url + '?token=' + token)` passes every test in this file, and the CSP would not stop it because the origin is the same.
  - Fix: Add two scans beside the key-field scan: reject `\bEventSource\b` in any page script, and reject a token reaching a URL (e.g. any string concatenation or `URLSearchParams` naming `token`/`access_token` used in a `fetch`/`Navigate` argument - the cheap version is to reject the literal substring `token=` and `EventSource` outright, both of which the page legitimately never needs).
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:260** - T043's origin guard has no test. `OnNavigationStarting`/`OnNewWindowRequested` cancel anything outside `https://swreview.invalid/`, and this is the control that stops a link in model-authored text from navigating the pane with the backend token live in the renderer - yet nothing asserts it, and the predicate `IsOwnPage` (line 299) is a private static that would be trivial to cover.
  - Fix: Make `IsOwnPage` internal (with `InternalsVisibleTo`) or public static and assert the table directly: the page URL and `about:blank` allowed; `https://swreview.invalid.evil.com/`, `http://swreview.invalid/`, `file:///c:/`, `https://example.com`, null and empty refused. The `OffscreenReviewPage` harness already exists if you would rather drive a real cancelled navigation end-to-end.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageContractTests.cs:124** - The class doc claims the three page behaviours it cannot prove - reconnect sends the highest seen `seq`, event 501 evicts event 1, the follow-up box is disabled between `tool.started` and `turn.ended` - "are covered by the host-side tests plus quickstart Scenario 1". They are not: all three live entirely inside app.js's closure and no host-side test in this assembly touches them. tasks.md leaves this as an open question ("Behavioural tests for the two pages"), and the comment reads as if the question had been answered. A false coverage claim in a test file is worse than an acknowledged gap, because the next reader stops looking.
  - Fix: Reword the comment to state the gap plainly, and reconsider the trade-off now that T042a has built a real in-page harness: exposing the three values app.js already holds (`state.lastSeq`, `state.events.length`, `ui.followupText.disabled`) on a debug-only accessor would let `OffscreenReviewPage.Evaluate` drive `onFrame`-shaped input and assert all three for a few dozen lines.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewHostTests.cs:578** - The `ReviewWorld` harness duplicates `PageMessageTests`' harness: `FakeChannel` (line 722), `FakeBackend` (line 798), and the whole `Receive`/`Reply`/`AllPosted`/`LastPosted` plumbing (lines 655-693) all exist twice, ~120 lines. The file's comment defends the split ("they fake different seams"), which is fair for the fakes - but the JSON envelope plumbing is mechanical, identical, and is the part that will silently drift when the envelope shape changes: the two copies would then disagree about what a reply is and one suite would keep passing on a stale contract.
  - Fix: Pull only the transport plumbing into a shared base or helper (`PageWorld`: `Posted`, `Receive(type,id,payload)`, `Reply`, `AllPosted`, `LastPosted`, `AssertNothingPostedContains`, `FakeChannel`) and let each file keep its own fakes and its own `Open()`. That keeps the stated independence of the fixtures while removing the duplication the project's DRY rule targets.
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/pane-host-messages.md:46** - Contract drift the T042 scan structurally cannot see. The `review.start` row documents the payload as `{}`, but T041/FR-028 require Retry to carry `retry_of` - app.js sends `{retry_of: chatId}` (app.js:572) and ReviewHost reads it (ReviewHost.cs:652) and `ARetryCarriesTheChatItIsRetrying` tests it. The scan compares message *types* only, so every payload-field disagreement between page and host is invisible; this one is real today.
  - Fix: Update the `review.start` row to `{retry_of: chat_id | null}` (and note the host defaults it to null). If payload drift is worth catching generally, the same table parser already in `PaneContract.Load` could be extended to pull the backticked field names out of the payload column and check that the page names no field the row does not list.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:215** - `_initializing` is set to true on entry and never reset, so `InitializeAsync` is a one-shot. After the fallback path runs (missing runtime, a transient `EnsureCoreWebView2Async` failure), there is no way to retry in that SOLIDWORKS session even though the cached faulted environment task is the only thing that would need replacing - the engineer must restart SOLIDWORKS after installing the runtime the fallback panel just told them to install.
  - Fix: Reset `_initializing = false` in a `finally`, and/or give the fallback panel a "Try again" button that clears `_environment` and re-runs `InitializeAsync`. If one-shot is deliberate, say so in the comment - the current code reads like an in-flight guard rather than a permanent latch.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:313** - `ShowFallback` drops the failed `WebView2` control without disposing it: `AttachReviewPageAsync` adds the view to `_reviewTab` and assigns `_reviewView` before awaiting `EnsureCoreWebView2Async`, and on failure `ShowFallback` sets `_reviewView = null` and `Fill` calls `page.Controls.Clear()`, which removes but does not dispose. A half-initialized WebView2 can already own a browser process, and `Dispose(bool)` can no longer reach it because the field was nulled.
  - Fix: Capture the view in a local, and dispose it on the failure path (or add the control to the tab only after `EnsureCoreWebView2Async` succeeds, keeping the "Starting the review page..." note in place until then).

## Feature 002 Phases 5-6 (tool service, terminal, MCP)


### tool-service (16)

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/InProcPipeServer.cs:502** - `Answer()` queues every syntactically valid request onto the application-thread pump before the secret is looked at: the `ISecretPolicy` check lives in `SwBridgeDispatcher.Dispatch`, which runs inside the `Control.BeginInvoke` delegate. So a caller holding no secret at all (or the general-chat CLI spamming `interference`) still occupies the SOLIDWORKS UI thread once per refused request, serialized behind real work. Serve/PROTOCOL.md's claim that 'a refused request never reaches SOLIDWORKS' is true of SOLIDWORKS *calls* but not of the SOLIDWORKS *thread*, and the refusal is a pure ordinal string compare that needs neither.
  - Fix: Give `InProcPipeServerOptions` the `ISecretPolicy` (or a `Func<string?, string, bool>`) and refuse in `Answer()` on the reader thread, returning `BridgeResponse.Failed(request.Id, SwBridgeDispatcher.UnauthorizedError)` before `_queue.Add`. Keep the dispatcher's own check as defence in depth so `BridgeSecretPolicyTests` still pin it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:350** - `reviewOptions.Bridge` is only set once `ToolServiceHost.Start` returns, and that start walks the whole component tree on the application thread. The window is not 'before the first document' as the comment says — it is 'until the attach finishes', which on a large assembly is tens of seconds starting exactly when the engineer opens a document and clicks Review. A review begun in that window posts `POST /sessions` with no `bridge`, so `chat/server.py` passes `bridge=False` and the three live tools are never registered at all. Nothing tells the engineer: the run simply has no capture/measure/interference, and those checks are absent from coverage rather than recorded as failed, which is weaker than the `document no longer open` path this same task took care to make visible.
  - Fix: Either post a `status` message to the Review page while the tool service is attaching and keep `review.start` disabled until `ToolService` answers non-null, or have `ReviewHost` wait briefly on the gate before building `NewSessionRequest` and, if the attach has not finished, say so in the status line it already sends.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:675** - `bridge = body.get("bridge") or None` accepts anything truthy. A body with `"bridge": "swreview-abc"` (a string) reaches line 942 as `bridge.get("secret")` and raises `AttributeError`, which surfaces as an unexplained 500 on `POST /sessions` rather than the 400 every other malformed field gets (`retry_of`, `provider`, `run_dir` are all validated). A well-formed object carrying `pipe` but no `secret` is worse: `bridge_secret` becomes `None`, the client omits the field, and the in-process host answers `unauthorized` on the very first call — a misconfiguration that only shows up as a dead bridge partway into the run.
  - Fix: Validate alongside the other fields: require `bridge` to be a Mapping with a non-empty string `pipe`, reject anything else with the same 400 path used for `provider`, and reject a `bridge` without a non-empty `secret` (the console host is reached through the CLI, never through `POST /sessions`, so a secret-less bridge over HTTP is always a mistake). Add a `test_chat_server.py` case per rejection.
