- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:942** - The bridge secret arrives in the request body but is never added to the run's redaction set — `self.secrets` is `environment_secrets()` plus the HTTP token, both established at process start. `self.redact` therefore masks the provider key and the bearer token but not the bridge secret, so any path that puts request state into text (a `bridge_factory` that raises with its arguments in the message, a future traceback log of the session body) writes it to the backend log in the clear. The C# side is explicit that the secret must never be logged; the Python side only guarantees it by the client never formatting it.
  - Fix: Extend the run's redactor with the session's bridge secret where `start_review` is called — pass a `redact` that wraps `self.redact` with `redact(text, (*self.secrets, bridge_secret))` for that chat — and assert in `test_chat_server.py` that the secret cannot appear in a reported error.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/InProcPipeServer.cs:509** - A line that fails `BridgeCodec.ReadRequest` is answered and returned before the request ever reaches `ToolServiceRequestLogger`, so nothing about it is written to the tool-service log. That contradicts the logger's own stated property ('Every request produces a line, including refusals and errors, because an audit of what did *not* happen is only as good as its record of what was attempted') and leaves the SC-004 artifact with no trace of a client sending garbage at the pipe — the one thing an audit most wants to see.
  - Fix: Log the protocol error from `Answer()` through the same `Action<string>? Log` the timeout and unavailable paths already use (`request malformed: <BridgeProtocolError.Message>`), which carries no client-supplied value, and add a case to `AMalformedLineIsAnsweredWithAnEmptyIdAndTheListenerSurvives` asserting the line is written.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceHost.cs:331** - `ToolServiceLog.Write` swallows `IOException` and `UnauthorizedAccessException` with empty catch blocks. This file is the artifact SC-004 is audited against (T047), so a log folder that is read-only, full, or held by another process produces a review that looks entirely normal and an audit with nothing in it — the reviewer cannot distinguish 'no gated member outside the reader set' from 'no records at all'. `ToolServiceLog`'s constructor calls `Directory.CreateDirectory` and will throw for a wholly unusable folder, but a mid-run failure is silent.
  - Fix: Keep the swallow (a log must not fail a review) but make it loud once: latch a `bool _failed` plus the first exception on `ToolServiceLog`, expose it, and have `ToolServiceHost.Start`/`ToolServiceGate` report it through the same `Report` delegate a failed start uses, so the SC-004 audit in T051 is told the log is incomplete rather than reading an empty file as a pass.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/bridge/client.py:249** - The `BridgeOpenError` text is hard-coded to 'the bridge circuit is open after three consecutive failures', but the circuit now has three ways to open and two of them are not three failures: the host's own `circuit_open`, and a single `BridgeUnauthorizedError` (line 271, `opened=isinstance(exc, BridgeOpenError | BridgeUnauthorizedError)`). An engineer reading the coverage report after a wrong-secret run is told SOLIDWORKS failed three times when in fact nothing ever ran. The `(last: ...)` clause carries the real cause, so the sentence in front of it is actively misleading rather than merely vague.
  - Fix: Record why the circuit opened in `_fail` (a small enum or the opening exception type) and phrase the message from it: 'after three consecutive failures', 'because the host reported circuit_open', 'because the tool service refused this run's secret'. Add a `test_bridge_client.py` assertion on the unauthorized wording.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:296** - `entity.show` now prefers `_toolService?.Session`, which is bound to whichever document was active when the tool service attached and is never re-attached (`ToolServiceGate` starts exactly once per add-in instance, by design). The previous code attached per Show with `documentPath: null`, i.e. against whatever is active now. After the engineer opens a second assembly, Show resolves and selects inside the first — still-open, possibly hidden — document, and `SelectByID2` may well report success, so the engineer sees 'shown' with nothing highlighted. T048 does say `entity.show` uses the same scope, so the direction is right; what is missing is the check that the scope still matches what the engineer is looking at.
  - Fix: Before using the tool service's session, compare `_toolService.DocumentPath` with `CurrentDocument()?.Path` (ordinal, case-insensitive) and fall back to the per-Show `SwSession.Attach` when they differ — or keep the scope and have `entity.shown` report `ok: false` with a message naming the document the reference belongs to, which the `full_path` field from T039 already has room for.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/InProcPipeServer.cs:611** - The accept loop `return`s permanently when `CreateInstance()` throws. With `MaxServerInstances = 4` (line 325), the listening instance counts toward the limit, so the 4th simultaneous client connection makes the creation of the next listener throw IOException ("All pipe instances are busy"), the accept thread exits for good, and the tool service accepts no client ever again - even after every client disconnects. Only a log line records it; `Dispose` is the only way out, i.e. unload the add-in. The comment at line 320-324 claims the consequence is "a refused connection is an opaque failure at the client", which is materially milder than what the code does. The same `return` also makes any transient creation failure terminal.
  - Fix: On a creation failure, `continue` after a short backoff (and re-try when a connection closes) instead of returning; keep the hard `return` only for `_stopping`. Either raise `MaxServerInstances` or correct the comment to say the ceiling is 3 concurrent clients plus the listener. Add a test that opens `MaxServerInstances` clients at once, closes them, and proves a later client still connects.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/InProcPipeServerTests.cs:60** - No test ever holds two pipe connections open at the same time - every test uses a single `Connect(...)`. Yet "two clients exist by design - the review backend holds the review secret and the CLI's MCP server holds the general-chat one" is the stated premise of the whole two-secret design (class docstring, lines 33-36) and of `MaxServerInstances`. The T046 clause "concurrent requests serialize in arrival order" is proved only through direct `server.Answer(...)` calls on three threads, which bypasses the accept loop, the per-connection reader/writer threads and the multi-instance pipe creation entirely. The one part of the transport the two-client design depends on is untested.
  - Fix: Add a test that opens two `PipeClient`s simultaneously, sends `interference` on the review-secret connection and on the general-chat connection, asserts one `ok`/one `unauthorized`, and (with a manual `FakeAppThread`) asserts the two connections' requests serialize through the single pump in arrival order.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceHost.cs:526** - `ToolServiceHost.Start` mints two secrets and hands them to a private constructor as two adjacent `string` positional parameters (`reviewSecret, generalChatSecret`, lines 416-417 / 524-527), which then populate `ReviewBridge` and `GeneralChatBridge`. Swapping those two arguments would give the CLI's generated profile the secret that authorizes `interference` - exactly the failure the scoping exists to prevent - and no test would fail. `Start` needs a live `ISldWorks`, so the composition is untestable as written, and `ToolServiceWiringTests`' `FakeToolService` only proves the *gate* routes whatever the host says. `NewSecret` (entropy, base64url, distinctness) is likewise untested.
  - Fix: Extract the composition below the attach (mint two secrets -> `ScopedSecretPolicy` -> dispatcher chain -> `InProcPipeServer`) into an `internal static` method taking a `BridgeServices` and an `IAppThreadInvoker`, and test it with the existing fakes: assert `ReviewBridge.Secret != GeneralChatBridge.Secret`, that a request carrying `ReviewBridge.Secret` gets `interference` through, and that one carrying `GeneralChatBridge.Secret` gets `unauthorized`.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/InProcPipeServerTests.cs:258** - `TheLateDelegateCannotWriteAStaleResponse` does not test what its name claims. I deleted the `if (_abandoned) { return; }` guard from `AppThreadCall<T>.Publish` and this test stayed green - only `AnAbandonedCallNeverPublishesItsResult` (line 285) went red. The property it asserts (a later request gets its own answer) is structurally guaranteed by one `AppThreadCall` per request plus one queue, not by `Abandon`, so the test would pass against an implementation with no abandon semantics at all. The T046 clause is still covered, but by the unit test next door, and the misnamed test invites someone to delete the real coverage believing this one has it.
  - Fix: Either rename it to what it proves (a timed-out request does not desynchronise the connection) or strengthen it: expose the in-flight `AppThreadCall` (or count publishes) and assert `Completed == false` / `Abandoned == true` for the stale call after `ReleaseOne()`, so the guard's removal turns this test red too.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/InProcPipeServer.cs:276** - The comment justifying the 120s default says it is "deliberately longer than the Python client's own 60s (reviewer/src/swreview/bridge/client.py, DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_S), so the two sides cannot disagree about whether a call is still running". `NamedPipeTransport.__init__` stores `timeout_s` but never applies it - `open(self.path, "r+b", buffering=0)` and the byte-at-a-time `_read_line` block forever - so the client has no timeout at all and the stated invariant is imaginary. (The desync it worries about is actually prevented by the request-id check in `_exchange`, which is real.) A false invariant in a comment is worse than none: the next person will reason from it.
  - Fix: Either say what is actually true ("the client blocks until the host answers; the host's bounded wait is what guarantees an answer arrives, and the id check in `_exchange` is what guarantees it is the right one"), or make `NamedPipeTransport` honour `timeout_s` - the latter is a feature-001 change and probably out of scope here.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:296** - `() => _toolService?.Session ?? SwSession.Attach(...)` pins `entity.show` to the tool service's session for the rest of the SOLIDWORKS session. That satisfies T048's "entity.show uses the same scope", but it silently drops the adaptive behaviour the fallback comment still describes: `SwEntityResolver` calls `session.Document` (SwEntityResolver.cs:77), so once the tool service has attached to document A, every Show acts on A even after the engineer closes it or switches to B. With a closed document that is a dead COM pointer on the application thread. No test covers it - `ToolServiceWiringTests.EntityShowSeesTheToolServiceScopeOnceItIsListening` asserts reference identity only, never what happens when that scope's document is gone.
  - Fix: Confirm the intended behaviour with the spec owner, then pin it: a test that the resolver reports a clean failure (not an unhandled COM exception on the pane thread) when the tool service's document has been closed, and - if switching documents should re-target Show - either restart the gate on `ActiveDocChangeNotify` or keep the per-Show attach when the run's package document differs from `IToolServiceAccess.DocumentPath`.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceHost.cs:108** - The logger's contract says "Every request produces a line, including refusals and errors, because an audit of what did *not* happen is only as good as its record of what was attempted" - but a line that fails `BridgeCodec.ReadRequest` is answered and returned inside `InProcPipeServer.Answer` before the queue, so it never reaches `ToolServiceRequestLogger` and leaves no trace in the SC-004 artifact. `AMalformedLineIsAnsweredWithAnEmptyIdAndTheListenerSurvives` asserts the wire answer but nothing about the log, so the gap is invisible. For an audit log whose job is recording attempts against the tool service, "someone sent 10,000 unparseable lines" is exactly the thing you want recorded.
  - Fix: Either log the parse failure in `InProcPipeServer.Answer` (id and error text only - never the offending line, which could carry a secret-shaped string), or soften the claim in the docstring to "every parsed request". Add the corresponding assertion to the malformed-line test.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ProcessLifetimeTests.cs:221** - `AConPtyChildIsInsideTheJobBeforeItsFirstThreadRuns` is still `[Fact(Skip = "T047/US3: no ConPTY child exists to start yet...")]` and throws `NotImplementedException`. It is the single skipped test in the C# run. The premise is now stale: `Terminal/ConPty.cs` and `Terminal/TerminalSession.cs` exist in the tree, so a ConPTY child does exist, and T050's explicitly named clause - the CreateProcess-suspended window in which a fast-exiting child escapes the job - is therefore unproven for the one child process it was written about. (Terminal-branch file; flagging, not touching.)
  - Fix: Hand this to the terminal branch: start the ConPTY child through `ChildProcess.StartSuspended` into the host's `JobObject`, assert `job.Contains(pid)` and that the marker file is still absent before `Resume()`, and un-skip. If the ConPTY path genuinely cannot use the job yet, update the skip reason to name the task that will, so it is not read as already-done work.

### mcp-server (12)

- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:273** - The bridge is tested only through the tool *list*. No test calls a `bridge_*` tool through the server, and the `bridge_factory` seam that `create_server` advertises ("a test passes its own", server.py:335) is never exercised - `create_server(run_dir, bridge_pipe="swreview-test")` in these tests builds a real `BridgeClient` against a pipe name that does not exist and then never calls it. That gap is exactly why the dropped secret above is invisible, and it also leaves untested that a bridge failure comes back as an error result with a `status: "error"` chat-log line (FR-022/SC-003 for the one tool group that can actually fail at runtime).
  - Fix: Add tests that pass a fake `bridge_factory`: (a) assert the factory is called with the pipe name and the secret; (b) call `bridge_capture` with a fake bridge that returns a capture and assert the result plus one `status: "ok"` chat-log line; (c) call it with a fake bridge raising `BridgeUnauthorizedError` and assert an error result plus a `status: "error"` line.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:556** - `test_swreview_mcp_never_takes_the_secret_itself` proves the rule with a substring check on typer help text (`assert "--bridge-secret " not in output`). Help text is wrapped and reflowed by typer/rich, so a future `--bridge-secret` option rendered at a line break, in a different column, or truncated would leave this assertion green while a secret became passable on the command line - the one thing contracts/cli-profiles.md and T056 forbid.
  - Fix: Assert behaviour rather than formatting: invoke `cli.app` with `["mcp", "--run-dir", str(run_dir), "--bridge-secret", "s3cret"]` and assert a non-zero exit (typer reports exit 2 for an unknown option), keeping the help-text check only as a secondary assertion.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:1115** - `--bridge-secret-env` given without `--bridge-pipe` is accepted silently: the variable is resolved (and hard-fails if unset, which is confusing on its own) and then discarded, because `create_server` only builds a bridge when `bridge_pipe` is truthy (server.py:346). The CLI then runs with no `bridge_*` tools at all and says nothing, so a profile-generation bug that emitted the secret variable but lost the pipe name would look like a working session with a mysteriously smaller toolset.
  - Fix: In the `mcp` command, raise a `ValueError` (already in `HANDLED_ERRORS`, so it becomes one stderr line and exit 1) when `bridge_secret_env` is given and `bridge_pipe` is not, naming both flags; add a CLI test for it beside the existing unset-variable test.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/mcp/server.py:362** - `toolset.sink.cli = _client_name(context) or toolset.sink.cli` is sticky and never resets. Since `Toolset`/`ChatLogSink` are created once per `create_server`, a client that identified itself once and a later request whose `client_params` are absent both write the earlier name; more relevantly, the fallback quietly relabels rather than falling back to `UNKNOWN_CLI`. The chat log is what the SC-003 audit reads to say which CLI made a call, so a wrong `cli` is worse than `"unknown"`.
  - Fix: Set the field per call to `_client_name(context) or UNKNOWN_CLI`, or pass the name into `record`/`ToolCallRecord` for that call instead of mutating shared state; add a test for a client that sends no `clientInfo` asserting the line reads `"unknown"`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:426** - SURVIVING MUTANT. `test_the_report_resource_appears_only_once_a_report_exists` calls `create_server(run_dir)` a *second* time (line 437) after writing `report.md`, so it only proves a freshly started server sees the report. The documented behaviour is stronger and is the whole point of the resource — server.py:265-268: "The report is checked per request rather than at startup because a review running in the Review tab writes one while the CLI is connected." I verified this is untested by patching `build_toolset` to snapshot `report.md`'s presence once per server instance and having `_resources` read that snapshot: all 26 tests still pass. A server that froze the decision at startup ships green.
  - Fix: Use ONE server for the whole test: inside a single `talk_to` scenario, `list_resources` (assert `[PACKAGE_SUMMARY_URI]`), then write `report.md` from inside the scenario (or via an `anyio.to_thread.run_sync`), then `list_resources` again and `read_resource(REPORT_URI)` on the same session.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:245** - Vacuously passable. `test_schemas_and_descriptions_are_the_canonical_ones` is a bare `for tool in talk_to(...)` with no non-empty guard, so a server offering zero tools passes it. Confirmed with an `emptylist` mutant (forced `Toolset.specs = ()`): only `test_bridge_tools_appear_with_a_bridge_pipe` and `test_missing_package_json_still_offers_every_tool` go red; this test and line 255 stay green. T058 names "schemas are the canonical schemas" as its own case, so it should fail on its own rather than lean on the list tests as a backstop.
  - Fix: Bind the list first and assert its size: `tools = talk_to(create_server(run_dir, bridge_pipe="swreview-test"), scenario); assert len(tools) == len(OFFLINE_TOOLS) + len(BRIDGE_TOOLS)`, then loop. Same one-line guard at line 261 with `len(OFFLINE_TOOLS)`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/mcp/server.py:424** - `serve()` is never executed by any test. `_invoke_mcp` (test_mcp_server.py:489) monkeypatches `mcp_server.serve` away, so `configure_logging_redaction((bridge_secret,))` on line 424 has zero coverage in this feature. The redactor itself is well tested (test_provider_settings.py:623-758), but nothing proves the MCP process installs it — and this is precisely the process whose stderr carries the bridge secret and whose logs the branch notes say are collected off the workstation.
  - Fix: Add a test that runs `serve` with `mcp_server.configure_logging_redaction` and `mcp_server.stdio_server`/`anyio.run` monkeypatched, asserting the redactor was called with a tuple containing the secret before the server was created.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:305** - `UNKNOWN_CLI` is never asserted. chat_log.py:53-54 states "the `cli` of a client that did not say who it is; a line is never written without one", and `_client_name` (server.py:237-242) has an explicit two-branch `None` path, but `test_the_chat_log_names_the_cli_that_called` only covers a client that *did* introduce itself. An implementation that wrote `cli: null` or omitted the field for an anonymous client would pass. (A `nocli` mutant forcing `_client_name -> None` is killed by this test only because the fallback happens to be correct today; the fallback's own value is unpinned.)
  - Fix: One test: drive a session whose `client_info` is omitted (or monkeypatch `mcp_server._client_name` to return `None`) and assert `[e["cli"] for e in chat_log(run_dir)] == [UNKNOWN_CLI]`, importing `UNKNOWN_CLI` from `swreview.mcp.chat_log`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:358** - `test_a_withheld_tool_called_by_name_is_refused` asserts the error result but never reads `chat-log.jsonl`, while its immediate neighbour `test_an_unknown_tool_name_is_a_result_and_is_logged` (line 345) does. SC-003 ("no missing call, including failures") applies to a refused withheld tool identically — an engineer reading the log should see that the CLI tried to call `check_interference_group`. Asymmetric coverage on the same code path.
  - Fix: Append one line: `assert [(e["tool"], e["status"]) for e in chat_log(run_dir)] == [("check_interference_group", "error")]`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:301** - Two near-tautological assertions in the flagship chat-log test. `assert entry["elapsed_s"] >= 0.0` cannot fail: `record_call` clamps with `max(elapsed_s, 0.0)` at registry.py:403. And `assert entry["at"].endswith("Z") or "+00:00" in entry["at"]` accepts two shapes when chat_log.py:72 pins exactly one (`.isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")`) — the `or` branch is dead and would silently accept a regression to the non-`Z` spelling that a CLI or the Terminal tab might parse differently.
  - Fix: `assert isinstance(entry["elapsed_s"], float)` (plus `< TIMEOUT_S` if you want a bound), and `assert entry["at"].endswith("Z")` with a `datetime.fromisoformat(entry["at"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))` round-trip to prove it parses.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/mcp/server.py:361** - The `calling` lock and the shared-sink `cli` mutation on line 362 are untested: no test ever issues two overlapping `tools/call` requests on one session. The comment says the lock exists because "the tools are blocking and the sink is shared" — but `ChatLogSink.cli` is a mutable field on a single shared `Toolset`, so if the lock were dropped, two concurrent calls could attribute a log line to the wrong CLI, and two `handle.write` calls could interleave. Low blast radius (stdio, one client) but it is an explicit correctness claim with no test.
  - Fix: One test: in a single `talk_to` scenario, `anyio.create_task_group()` firing two `client.call_tool` calls for different tools, then assert `chat-log.jsonl` has exactly two well-formed lines with the two expected tool names and the right `cli`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_mcp_server.py:408** - The contract's closing sentence — `contracts/mcp-toolset.md`: "Prompts: none in v1" — has no assertion. `create_server` (server.py:379-391) registers no `on_list_prompts`, so a CLI issuing `prompts/list` gets a method-not-found error rather than an empty list. Whichever is intended, nothing pins it, and a future hand-off of prompt support would not be noticed by this suite.
  - Fix: Add one test in the resources section asserting the intended behaviour for `client.list_prompts()` — either `pytest.raises(MCPError)` (method not registered) or `== []` — with a comment citing the contract line.

### terminal (11)

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Terminal/TerminalPage/term.js:214** - The page consumes `init` CLI fields the contract does not define. contracts/pane-host-messages.md's `ready` row is `init {clis: [{name, found, version, path, minimum}], last_choice}`, but term.js reads `cli.status` (line 214), `cli.display_name` (222), `cli.message` (289) and `cli.install_steps` (301). The page degrades gracefully to `found`, but the install-steps view that FR-023 requires only has content when the host sends `install_steps`/`message`, which no contract row obliges it to send — and the host that will answer this message does not exist yet (finding 1), so the shape is unpinned in both directions.
  - Fix: Update the `init` row in contracts/pane-host-messages.md to the row the page actually needs (`{name, display_name, status, found, version, path, minimum, install_steps, message}`), and extend the TerminalPageContractTests payload check to assert the page reads only documented fields, so page and host cannot drift.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Terminal/TerminalPage/term.js:83** - `request()` registers a pending entry and never times out, and `start()` (line 441) shows a banner only on rejection. If the host throws before replying, or replies without echoing the id, the promise never settles: the dropdown stays empty, Start stays disabled and the engineer is given no message at all — a silent failure of exactly the kind the install-steps view exists to avoid.
  - Fix: Give `request()` a timeout (a few seconds for `ready`, longer for `terminal.start`) that rejects with a message naming the message type, and delete the pending entry when it fires.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Terminal/TerminalSession.cs:231** - `ExitCommand` defaults to `"exit\r"` for every session, and `Stop()` writes it before the grace period. For `cmd.exe` that is right; for the Codex TUI, typing `exit` at the prompt submits a chat turn to the model rather than quitting — so the last act of the stop path is to send the provider a prompt and pay for a turn, and the session then ends by `Kill()` plus the job three seconds later. T053's sentence is 'stop with exit command then process-tree termination'; the exit command has to be the CLI's.
  - Fix: Carry the exit command on `CliDefinition` (Codex: `/quit\r`, or Ctrl+C twice if that is what 0.115 honours — verify on the workstation in T063) and have the host set `TerminalSessionOptions.ExitCommand` from the definition, leaving `"exit\r"` only as the raw-process default the ConPTY tests use.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ProcessLifetimeTests.cs:221** - `AConPtyChildIsInsideTheJobBeforeItsFirstThreadRuns` is still `[Fact(Skip = "T047/US3: no ConPTY child exists to start yet...")]`, but T053 landed exactly that child: `TerminalSession.Start` calls `ChildProcess.StartSuspended(info, options.Job, tree)` with `PseudoConsole` set (TerminalSession.cs:347-360). The skip reason is now false, so T050's 'assigned to a job with kill-on-close before their initial thread is resumed' is asserted for the backend child and left unproven for the terminal child — the one the task singles out as the risky case.
  - Fix: Unskip it and implement the body against `TerminalSession`/`ConPty` (assert `job.Contains(pid)` and that the child's marker file is still absent before `Resume()`). The file belongs to the tool-service branch's task, so coordinate before editing, or raise it there.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Terminal/CliProfileWriter.cs:454** - `<pipe>` and `<secret>` are substituted into TOML basic strings by plain `String.Replace` with no escaping (`.Replace("<pipe>", bridge.Pipe).Replace("<secret>", bridge.Secret)`), unlike every path, which goes through `PathText`. Today both values are safe by construction (`swreview-<guid>` and base64url from `ToolServiceHost.NewSecret`), so the byte-for-byte tests pass; a future secret alphabet or pipe spelling containing `"` or `\` would silently emit a config.toml Codex cannot parse, which surfaces as a CLI that ignored the generated profile.
  - Fix: Escape the two values for a TOML basic string (or assert their alphabet at the boundary with a clear exception), and add a test that a secret containing a quote and a backslash round-trips through the generated file.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Terminal/ToolListingCheck.cs:357** - `CodexBuiltIns` is `{ "apply_patch" }` and deliberately omits `shell`, while contracts/cli-profiles.md's 'Verification on first launch' paragraph — which T062 names as the source of the intended built-in set — still says 'Codex: the read-only shell and apply_patch'. The code is the stricter and correct reading under the 2026-09-13 `features.shell_tool = false` decision, but an authoritative contract and the gate it governs now disagree in writing, with only a code comment recording it.
  - Fix: Correct that sentence in specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/cli-profiles.md to drop the shell from Codex's intended built-ins (it is a one-line documentation edit in a file this branch may touch, or fold it into the T064 documentation task), so the contract and the gate state the same set.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/CliProfileWriterTests.cs:289** - Tautological half of `ShipsTheContractToolAllowlist`: the loop `foreach (string tool in CliProfileWriter.EnabledTools) Assert.Contains("\"" + tool + "\"", config)` cannot fail, because `BuildConfig` substitutes `<enabled-tools>` from that same `EnabledTools` list. It reads like a check that the profile ships the contract's allowlist, but it only checks that string interpolation works. (The `withheld` loop and the byte-for-byte config test are the assertions actually carrying that guarantee.)
  - Fix: Either delete the first loop, or make it non-circular by reading the expected tool names out of `contracts/mcp-toolset.md` (as the fixture README and T058 do) and asserting `CliProfileWriter.EnabledTools` equals that list in order.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/CliLocatorTests.cs:211** - `TheProbeGivesUpOnACliThatDoesNotAnswer` states in its summary that a timed-out probe 'must not leave a process behind when it is given up on', but asserts only `output.Failure != null` and that the text does not contain 'too late'. The `ping -n 30` grandchild that `CliLocator.Probe`'s `finally` is supposed to kill via `tree.Dispose()` is never observed, so a regression that dropped the JobObject teardown (leaving a 30-second orphan per dropdown refresh) would keep this test green.
  - Fix: Capture the pid before the timeout (or enumerate `cmd.exe`/`ping.exe` children of the probe) and assert the tree is gone within a bounded wait after `Probe` returns - e.g. expose the JobObject or assert `Process.GetProcessById(pid)` has exited.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/CliLocatorTests.cs:305** - `AShortVersionIsNotOlderThanTheSameLongOne` computes its own expected value from the data under test: `Assert.Equal(minimum.Build == 0 && minimum.Revision <= 0 ? CliStatus.Ready : CliStatus.TooOld, found.Status)`. With today's 0.115.0 minimum it asserts Ready; if the minimum ever becomes 0.116.1 the test flips its expectation and keeps passing whatever `ParseVersion` does with the missing component - which is the exact bug the test exists to prevent.
  - Fix: Assert against a fixed CliDefinition/minimum constructed in the test (or parametrize `ParseVersion("0.115") >= new Version(0,115,0,0)` directly) so the expectation does not track the production constant.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/TerminalPageContractTests.cs:394** - T053 requires the `terminal.output` post to be marshalled onto the UI thread (the read loop is a background thread and `PostWebMessageAsJson` has UI-thread affinity). The only channel test, `TheTerminalChannelSwallowsAPostWhenThereIsNoPage`, posts from the STA test thread onto a pane with no page, so it exercises neither `InvokeRequired` nor the `BeginInvoke` branch in `TaskPaneControl.PostToPage` (line 474). A change to `Invoke` - which would deadlock against `TerminalSession.Stop`'s `_watcher.Join` if Stop is ever called on the UI thread - or a dropped marshal would not be caught.
  - Fix: Add a test that, on a pane with a created handle, posts from a `new Thread(...)` and asserts the call returns without blocking and that the work is queued to the UI thread (e.g. record the thread id inside a stubbed `PostOnUiThread` seam, or assert the posting thread is not the one that runs the queued action).
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Terminal/TerminalSession.cs:414** - `Stop()` blocks its caller for `StopGrace` (3 s by default) plus `_watcher.Join(WatchdogMilliseconds)` (15 s), and the contract puts it on the host's `terminal.stop` handler, which is raised on the SOLIDWORKS UI thread. Nothing in the suite pins down where Stop runs; the ConPTY tests call it from a test thread with a 750 ms grace. As written, the first real `terminal.stop` on a CLI that ignores `exit\r` freezes SOLIDWORKS for at least 3 s, and up to 18 s if the last read wedges.
  - Fix: Decide and test the threading rule: either run `Stop()` off the UI thread and reply `terminal.stopped` from the completion (with a test that the handler returns promptly), or document a much shorter watchdog for the interactive path. Also catch/ignore `Win32Exception` in `TerminalSession.Resize` (line 407) - `ConPty.Resize` throws on any non-zero HRESULT and that would travel out of the page-message handler on the UI thread, contradicting the 'a resize arriving while the CLI is exiting is ordinary' comment.

## Feature 001 DRY review (T104), report-only findings

- The metre-to-millimetre factor is hand-written at nine sites (`geometry/axis.py`, `tools/measure.py`, `tools/checks_fastener.py`, `checks/hole_alignment.py`, `checks/fastener.py`); one `units.metres_to_mm()` should replace them, with a golden re-run.
- `tools/checks_fastener.py` `_bbox_extent_mm` computes an engineering number in the tool layer; move it to `geometry/`.
- `tools/checks_fastener.py` loses why a clamped thickness is unknown (unparseable vs absent `Thickness` property); distinguish in the layer source.
- `SourceRef` is rendered twice (`checks/result.py:cite()` and `report/markdown.py:_fmt_source_ref()`); one formatter.
- `{component.id: component}` maps are built in five modules; one `components_by_id(package)` in `ir/`.
- `cli.py` hard-codes the five coverage bucket names already declared by `report.session.Coverage`.
- `checks/engagement_rules.py` and `checks/tool_envelopes.py` are near-identical cached YAML loaders and disagree on `.resolve()`; unify path handling.
- `checks/golden.find_dimension` is a single-use alias for `resolve_dimension`; drop it.
- `checks/golden_fastener.py` re-implements fastener/hole/material lookup that `ToolContext` already owns.
- C#: `Dump/GapCollector.cs` `TryStep(Action)` and `TryStep<T>` repeat the same three-catch block.

## Polish leftovers (T064-T068 agents)

- `specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/cli.md`: the closing paragraph still describes the add-in as three Task Pane buttons; stale after the two-tab pane.
- `swreview-extract --help` lists options the CLI contract does not (`capture --note`, `serve --doc/--config/--out`); align the contract.
- `.github/workflows/reviewer.yml` `paths` filters do not include `specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/**`, so a contract-only change does not trigger the job.
- `extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Console/Serve/PROTOCOL.md` stayed in the console folder after the dispatcher moved to `SwReview.Extractor/Bridge/`; move it and update the four Python docstrings that cite the path.
- Workstation tasks still open: feature 001 T061 and T076; feature 002 T044, T051, T063, and the SOLIDWORKS/credential half of T067; Gemini spike T055a deferred.

## Feature 003 implementation reviews (2026-09-16)

Should-fix findings from the adversarial reviews of each feature 003 phase, the add-in load fix, the pane cleanup, and the feature 004 spec review. Must-fix findings were applied in the phase they were found; these were not. Grouped by review round; each names the file and line as reviewed, the problem, and the proposed fix.

## Phase 1-2 (2026-09-16)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:249** (python-ir-builder) - Regenerating `ir.schema.json` from the models dropped documentation from the feature 001 contract. Diffing every `description` string in the old committed contract against the new one, exactly one is lost: `components[].transform` -> "Row-major 4x4, translation in meters". That string only ever lived in the hand-written contract; `Transform` is a bare `Annotated[list[...]]` alias (models.py:90) and `transform: Transform` carries no `Field(description=...)`, so the generator has nothing to emit. The C# DTO still documents the convention (extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/ComponentInstance.cs:42), so the contract is now the least informative of the three copies about a unit-and-ordering convention a reader must not guess. Fix: Give the field the description it used to have - `transform: Transform = Field(description="Row-major 4x4 relative to the root assembly, translation in meters")` - and re-run `python -m swreview.ir.schema --write ../specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/ir.schema.json`. (Everything else about the regeneration checks out: the file is byte-identical to the generator output, and a canonicalised old-vs-new diff shows exactly four additions - `features`, `equations`, `rms_suppress_test`, `components[].constrained_status_raw` - and nothing else changed.)
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_features.py:113** (python-ir-builder) - DRY: `build_run` (line 113) and `build_row` (line 95) are second builders for `SuppressTestRun`/`SuppressTestRow`, duplicating `suppress_run` (tests/support/features.py:365) and `suppress_row` (features.py:338) added by T003 in the same change set. `build_run` is a near field-for-field copy of `suppress_run` with the same defaults (acknowledged True, baseline 0, restore_verified True, features_present defaulted from the rows). `component_fields` (line 134) is likewise a third copy of the `ComponentInstance` field set that already exists inline in tests/support/packages.py and in features.py. The constraint is explicit: never add a second mechanism for something the repo already has. Fix: Build the happy-path run/row through `suppress_run`/`suppress_row` from `tests.support.features` (`suppress_row(build_feature(), "ok")` works - the helper takes a built `Feature` and both helpers already expose outcome, messages, messages_truncated, limit, group and plan_file as kwargs), and keep a local constructor only for the cases that must pass values the IR should reject (`outcome="restored"`, 21 messages, `messages_truncated=-1`, `constrained_status_raw="over_defined"`), with a one-line comment saying why those cannot go through the shared builder.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:534** (python-ir-builder) - Two invariants that data-model.md section 1 states as rules are only written in `Field(description=...)` prose, not enforced: `features_present` "equals len(rows)" (line 534 / 539) and `unrestored_feature_ids` "Empty when restore_verified" (line 538). A `SuppressTestRun` that drops rows, or claims a verified restore while naming unrestored features, loads silently and the T056 rule then computes `<tested>/<present>` off a number nothing checked. The IR already has precedent for exactly this kind of cross-field check (`SourceRef._require_a_locator`, models.py:160), so this is not a new mechanism. Fix: Add one `@model_validator(mode="after")` on `SuppressTestRun` asserting `len(self.rows) == self.features_present` and `not (self.restore_verified and self.unrestored_feature_ids)`, and pin both in `test_ir_features.py`. If you would rather keep the model permissive so a partially-written run still parses for diagnosis, say so explicitly in the docstring rather than leaving the rule as prose - but then T054's C# assertions become the only enforcement.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_features.py:417** (python-ir-builder) - `test_the_new_gap_entity_kinds_validate` is tautological: `Gap.entity_kind` is a plain `str` (models.py:579), so the parametrized test passes for any string at all - "banana" included - and asserts nothing about the ten names data-model section 1 adds. T005 asks that "the new gap `entity_kind` values validate per data-model section 1"; as written the test documents the names but cannot fail if a producer misspells one. (Widening the field to a `Literal` is the wrong fix - feature 001 emits free-form kinds from `ingest/bom.py`, `package_builder.py`, `pdf_drawing.py`, `tools/bridge.py`, so a closed enum would be the breaking change the constraints forbid.) Fix: Make the tuple load-bearing instead of decorative: assert that the kinds the T003 builder actually emits (`feature_tree_unavailable`, `mate_suppression` in tests/support/features.py) are drawn from `NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS`, and have the later dumper/rule tasks assert the same against their producers. Failing that, keep the test but say in its docstring that it pins the vocabulary as documentation only, so a later reader does not mistake it for validation.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:311** (python-ir-builder) - Inconsistent bounds on the new integer fields. `messages_truncated` gets `ge=0` (line 536) but `Feature.index`, `Feature.depth` (lines 311-312), `Equation.index` (line 334), `SuppressTestRun.baseline_whats_wrong_count`, `limit`, `timeout_seconds`, `features_present` and `SuppressTestRow.whats_wrong_count`/`elapsed_ms` accept negatives. None of these can be negative in the domain (an index, a tree depth, a count of errors, an elapsed time), so a -1 from a mis-wired dumper would round-trip as valid engineering data. Fix: Add `ge=0` to the counts and indices that cannot be negative (`index`, `depth`, `features_present`, `baseline_whats_wrong_count`, `whats_wrong_count`, `elapsed_ms`) and `ge=1` to `limit`/`timeout_seconds` if zero is meaningless there, then regenerate the contract so the C# side validates against the same bounds. Low effort, no behaviour change for valid data; the only risk is a fixture that happens to use a sentinel negative, and there is none today.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_features.py:417** (python-ir-builder) - `test_the_new_gap_entity_kinds_validate` is tautological: `Gap.entity_kind` is a bare `str` and `git diff reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py` shows `Gap` is untouched by this change, so all ten parametrizations pass unmodified against pre-T006 code. The test asserts `gap.entity_kind == entity_kind` - i.e. that pydantic stores a string it was given. It is the one case in the T005 list ("the new gap `entity_kind` values validate per data-model section 1") that pins nothing about the implementation. The NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS constant does match data-model section 1 exactly (all ten, same spelling), so the value here is documentary, not behavioural. Fix: Option A (recommended): turn the constant into a real cross-check by asserting the ten kinds are exactly the ones the repo emits - `tests/support/features.py` already emits `feature_tree_unavailable` and `mate_suppression`, and the C# dumpers emit the rest, so a cheap version is to assert the list is a superset of `{gap.entity_kind for gap in <built rms_package>.gaps}` plus keep the per-kind validation. Effort ~6 lines, catches a future typo in a builder or dumper. Option B: leave it as an executable copy of the data-model list and say so in the docstring ("documents the vocabulary; `Gap.entity_kind` is intentionally unconstrained so a new extractor can name a gap this build has not heard of"). Zero effort, honest about what it is. Option C: constrain `Gap.entity_kind` to a Literal - this I would NOT do: it breaks forward compatibility with a newer extractor and would be a schema-breaking change to a feature 001 contract. I lean B given the constraint that the IR bump adds optional members only, with A as a nice-to-have if you want the list load-bearing.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_support_features.py:66** (python-ir-builder) - `assert_round_trips` only round-trips through the pydantic models (`EvidencePackage.model_validate(package.model_dump())` in python and json mode). Nothing on the Python side validates a *populated* 1.1.0 package against `specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/ir.schema.json`. `test_schema_sync.py` validates only `build_package()`, which has no features, equations or suppress-test run; the only populated-1.1.0 contract check lives in C# `IrSerializerTests`. Since the T030/T036/T043/T046 golden `package.json` files are produced by this builder and are the artefacts the extractor contract governs, a divergence between the models and the committed contract on a new member would not be caught on the Python side. (I verified the builder's output does validate cleanly today - errors: [] - so this is a missing assertion, not a live bug.) Fix: Option A (recommended): reuse the existing helper - `from tests.support.contracts import contract_validator`, and in `assert_round_trips` add `assert not list(contract_validator("ir.schema.json").iter_errors(package.model_dump(mode="json")))` with the error messages in the assertion message. ~4 lines, uses the repo's existing mechanism (no second validator), and every package the builder emits is then held to the same contract as the C# writer. Option B: a single dedicated test that validates one fully-loaded `rms_package(...)` against the contract, leaving `assert_round_trips` alone. Cheaper and faster, but only covers the one shape you remember to build. Option C (do nothing): rely on `test_schema_sync` plus the C# side. I recommend A - it is the DRY option, it costs nothing per call, and it puts the check on the path every future fixture already takes.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:534** (python-ir-builder) - `SuppressTestRun.features_present` carries the invariant `len(rows) == features_present` in its own Field description and in data-model section 2, but nothing enforces or tests it. `tests/support/features.py::suppress_run` lets a caller pass a `features_present` that disagrees with `rows`, and `test_ir_features.py::test_suppress_test_run_records_the_audit_fields` asserts the equality only for a run that happens to satisfy it. T054 asserts `len(rows) == features_present` on the C# producer side, so a Python-side fixture that violates it would sail through and then feed T056's rule tests a run shape the real command can never produce. Same class of looseness: `limit`, `timeout_seconds`, `features_present`, `baseline_whats_wrong_count` and `Feature.index`/`depth` are unconstrained ints (negatives validate), while `SuppressTestRow.messages_truncated` does carry `ge=0`. Fix: Option A (recommended): add a `@model_validator(mode="after")` on `SuppressTestRun` raising when `len(self.rows) != self.features_present`, and a test asserting the mismatch is refused. ~6 lines, makes the documented invariant real for both producers, and is the kind of edge case the repo's preferences say to catch rather than trust. Risk: if the C# command ever legitimately writes a partial run it would now be rejected - but T054's own assertion says it never does. Option B: enforce it only in the test builder (`suppress_run` raises on a mismatch). Keeps the IR permissive toward a future extractor, but leaves the invariant unenforced for real packages read off disk. Option C: add `ge=0` to the int fields and leave the row-count invariant to the C# side. Partial, and it leaves the one invariant a rule actually reads unguarded. I recommend A plus the `ge=0` constraints on `features_present`/`limit`/`timeout_seconds`/`baseline_whats_wrong_count`; I would leave `Feature.index`/`depth` alone since the indexer owns them and the C# DTO is already the authority.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_features.py:113** (python-ir-builder) - DRY: `build_row`/`build_run` here duplicate `suppress_row`/`suppress_run` in `tests/support/features.py` (T003), field for field, and the two sets of defaults have already drifted - `build_run` defaults `group="Detail"`, `suppress_run` defaults `group="4-Detail"` (only the latter is an actual RMS group name from `rms_types.yaml`). `component_fields` likewise re-states the `ComponentInstance` field set that `tests/support/packages.py::build_package` already owns. Two parallel definitions of the same fixture shape is exactly the repetition the project rules say to flag aggressively; the concrete risk is a later reader taking `group="Detail"` for a real group name. Fix: Option A (recommended): keep the local builders (the T005 module deliberately exercises the models without going through a builder layer, and it needs to pass invalid kwargs like `is_folder=True` that the specs layer cannot express) but remove the drift - make `build_run`'s `group` default `"4-Detail"`, and add a one-line comment saying why this module does not import `tests.support.features`. Effort ~2 lines, zero risk, keeps the layering argument explicit rather than accidental. Option B: have `build_row`/`build_run` delegate to `suppress_row`/`suppress_run`. Genuinely DRY, but it couples the model-contract tests to the fixture layer built on top of those models, so a bug in the builder reads as a bug in the IR. Option C (do nothing): the duplication is ~35 lines of flat literals and the divergent default is harmless. I recommend A: it gets the honesty of B's intent without inverting the dependency, and it matches "explicit over clever".
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_support_features.py:533** (python-ir-builder) - Several builder options that downstream tasks will depend on are never exercised, so their first use will be inside a rule test where a silent misbehaviour reads as a rule bug: `InstanceSpec.referenced_configuration` (the override path - only the default `spec.referenced_configuration or configuration` branch is covered), `PartSpec.material`, `MateSpec.alignment`, `suppress_run`'s `limit`/`timeout_seconds`/`plan_file`/`features_present` overrides, `end_tag(**kwargs)` pass-through, and `rms_package(assembly=..., parts=())` - the assembly with no child components that T032 needs for "no children skips". I probed the last two by hand and both behave correctly today (assembly-only yields components `['cmp:0001']`; `referenced_configuration='Machined'` overrides a `Default` part), so this is coverage, not a defect. Fix: Option A (recommended): add two small tests - one asserting `rms_package(assembly=AssemblySpec())` yields exactly the root instance and no part documents (the T032 precondition), and one asserting the `InstanceSpec.referenced_configuration` override wins over the part's configuration. ~12 lines, and they cover the two options a later task actually depends on. Leave `material`, `alignment` and the `suppress_run` numeric overrides uncovered - they are plain pass-throughs with no branching. Option B: cover every option exhaustively, one test per parameter. Matches "too many tests rather than too few", but most of them would assert a dataclass field reached a pydantic field unchanged, which is the tautology class flagged above. Option C (do nothing): the rule tests will exercise these in a few tasks' time. I recommend A - it targets the two options with real branching logic behind them and leaves the pass-throughs to their first real use.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:26** (table-groups-registry) - T012's scope is `checks/rms/registry.py` and `checks/rms/__init__.py`. This change also creates three source modules owned by later tasks - part.py (T020), assembly.py (T033), equations.py (T040) - which other agents are editing concurrently, and each ends with `for _rule in evaluable(): if _rule.scope == SCOPE: bind(_rule.id)(_not_implemented)`. That loop binds whatever the registry happens to list, so T011's required assertion 'every evaluable rule ... has a function' (test_rms_registry.py TestEvaluableRules / TestInvariant) can never fail for these scopes: an id added to the registry with no evaluator is auto-stubbed rather than reported. It also hides the T057 sequencing problem (rms.detail.individually_suppressible is bound by the loop today, and T020 will have to keep stubbing it until T057). Fix: Either drop the three modules and record the red T011 invariant as a declared deviation until T020/T033/T040 land, or keep them but bind each rule id by an explicit literal list (`@bind('rms.folders.present')` ... one line per id) so the file names exactly what it owes and the registry/evaluator drift the test is meant to catch stays catchable. Flag the out-of-scope file creation to the orchestrator either way.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:193** (table-groups-registry) - `_parse_classes` enforces that the eleven class sets are pairwise disjoint, but nothing enforces that `ambiguous` is disjoint from them, and `classify` (line 117) checks the class sets FIRST. So a table that lists ICE in both `ambiguous` and `cut` silently classifies it `cut`. The shipped YAML's own comment records that upstream keeps ICE in both its cut and hole sets, so this is the exact mis-edit an engineer is likely to make, and it turns 'unknown stays unknown' into a definite class: rms.detail.holes_last would grade an ICE feature instead of reporting it unresolved. Only a test (test_the_ambiguous_set_is_disjoint_from_every_class) guards it, and only for the shipped file. Fix: In `_parse`, after `_parse_classes`, raise ValueError naming the offending type name(s) when `ambiguous` intersects any class set (same message shape as the existing disjointness error), and add a loader test writing such a table.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/registry.py:51** (table-groups-registry) - `CoverageBucket = Literal['unresolved', 'out_of_scope']` shadows the existing public `swreview.report.session.CoverageBucket = Literal['checked','skipped','unresolved','failed','out_of_scope']` with the same name and a different value set, and re-exports it from `checks/rms/__init__.py`. Two same-named bucket types in one codebase is exactly the 'second mechanism for something the repo already has' the constraints forbid; a report-layer author importing the wrong one gets no type error until a bucket is missing. Fix: Rename to `RuleCoverageBucket` (or `CoverageOnlyBucket`) and derive it from the session type so the subset relationship is explicit, e.g. keep the Literal but add a module-level assertion that its members are a subset of `swreview.report.session.CoverageBucket`'s.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:265** (table-groups-registry) - DRY: `_parse(...)` + `@lru_cache(maxsize=4) def _load_cached(path) -> T: return _parse(yaml.safe_load(path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')), path)` + `load_x(path=None)` is now the third verbatim copy of the same loader trio (engagement_rules.py:91-101, tool_envelopes.py:91-99). The constitution requires extraction 'once it appears twice with the same intent'; it has now appeared three times, and the copies have already drifted - tool_envelopes.load_envelopes does NOT call .resolve() while the other two do, so its 'cached by resolved path' claim is false. Fix: Extract one helper (e.g. `checks/_yaml_table.py` with `cached_loader(parse)` returning a `load(path=None)` that resolves and caches), and have all three tables use it. Keep each module's `_parse` as-is. Out of T008's stated scope, so raise it with the orchestrator rather than editing engagement_rules.py/tool_envelopes.py unasked.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:104** (table-groups-registry) - `RmsTypeTable` is `frozen=True` but two of its fields are plain mutable dicts (`classes`, `constrained_status_map`), and `load_table` hands every caller the SAME cached instance. One rule module doing `table.classes['cut'] |= {...}` or `table.constrained_status_map[7] = 'fully_defined'` mutates the table for the whole process, which is precisely how a favourable default for engineering data gets introduced without a file change. Fix: Wrap both in `types.MappingProxyType` at construction (the values are already frozensets), or store them as tuples of pairs and expose lookups only through `classify`/`constrained_status`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:238** (table-groups-registry) - `_parse` reaches for `document['assembly']`, `document['version']`, `document['groups']`, `document['folder_type']`, `document['ambiguous']`, `document['tolerated_loose']`, `document['default_names_excluded']` and `assembly['reference_entity_kinds']` with bare subscripts, so a table missing a key raises a bare KeyError with no path in the message, while every other malformed-table case raises `ValueError(f'{path}: ...')`. An engineer editing rms_types.yaml gets two different failure shapes for the same class of mistake, and `groups` is never checked for the six unique names `assign_groups` then indexes into. Fix: Add one `_require(document, key, path)` helper raising `ValueError(f'{path}: missing key {key!r}')`, use it for every top-level read, and validate that `groups` is a non-empty list of unique names.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_rms_groups.py:223** (table-groups-registry) - Every derived-subfolder test goes through `build_features`, whose 'flat' shape flattens a feature's sub-features as well as a folder's contents, so no test in the module ever produces a row whose `folder_id` points at a non-folder, nor a document that mixes flat folders with sub-feature nesting. Both must_fix defects above sit entirely in that blind spot and the module is otherwise thorough, so the coverage gap is what let them through. Fix: Add two tests that construct `Feature` rows directly (as test_rms_type_table.py's `build_feature` already does) rather than through the builder: (a) nested shape, a sketch at depth 1 under an extrusion that itself sits in a non-group subfolder - assert the sketch's derived subfolder is the SUBFOLDER, not the extrusion; (b) flat folders with end tags plus that same sketch nesting - assert the flat folder's members still get its id. Separately, consider teaching `build_features` a per-spec 'sub_feature' flag so the builder can express the mixed shape (T003's owner).
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/groups.py:75** (table-groups-registry) - The traversal shape is decided once per document by `any(row.folder_id is not None or row.depth > 0)`, and since `folder_id`/`depth` come from sub-feature structure of ANY feature (not just folders), a mixed tree — folders reported flat with end-tag markers while features list their absorbed sketches as sub-features, which is the ordinary SOLIDWORKS tree shape — is classified `nested`. End-tag markers are then ignored, so every flat folder's membership is silently lost and `subfolder_by_feature_id` is null for all of its members (and misattributed for the nested sketches). research.md's open question 1 says the probe (T062) has not yet shown which shape 2024 reports, so this is not hypothetical. Fix: Decide membership per folder rather than per document: a folder whose id appears as some row's `folder_id` owns those rows; otherwise fall back to the end-tag span. Failing that, restrict the `nested` test to rows whose enclosing row is a folder (`table.is_folder`), which removes the sketch-under-feature false positive, and record the decision in the docstring.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/groups.py:75** (table-groups-registry) - A document with no nesting anywhere is ambiguous between the two shapes and is read as flat, so an empty non-group folder captures every following feature as its derived subfolder. Verified: nested specs [folder('Misc'), A, B] yields subfolder 'Misc' for A and B although in the nested shape they are outside it. That is the unsafe direction for a `fail` rule — it manufactures a coupled pair — and it contradicts the module's own reasoning elsewhere ("a fail rule must not be excused by a missing tag"). The neighbouring test test_a_flat_folder_left_unclosed_keeps_its_members_to_the_end pins the same ambiguous input to the flat reading, so the choice is deliberate but undocumented as a risk. Fix: Either treat an ambiguous document (no nesting, no end-tag rows anywhere) as nested, so a folder with no listed contents owns nothing, or keep the flat reading and state the trade-off in the docstring plus a test named for it. Note the current unclosed-folder test would need revisiting under the first option.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:227** (table-groups-registry) - `_parse` indexes required top-level keys directly (`document["assembly"]`, `document["groups"]`, `document["folder_type"]`, `document["version"]`, `document["ambiguous"]`, …), so a table missing one raises a bare `KeyError('assembly')` with no file path, while every other malformed-table path raises a `ValueError` naming the path. The "a table that cannot be trusted" block (tests 375-430) covers overlapping classes, an unknown class, a missing class, an unavailable status value, non-int status keys and a non-mapping document, but no missing required key — so the inconsistency is untested. Fix: Validate the required top-level keys once and raise `ValueError(f"{path}: missing ...")`, and add a parametrized test that deletes each required key and asserts the message names the path and the key.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_rms_registry.py:280** (table-groups-registry) - `test_binding_returns_the_function_unchanged` inserts a synthetic rule into the module-global `RULES` dict and removes it in `finally`. pytest-randomly is installed (the suite randomises order), and any future parallelism or a failure between insert and delete leaves a 35th rule visible to `test_exactly_the_contract_ids_are_registered`, `by_scope` and the count assertions. The mutation is invisible from the other tests' point of view only by luck of single-threaded execution. Fix: Exercise `bind` against a local dict (e.g. give `bind` the registry to bind into, or patch `registry.RULES` with `monkeypatch.setitem`/`monkeypatch.setattr` so pytest restores it) rather than mutating the shared catalogue by hand.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:268** (exceptions-recording) - `fingerprint(..., "feature_tree")` is chosen for every check id starting with `rms.` (fingerprint_kind_for, line ~88), but it only hashes `package.features`/`package.equations` for the components' documents. Assembly documents carry no `features[]` rows (data-model.md §1: `document_id` is "the part document that owns the tree"), so an exception accepted for a fail-severity assembly rule - `rms.assembly.mates_to_reference_geometry` or `rms.assembly.first_component_fixed` (contracts/rules.md:80-81), both acceptable through the import per data-model.md §3 - hashes `{"document_id":"doc:1","features":[],"equations":[]}`, a digest that can never change. `refresh` will never move such a waiver to `needs_review` no matter how the assembly is re-mated or re-fixed: a silent permanent waiver. tests/unit/test_exceptions_feature_tree.py:406 asserts this as intended behaviour without noting the consequence. (The digest does at least differ per document, since `document_id` is in the payload.) Fix: data-model.md §3 only contemplates part documents here, so this needs the owner's call rather than an unilateral redesign. Options: (A) refuse it loudly - raise from `fingerprint` when `kind == "feature_tree"` and the selected documents contribute zero feature and zero equation rows, so an un-bindable waiver fails at `accept` instead of silently freezing (small, matches "no silent failure"); (B) extend the feature-tree payload for assembly documents with the mate rows and the instances' `constrained_status_raw`/`is_fixed`/Toolbox identity (correct, but is new contract surface beyond T015/T016); (C) do nothing and record the limitation in the module docstring plus a backlog entry. Recommend (A) now and raise (B) with the owner before T033/T035 land assembly findings that can be accepted.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:210** (exceptions-recording) - `_feature_row` collapses absence with unknown in two slots, against the constitution's "unknown stays unknown, absence is null plus a Gap": `None if sketch is None else sketch.raw_status` gives `None` both for a feature that is not a sketch and for a sketch whose `GetConstrainedStatus` failed (the `sketch_status` gap), and line 201/212 gives `None` both for a feature with no `fillet` object and for a simple fillet whose `default_radius` could not be read (the `fillet_radius` gap, which is also how a variable fillet is recorded). A feature that stops being a fillet and a fillet whose radius became unreadable are different facts for `rms.quarantine.largest_fillet_first` (contracts/rules.md:35) but hash identically. In practice `type_name` usually separates the sketch case, and nothing today separates the fillet case. Fix: Make the slots two-valued instead of one: emit a presence flag next to the value, e.g. `[sketch is not None, None if sketch is None else sketch.raw_status, ...]` and `[fillet is not None, None if radius is None else [...]]`. Cheap, explicit, and it keeps "no fillet", "variable/unreadable fillet" and "0.002 m fillet" as three distinct digests. Add a test pairing `fillet=None` against `FilletInfo(default_radius=None)` on the same row.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/context.py:186** (exceptions-recording) - `replace_coverage` drops the check's items only from the bucket being written, which is exactly what data-model.md §2 says, but it means a rule that changes bucket between two runs of the same tool leaves a stale item behind: run 1 writes `rms.detail.holes_last` into `skipped`, the engineer adds a hole and re-runs, run 2 writes it into `checked` via `replace_coverage(check, "checked", ...)` and the `skipped` item from run 1 survives. The session then reports the same rule in two buckets, and the `modeling.resilience` summary ("in `checked` when no rule was unresolved for any document") is computed over a coverage set that contradicts itself. Nothing in T017/T018 is wrong; the hazard lands on T022's report layer, which is being written now. Fix: Leave the per-bucket contract as specified and make the obligation explicit: note in the `replace_coverage` docstring that the caller must call it for every bucket a rule can land in (passing an empty/absent item is not possible, so the report layer must clear the other buckets), or add a sibling `replace_coverage_across_buckets(check, bucket, item)` that filters `check` out of all buckets before recording. Recommend the docstring note plus a T022 test asserting that a rule which moves from `skipped` to `checked` appears exactly once in the session - flag it to the T022 owner rather than changing the contract here.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_exceptions_feature_tree.py:585** (exceptions-recording) - test_a_feature_tree_exception_ignores_the_geometry is the one test here that can go green while asserting nothing. It mutates package.components[0].transform[3][0] to 0.001 and asserts store.refresh(package) == [], but never asserts that this mutation actually moves the geometry digest. If _component_token ever stopped hashing that row (or the transform shape changed), the test would keep passing while proving nothing about kind isolation. Its mirror image, test_a_geometry_exception_ignores_the_feature_tree, is safe because CHANGED_TREES['inserted'] is independently proven to move the tree digest. Fix: Add one line before the refresh assertion: `assert fingerprint(package, BOTH) != GEOMETRY_DIGEST_BOTH` (the mutated package's geometry digest must differ from the pinned constant), so the test proves the mutation is one a geometry exception would have noticed.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:190** (exceptions-recording) - Nothing pins that the feature-tree hash is LIMITED to data-model section 3's field list. Every listed field is proven to move the digest, but no test proves that a field outside the list does not. Feature.error_code in particular is rebuild-volatile: if it (or parent_ids, persist_ref, configuration, or id) ever crept into _feature_row, every RMS exception in the repo would silently flip to needs_review on an unrelated rebuild and not one test would fail. This is the whole point of excluding the description text, and that exclusion IS tested - the other exclusions are not. Fix: Add a parametrized negative case beside test_the_description_text_itself_is_not_hashed, e.g. UNHASHED_CHANGES over _with(base_features(), 1, error_code=2), parent_ids=[], persist_ref=persist_ref('other'), configuration='Cold', id='feat:0099', plus an Equation change to `text`/`value`, each asserting the feature_tree digest is unchanged.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:224** (exceptions-recording) - _document_rows sorts features and equations by `index` so the digest is independent of the order rows happen to sit in package.features. Nothing tests that. build_package's helper emits rows in index order, so deleting both `sorted(...)` calls would leave the whole suite green while quietly making the digest depend on extractor emission order - exactly the class of bug the geometry path's sorting was written to avoid and DOES have a test for (test_matching_is_independent_of_the_order_of_the_component_ids). Fix: Add test_the_feature_tree_fingerprint_is_independent_of_the_row_order: `assert fingerprint(rms_package(features=list(reversed(base_features())), equations=list(reversed(base_equations()))), BOTH, 'feature_tree') == fingerprint(rms_package(), BOTH, 'feature_tree')`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/context.py:186** (exceptions-recording) - T017 says replace_coverage 'removes the session's itemS with that check in that bucket' (plural). The implementation's `items[:] = [existing for existing in items if existing.check != check]` correctly removes all of them, but every test only ever seeds ONE item with the target check, so a naive single-removal implementation (e.g. a first-match `items.pop(i)`) would pass. Duplicates are reachable in practice: check_rms_part appending before the tool was switched to replace_coverage, or a partial run. Fix: In test_replace_coverage_replaces_that_check_in_that_bucket_only, record 'rms.part.grouping' twice (reasons 'first pass' and 'stale duplicate') before the replace, and assert the bucket afterwards holds exactly two items - the untouched holes_last one and the single new grouping one.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/recording.py:103** (exceptions-recording) - record_result forwards six arguments positionally: `result_to_finding(context, result, component_ids, drawing_locations, exception_id, tool_result_ids)`. exception_id and tool_result_ids are now adjacent trailing optionals, and no test in test_recording.py passes both together - yet that combination is exactly what T022 needs for a waived RMS finding (checked_within_scope is a NUMERIC_STATUS, so it needs a step id, AND it needs the exception_id). Reordering those two params in result_to_finding would be caught only by pydantic type coercion, not by an intent-level assertion. Against the repo's 'explicit over clever' rule this is also the wrong shape for a 6-arg call. Fix: Pass both by keyword in record_result (`exception_id=exception_id, tool_result_ids=tool_result_ids`) and add test_record_result_carries_an_exception_id_and_a_step_id_together asserting finding.exception_id and finding.tool_result_ids on a checked_within_scope result.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_exceptions.py:269** (exceptions-recording) - DRY: the literal "interference.static" is now hardcoded in five places in test_exceptions.py (lines 269, 279, 287, 296, 307) and again as INTERFERENCE_CHECK in test_exceptions_feature_tree.py, while the implementation deliberately passes the module constant swreview.checks.interference.CHECK. The tests therefore no longer track the single source of truth the implementation uses - the same mistake test_tools_context.py's own docstring calls out ('imported rather than spelled out, so these assertions track the single source of truth'). Related nit: T016 asked for `check=interference.CHECK`; both call sites (checks/interference.py:286, tools/checks_interference.py:97-102) pass it positionally - behaviourally identical, but the keyword would make the fourth argument self-describing at the call site. Fix: Import `from swreview.checks.interference import CHECK as INTERFERENCE_CHECK` in both test modules and use it everywhere instead of the literal; optionally spell the argument as `check=CHECK` at the two call sites.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:199** (exceptions-recording) - _feature_row collapses two distinguishable states into one digest: `sketch=None` (this feature has no sketch at all) hashes identically to `SketchInfo(raw_status=None, consumer_ids=None)` (the extractor could not read the sketch - a gap), and `fillet=None` hashes identically to `FilletInfo(default_radius=None)`. Data-model section 3 only names sketch.raw_status / len(consumer_ids) / fillet.default_radius, so this is arguably within spec, but it sits against the brief's rule that unknown must stay distinguishable from absent. Neither state is tested, so the collapse is currently undocumented behaviour. Lowest priority of the seven - flag it, do not necessarily change it. Fix: Either add the presence flags (`sketch is not None`, `fillet is not None`) to the row, or - the cheaper option given type_name already changes when a feature stops being a sketch - add a test that pins the collapse as deliberate with a comment citing data-model section 3.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureTreeIndexer.cs:103** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `if (node == null) { continue; }` silently drops a feature from the tree: no row, no id, no gap, and the sub-features under that node vanish with it. Every downstream RMS rule then reads a tree with a hole and reports pass/skip instead of unresolved, which is exactly the silent-absence failure Principle I exists to prevent. It is also untested - no test in FeatureTreeIndexerTests.cs passes a null node - so the branch is unexercised in the 554-test run. Fix: Decide and be explicit. Either the input can never hold a null (it is built by the T024 dumper), in which case throw `new ArgumentException("A feature walk must not contain a null node.", nameof(features))` so a dumper bug is loud, or nulls are a real interop outcome, in which case the indexer must not be the place that swallows them: have the dumper record a `feature_tree_unavailable`-style gap before it ever reaches the indexer. Add the matching test either way.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/SuppressTestRun.cs:30** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `Outcome` defaults to `SuppressTestOutcome.Truncated`. Per contracts/rules.md's outcome table, `truncated` maps to *skip* - so a row that T055 forgets to fill in silently drops that Detail feature out of `rms.detail.individually_suppressible` rather than surfacing it. This is a default standing in for engineering data on the permissive side. Fix: Default to `SuppressTestOutcome.Aborted`, which the same table maps to *unresolved*: an unfilled row then shows up as "not proven" instead of "deliberately not tested". Update the XML doc to say why the default is the unresolved outcome.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/MateDumper.cs:195** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `FirstFlag` has four branches (bool, bool[], object[] of boxed bool, and the null fallback) and `ReadSuppressed` has three outcomes (read value, TryStep caught, null-answer gap). None of it is covered: the new PackageWriterTests case pins only PackageWriter's pass-through of a value the fake IMateSource fabricates, so MateDumper's VARIANT unwrapping and both of its gap paths run for the first time on a seat. T006 asks for `false` plus a `mate_suppression` gap on failure and nothing proves that happens. Fix: `FirstFlag` is already static and interop-free - make it `internal static` (with InternalsVisibleTo, which the test assembly likely already has) or lift it next to the other pure helpers, and add a small `MateDumperTests`/`FirstFlagTests` covering `true`, `new[]{true}`, `new object[]{false}`, `new bool[0]`, and `"nonsense"` -> null. If the gap paths cannot be reached without a seat, say so in the return note; the unwrapping itself has no excuse.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureTreeIndexer.cs:13** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `FeatureTreeNode` and `FeatureTreeRow` are dump contracts shaped exactly like `ComponentNode`/`ScopedComponent` (same `Handle` as `object?` with the same verbatim comment, same id-assigned-later pattern), but they live in the algorithm file instead of Dump/DumpContracts.cs, where every other dump contract type is declared (DumpOptions, ComponentNode, ComponentTreeResult, ScopedComponent, FaceRequest, HoleDumpResult, DumpScope and the eight I*Source interfaces). T024's FeatureDumper and its `IFeatureSource` will look for them in the contracts file; two homes for dump contracts is the start of the second mechanism the constraints forbid. Fix: Move `FeatureTreeNode` and `FeatureTreeRow` into Dump/DumpContracts.cs next to `ComponentNode`/`ScopedComponent` and leave FeatureTreeIndexer.cs holding only the static indexer. No behaviour change; the test file needs no edit.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/ComponentTreeDumper.cs:140** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `RootNode` sets every single ComponentNode member explicitly - including the ones whose C# default would already be right (`ParentKey = null`, `PatternId = null`, `IsToolbox = false`) - but the new `ConstrainedStatusRaw` is omitted and silently takes the `null` default. The root assembly genuinely has no constrained status, so `null` is the correct value, but the omission reads as an oversight rather than a decision and breaks the file's own explicit-over-clever convention. Fix: Add `ConstrainedStatusRaw = null,` after `IsToolbox = false,` with a one-line comment: the root assembly is not constrained by anything, so Python's table maps null to `unavailable` and the first-component rule never asks about it.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/MateDumper.cs:102** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `ReadSuppressed` records its `mate_suppression` gap against `mateId` before the mate is known to be complete. If any later step in `ReadMate` throws, the outer `scope.Gaps.TryStep("mate", ...)` at MateDumper.cs:85 swallows it and the mate is never added to `mates` - leaving a gap whose `entity_id` names a `mate:NNNN` that does not exist in the package. (The id hole in the allocator predates this change; the dangling entity_id does not.) Fix: Low cost, low frequency - reasonable to do nothing and note it. If you want it closed, read suppression into a local in `ReadMate` and only flush the gap once the IrMate has been appended to `mates`, or have the outer catch drop gaps whose entity_id is the abandoned mate id.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/PackageWriterTests.cs:71** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `Build_CarriesMateSuppressionThroughToThePackage` is tautological with respect to the change T006 made. PackageWriter.cs line 155 is `package.Mates.AddRange(_mates.Dump(scope))` - the fake IMateSource constructs a finished `IrMate` with `Suppressed = MateSuppressed` (PackageWriterTests.cs line 600) and the writer appends that same object. The test asserts a field the fake itself set on the object under assertion; no production line T006 touched is executed. Reverting MateDumper.cs to the previous hardcoded `Suppressed = false` leaves this test green. The task's "pinned through the same writer tests" is satisfied only in letter: unlike the component path, where the fake yields a `ComponentNode` the writer maps (so Build_CarriesTheComponentsRawConstrainedStatusThroughToThePackage at line 56 genuinely fails if `ConstrainedStatusRaw = node.ConstrainedStatusRaw` is deleted), the mate fake bypasses every mapping line.
