Fix: Keep the test as cheap regression cover for the AddRange path, but add a comment stating what it does and does not pin, and make the real coverage exist via the must_fix. Do not let this test stand as the evidence that the IsSuppressed2 read works.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/IrSerializerTests.cs:261** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `Serialize_WritesEnumsAsTheSchemaStrings` is the repo's single DRY home for pinning enum wire strings and was not extended for the new `SuppressTestOutcome`. The sample package only ever serializes `Ok` and `Aborted`, so only "ok" and "aborted" are validated against the schema's enum. `RebuildErrors`, `AlreadySuppressed`, `NotApplied` and `Truncated` have no test producing their wire form. This is a C#-writes / Python-reads boundary (the Python T005 task explicitly covers "all six outcomes"), so a naming mismatch on those four would surface only on a live seat. I hand-checked SnakeCaseLowerNamingPolicy and all six are correct today, so this is a coverage gap rather than a live bug - but nothing stops a future rename from breaking it silently. Fix: Add the four missing SuppressTestOutcome values to Serialize_WritesEnumsAsTheSchemaStrings alongside the existing enum assertions, asserting the exact strings "rebuild_errors", "already_suppressed", "not_applied", "truncated".
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureTreeIndexer.cs:103** (csharp-ir-indexer) - `if (node == null) { continue; }` inside `Visit` is defensive handling for a scenario that cannot occur - FeatureTreeNode lists are built by the indexer's own caller and by `SubFeatures`, a private-set List<FeatureTreeNode> - and it is the only branch in the file with no test. It also fails silently rather than loudly: a null would be dropped from the tree with no gap and no exception, the opposite of the "unknown stays unknown, absence is a Gap" discipline this feature follows everywhere else. The two ArgumentNullException guards on `Index` (lines 78, 83) are likewise untested, though those are defensible as public-API guards. Fix: Delete the `node == null` branch - the loop variable cannot be null from any caller, and a null-bearing sequence should throw rather than quietly shrink the tree. If you prefer to keep a guard, make it `throw new ArgumentException` and add a test for it; either way, no untested silent skip.
- **specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/ir.schema.json:1** (csharp-ir-indexer) - The `--write` regeneration rewrote the entire feature-001 contract file, not just the 1.1.0 additions: previously-inline $defs are hoisted (Design, ExtractorInfo, Manifest, MateEntity, PlaneFace, CylinderFace, BBox3D, SheetView, InterferenceSettings added; PersistRef and Transform removed), `title` annotations are added throughout, and the annotative "default": [] is dropped from mates/holes/interferences/captures/drawings. I verified this is validation-neutral - every shared $def keeps identical `required` and `additionalProperties: false`, top-level `required` and `additionalProperties: false` are unchanged, and the only new property on an existing def is the optional ComponentInstance.constrained_status_raw - so no 1.0.0 package stops validating. But the sole negative test guarding the contract, SchemaValidation_RejectsAPackageThatBreaksTheContract (IrSerializerTests.cs line 36), only checks one id pattern, so a regeneration that HAD loosened `required` or `additionalProperties` would not have been caught by the suite. Fix: No schema change needed - it is faithful. Strengthen the guard instead: extend SchemaValidation_RejectsAPackageThatBreaksTheContract with one case that removes a required field and one that adds an unknown property, so future regenerations cannot quietly loosen the contract. Separately, confirm with the owner that an annotation-only rewrite of the 001 contract is acceptable, since it is broader than "the IR bump adds optional members with defaults only" literally implies.

## Phase 3 (2026-09-16)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:226** (part-rules) - `_verdict` emits a zero-subject `pass` whenever a rule had neither a violation nor an unresolved subject ("a rule with nothing to look at at all passes vacuously"). Data-model section 2 aggregates `pass` into the `checked` bucket, so a rule that compared nothing is advertised to the engineer as checked. For the sketch rules on a part with no sketches this is defensible (the contract lists no skip), but for `rms.refs.direction` on an ungrouped part, and for `rms.groups.no_solids_in_ref_or_construction` when the group holds only non-content rows, it reports coverage that was never obtained - constitution Principle I, "a healthy feature tree is NOT an engineering acceptance result". Fix: Keep the vacuous pass only where the contract leaves no other bucket, and make the rules that exclude subjects (refs.direction, no_solids) return `skipped(...)` with an explicit reason (e.g. `no grouped content feature`) instead of a bare pass when their comparable set is empty; T021/T022 then record it as skipped coverage rather than checked.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/results.py:188** (part-rules) - `finding()` reads `row.suppressed` as a boolean, so `suppressed is None` - which `data-model.md` section 1 defines as "unreadable" - is silently treated as "not suppressed": the finding carries neither the `suppressed in <configuration>` marker nor any note that the suppression state could not be read. That is a default for engineering data on the evidence path (constitution Principle I: a missing input must stay unknown). Fix: Split the three states: `row.suppressed is True` keeps the current marker; `row.suppressed is None` adds a coverage limit such as `<id> <name>: suppression unreadable` so the unknown is visible in the finding. Add a case to `TestEvaluatePart.test_a_suppressed_feature_is_evaluated_as_present_and_marked` for `suppressed=None`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:1004** (part-rules) - `individually_suppressible` raises `NotImplementedError` when the package carries a suppress-test run for this document. Because `evaluate_part` dispatches every evaluable part rule, that raise propagates out of `evaluate_part`, and therefore out of `check_rms_part` (T028) and `swreview check rms` (T029), for any package produced after the T055 `suppress-test` command appended a run - a window that stays open until T057. A whole-review crash is a harsher failure mode than the unresolved item the constitution asks for, and the test at test_rms_part_rules.py:1295 pins the crash rather than a report. Fix: Until T057 lands, return `unresolved(rule, tree.document_id, "suppress-test run present; grading not implemented", rows)` instead of raising, so the review still completes and the gap is visible as unresolved coverage. T057 then replaces the branch wholesale.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:966** (part-rules) - The observed text of `rms.sketches.one_sketch_per_feature` counts raw ids (`len(row.sketch.consumer_ids)`) but names only the consumers `_consumers()` could resolve to rows of this document, so a consumer id that is not an indexed row yields evidence like "consumed by 2 features: Boss1". A finding whose count and name list disagree is hard to reproduce (constitution Principle VI). Fix: Either name the count of resolved consumers, or append the unresolved consumer ids verbatim (e.g. `... : Boss1, feat:0042 (not in this document's rows)`).
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:336** (part-rules) - `folders_ordered` recomputes the group ordering from `groups_seen` although `GroupAssignment.order_ok` (groups.py:125) already answers exactly that question and, after this change, nothing reads it. Two implementations of one predicate, with slightly different definitions (`order_ok` is strictly-increasing-pairwise; here it is every out-of-order pair), is the DRY violation the constitution calls out, and the dead field invites the two to drift. Fix: Use `tree.assignment.order_ok` as the verdict and keep the pair scan only to name the offending folders (`assert not offenders or not tree.assignment.order_ok or tree.assignment.duplicates`), or drop `order_ok` from `GroupAssignment` if the rule is its only consumer - whichever the T009/T010 owner prefers, but not both.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/results.py:188** (part-rules) - `finding()` decides suppression with `if row.suppressed`, so `suppressed=None` — which the IR defines as "unreadable" (ir/models.py: `suppressed: bool | None`, "null when unreadable") — is treated exactly like `False`: no `suppressed in <configuration>` marker, no coverage limit, no trace that the answer was unknown. That is a default written for engineering data, the one thing the feature constraint rules out, and it is inside the module whose docstring promises "no rule can name a suppressed feature and stay silent about it". No test exercises `suppressed=None`. Fix: Split the three states: `is True` → the existing marker and limit; `is None` → a `coverage_limits` entry such as `<id> <name>: suppression unreadable`; `is False` → nothing. Add a test alongside test_rms_part_rules.py:1366 asserting the null case carries the limit.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:812** (part-rules) - The sketch carve-out in `rms.detail.no_internal_references` tests `len(row.child_ids or ()) == 1`, but the contract says "a sketch consumed by exactly one feature", i.e. `row.sketch.consumer_ids`. The two are equal today only by accident of the extractor (FeatureDumper.cs:453 sets `ConsumerIds = childIds`) and of the fixture builder (tests/support/features.py:188 defaults `child_names` to `consumers`), so no test can tell the two readings apart; I had to override `child_names` by hand to see the difference. It also counts non-content children (folders, end tags), whereas `_dependents` filters them out, so the exemption and the comparison read the same field through two different filters. Fix: Read the field the contract names: `row.sketch is not None and len(row.sketch.consumer_ids or ()) == 1`, and add one test with a sketch whose `consumer_ids` and `child_ids` differ, so the choice is pinned rather than accidental.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:1063** (part-rules) - `_tree_not_read` invents a second mechanism and a second wording for "this document has no component instance": it returns `no component instance for <doc>; tree not read`, while report.py:129 already converts that exact condition into an unresolved coverage item reading `no component instance for <doc>` (data-model.md:126-130 assigns the condition to the report layer). Two phrasings for one condition is the "never add a second mechanism" case, and the branch has no test — I had to call `evaluate_part` with an empty `instances=[]` to reach it. Fix: Either drop the branch (return `None` and let report.py own the no-instance case — the 18 rules then evaluate an empty tree and pass vacuously, which report.py converts anyway), or keep it and add a test plus a comment saying why the wording differs from report.py's. My recommendation is to drop it: one condition, one owner.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/results.py:106** (part-rules) - `RuleResult.__post_init__` carries four invariant guards (result-without-outcome, outcome-without-result, missing reason, reason where none is allowed, mismatched `CheckResult.check`) and `finding()` carries a coverage-only guard, and not one of them is exercised by any test: no `pytest.raises` touches this module anywhere in reviewer/tests. Since T019 owns the part rules and T021 owns the report layer, no task currently owns results.py's own contract, so the module that exists precisely to enforce the outcome invariant is the untested one. Given the "rather too many tests than too few" standard this is the largest coverage hole in the change. Fix: Add a short `tests/unit/test_rms_results.py` (or a class in the part-rules file) asserting each guard raises with its message, plus `severity_of` for one warn / one medium / both high rules and `subject_input`'s exact format string.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:1004** (part-rules) - `individually_suppressible` raises `NotImplementedError` when the package does carry a run for this document. Until T057 lands, any package with an `rms_suppress_test` for a graded part will crash `evaluate_part`, and therefore `check_rms_part` / `swreview check rms` — an uncaught exception out of a check tool rather than a degraded result. The test at tests/unit/test_rms_part_rules.py:1295 pins the crash as intended behaviour, so the risk is deliberate, but it is a live landmine for whoever runs US4's suppress-plan before T057. Fix: Return `unresolved(rule, doc, "suppress-test outcome table not implemented", rows)` instead of raising, and keep the test asserting that reason (T057 then replaces the reason with the real table). If you prefer the loud failure, at least confirm no US4 path can produce a run before T057 merges.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:493** (part-rules) - Two smaller gaps in `holes_last`. (a) The no-hole-plus-unclassified branch (lines 493-503) hand-rolls the `unresolved(...)` construction that `_verdict` already owns — the one duplication of that shape in the module — and it is untested: I had to run a Detail group of only `Frobnicate`+`ICE` to see it emit `skip` + `unresolved` together. (b) On the pass path the subjects are `block` (the graded rows from the first hole onward), so Detail features examined *before* the first hole are named in no result at all; the test at line 366 asserts exactly `["Hole1", "Hole2"]` and so pins the narrower set without flagging it. Fix: For (a) route the branch through a small helper (or call `_verdict` with `violation=None, passing=(), unknown=unknown` after emitting the skip) and add the Detail-with-no-hole-but-unclassified test. For (b) decide whether a `checked` result should name every graded Detail feature or only the trailing block, and assert that choice.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/part.py:333** (part-rules) - `folders_ordered` skips on `len(seen) < 2`, where `groups_seen` counts group-folder *occurrences*, not distinct groups. A part with a single group folder duplicated (e.g. `3-Core` twice) therefore does not skip — I verified it reports `fail` naming both occurrences — although the contract's skip condition reads "fewer than two groups". I think the current behaviour is the right one (a duplicate must be reported, and skipping would hide it), but it is an unrecorded reading of the contract and nothing tests it: the existing duplicate test at line 190 uses two occurrences of one group only in combination with content, and no test asserts the one-group-duplicated shape skips-or-fails deliberately. Fix: Add a test for `[folder(CORE), folder(CORE)]` asserting `fail` with the duplicate observed text, and one comment line in the rule saying that occurrences, not distinct names, decide the skip — so a future reader does not "fix" it toward the literal contract wording.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:873** (report-tools-cli-golden) - DRY / drift: `check interference` gets its whole payload from the golden adapter (`golden_interference.interference_case`, cli.py:754), so what the CLI prints and what the 001 baseline pins are literally the same code path. `check rms` does not: it re-implements the package+exceptions wiring (`_context_for(..., exceptions=True)`, cli.py:575) and re-implements coverage flattening in `_coverage_rows`/`_coverage_counts` (cli.py:873-888), which is a copy of `checks/golden_rms.py:_coverage` (cli.py derives the bucket list from `get_args(CoverageBucket)`, golden_rms.py:38 hard-codes the same five names). Two mechanisms for the same answer, one of them a hand-maintained bucket list, and the CLI can now drift from the baseline that is supposed to pin it. Fix: Have `check_rms_command` call `golden_rms.part_case(package)` for `--scope part` (it already accepts a directory and reads `exceptions.json` from it) and delete `_context_for(..., exceptions=True)`, `_coverage_rows` and `_coverage_counts`; move the one shared bucket tuple into `report/session.py` (or derive it from `get_args(CoverageBucket)` in golden_rms too). If `--document` filtering makes `part_case` unsuitable, give `part_case` the optional `document_ids` argument rather than keeping a second path.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/rms_query.py:155** (report-tools-cli-golden) - `list_features(document_id, folder=<group name>)` returns `[]` when that group does not exist in that document: `folder in table.groups` succeeds against the table, then the comprehension at line 169 matches nothing. The module's own docstring (line 32) states the opposite rule - "an argument that names nothing in the package comes back as an error result, so the model sees the mistake as a tool result instead of as an empty list it reads as 'nothing there'" - and the tests cover a non-group name (`7-Nowhere`), a non-folder id and a foreign folder id but not this case. A model asking "what is in 6-Quarantine?" on a part that has no Quarantine folder is told the group is empty, which is the answer `rms.quarantine.*` skips on, not the answer the tree gives. Fix: In `_in_folder`, when `folder` is a group name, return `None` (error result) unless the group is in `assignment.groups_seen`, with a message that distinguishes the two states, e.g. `document 'doc:3' has no '6-Quarantine' group folder; its groups are ['3-Core', '4-Detail']`. Add a test for it next to `test_list_features_refuses_a_folder_that_names_nothing`.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:415** (report-tools-cli-golden) - `_write_summary` reaches into the session's coverage lists directly (`items[:] = [...]`, line 418) to pull the `modeling.resilience` item out of whichever of `checked`/`unresolved` it was in before. That is a second write path into `session.coverage` outside `ToolContext.record_coverage`/`replace_coverage`, and unlike `replace_coverage` it is done from the report layer, so nothing is emitted on the run's event stream: the pane sees the summary appear in `checked` and is never told it left `unresolved` (FR-013's whole point is that the session and the event stream cannot drift). The comment at line 66-68 acknowledges `replace_coverage` cannot express the move but solves it by bypassing the context rather than extending it. Fix: Add the cross-bucket case to the context - e.g. `ToolContext.replace_coverage(check, bucket, item, drop_from=other_buckets)` or a `move_coverage(check, bucket, item)` that removes the check from every bucket and records it in one - and call that from `_write_summary`. Keeps one path into the session and one event per change.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:397** (report-tools-cli-golden) - The `modeling.resilience` summary counts only items whose check is a dispatched rule (`RULES.get(item.check)` with `rule.coverage is None`). Excluding the four data-gap rules is defensible and documented; silently excluding `rms.types.unknown` is not. A package whose tree carries type names the calibrated table does not know, none of which any class-dependent rule happens to read (an unknown type sitting in Core, say), ends the review with `rms.types.unknown` in `unresolved` and `modeling.resilience` in `checked` - the summary line the engineer reads says the method was graded while the grader could not name part of the tree. Fix: Count `UNKNOWN_TYPES_CHECK` toward the summary's unresolved bucket (it is already written through `replace_coverage`, so it is in the session when `_write_summary` runs), and name it in the reason, e.g. `... ; 2 unknown type name(s)`. Add a test for the case where an unknown type produces no unresolved rule result.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:86** (report-tools-cli-golden) - `report_results` returns the first finding-build error immediately, after earlier findings have already been appended to the session and before any coverage, census, data-gap or summary item is written. The session is then left with N findings, no coverage for any rule, and no `modeling.resilience` item - a half-written review that reads like a run that found violations and graded nothing. It is reported (not silent), but the partial state survives in `session.json`. Fix: Either write coverage before bailing out (compute and write the aggregated items, then return the error), or collect the per-result errors and report them all in the returned envelope while still writing the coverage for everything that did report. Recommend the first: it is two lines and it keeps the invariant that every call either writes both halves or neither. Add a test with a result whose `CheckResult` cannot build a finding.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:213** (report-tools-cli-golden) - DRY: `_waived` (213) and `_needs_review` (236) duplicate `checks/interference.py:_excepted` (314) and `_needs_review` (336) almost sentence for sentence - same status change, same 'accepted by X on Y for this <geometry|feature tree> and configuration' observed text, same 'Re-review <id>' recommended action. The only real differences are the noun and the coverage-limit format (`exception:<id>: <note>` here, `exception:<id>` there, per data-model section 2). Two copies of "how an accepted exception rewrites a CheckResult" will drift the next time the exception wording changes. Fix: Extract one helper - e.g. `checks/exception_text.py: apply_exception(result, exception, *, subject_noun, limit_note: bool)` - and call it from both. Low risk: both call sites are covered by tests and by a golden baseline (bracket-assy-interference, rms-part), so a wording regression would show up as a diff.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:830** (report-tools-cli-golden) - Two smaller gaps in `check rms`. (1) For `--scope assembly|equations` the command validates `--document` through `part_documents` and then throws the result away (`documents = []`, line 830), so `check rms --scope assembly --document doc:2` silently reports `0 part document(s)`; `test_check_rms_scope_assembly_reports_that_it_is_not_in_this_build` bakes that in. (2) There is no test for a corrupt `exceptions.json` on this command, although `_context_for(..., exceptions=True)` newly reads one and `check interference` has exactly that test (`test_check_interference_with_a_corrupt_exceptions_file_exits_1`) - the path where a waiver file that cannot be parsed must exit 1 rather than grading as if nothing were accepted. Fix: (1) Either reject `--document` with a scope that does not use it, or keep reporting the documents the run would have graded; do not validate then discard. (2) Add the corrupt-exceptions test for `check rms` mirroring the interference one, and one asserting the file is left byte-for-byte after a run that has an exception file with real records (the existing byte-for-byte test uses an empty file, so `refresh` never rewrites anything).
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/rms_query.py:155** (report-tools-cli-golden) - `_in_folder` accepts any name in `table.groups`, so `list_features(document_id, folder="6-Quarantine")` on a document that has no Quarantine folder returns `[]` (verified). The module docstring, lines 20-26, states the opposite rule for this tool: "an argument that names nothing in the package comes back as an error result, so the model sees the mistake as a tool result instead of as an empty list it reads as 'nothing there'". The existing negative tests only cover a name that is not a group at all (`7-Nowhere`), a non-folder feature id, and another document's folder id, so the case the docstring is about is the one case not covered. Fix: Recommended: when `folder` is a group name that `assignment.groups_seen` does not contain for this document, return `error_result(f"document {document_id!r} has no {folder} group")`, and add the test beside `test_list_features_refuses_a_folder_that_names_nothing`. Alternative (do nothing): defensible only if an empty list is deliberately the answer for "the group exists in the method but not in this tree" - if so, say that in the docstring and pin it with a test, because right now code and docstring disagree.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/agent/prompts/system_v1.md:30** (report-tools-cli-golden) - The new step 4 instructs the model to run `check_rms_assembly` and `check_rms_equations`. Neither is registered in this build: `tools/registry.py::check_tools()` adds only `rms_checks.check_rms_part` (they arrive in T035 and T042). A review run on this build will emit two unknown-tool errors and two `tool.*` entries in the `failed` coverage bucket. Nothing guards prompt-vs-registry agreement - no test references `prompts/system_v1.md` at all. Fix: Option A (recommended): word step 4 for what exists now (`check_rms_part`, plus one sentence that the assembly and equation rules arrive with their own tools) and re-expand it in T035/T042, which already touch this area. Option B (do nothing): acceptable only if no review run happens between now and T042 - it costs two failed tool calls per run until then. Either way, a test asserting every backticked `check_*`/`list_*` name in the prompt is a registered tool name would be cheap and would have caught this.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/golden_rms.py:57** (report-tools-cli-golden) - The directory branch of `part_case` is dead: nothing calls `part_case` with a path. `golden_interference.interference_case` has the same branch and the CLI reuses it (`cli.py` line 753), but `check_rms_command` re-implements the load/refresh sequence itself in `_context_for(..., exceptions=True)` (cli.py lines 575-587). So the repo now has two code paths that load a package, load `exceptions.json`, refresh it in memory and run the RMS part rules - the DRY violation the file's own docstring says it exists to prevent. Related: `tests/golden/fixtures/rms-part/exceptions.json` is a second copy of the exception the harness never reads (the harness passes `case.json` `kwargs.exceptions` inline), so the two copies can drift. Fix: Recommended: keep the CLI as it is (it needs per-document dispatch and scope reporting that `part_case` does not offer) and delete the unused directory branch from `part_case`, or keep the branch and drop `exceptions.json` from the fixture - not both. My preference is to delete the directory branch and the fixture's `exceptions.json`: one input, one path, nothing to drift. If you would rather mirror `interference_case` exactly for symmetry, then keep the branch and add the test that exercises it (`part_case(fixture_dir)` equals the inline result), which is what makes a branch real.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:318** (report-tools-cli-golden) - `_write_unknown_types` rebuilds the `rms.types.unknown` item from only the documents in the current call's results and writes it with `replace_coverage`, so a narrow second call erases a wider first call's census. After `check_rms_part()` reports `NoSuchType x2` for doc:4, `check_rms_part(document_id="doc:3")` on a package where doc:3 has a different unknown type replaces the item and doc:4's unknown types vanish from the report. Per-rule coverage does not have this problem because it is keyed per rule and only the re-evaluated rules are replaced. The early `return []` at line 318 means the erase happens only when the narrow call has its own unknowns, which makes it intermittent rather than absent. No test covers a narrow re-run's effect on the census. Fix: Recommended: merge rather than replace - read the existing `rms.types.unknown` item off the session, union its documents and counts with this call's, then `replace_coverage` the merged item; add a test for `check_rms_part()` followed by `check_rms_part(document_id=<other part>)`. Alternative (cheaper, also defensible): compute the census over every document in the package rather than over the call's scope, since it is a package-wide census and not a per-call answer. Do nothing is tenable only because the agent normally calls the tool once with no argument.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:418** (report-tools-cli-golden) - `_write_summary` drops the summary from the bucket it is leaving by mutating the session list in place (`items[:] = [...]`) instead of going through `replace_coverage`/`record_coverage`. `ToolContext.record_coverage`'s docstring (context.py) says the `record_*` methods are "the only places a finding, a coverage item or an evidence request reaches the session, so 'written to the session' and 'on the event stream' cannot drift apart"; this is a second write path, and a streaming pane sees the summary appear in `unresolved` while never being told it left `checked`. Fix: Recommended: add the one primitive this needs - e.g. `ToolContext.move_coverage(check, from_bucket, to_bucket, item)` or let `replace_coverage` take the buckets to clear - so the removal emits an event like every other coverage write. Low effort, keeps one write path. Alternative (do nothing): the only consumer that would notice is the live pane, and the session file ends up correct either way - but then say so in a comment at line 415, because the code currently reads as an accident.
- **reviewer/tests/golden/fixtures/rms-part/generate_package.py:193** (report-tools-cli-golden) - The `widget()` docstring - which is the hand-check artifact T030 asks for - says `Boss-Extrude1`'s null `child_ids` is "for the three reference rules". The baseline shows it reaches only one: `rms.refs.direction` is unresolved for doc:4, while `rms.refs.quarantine_has_no_children` and `rms.detail.no_internal_references` are `checked` for doc:4, because those two only report a null `child_ids` on a feature that is in Quarantine or in Detail, and `Boss-Extrude1` is in Core (contracts/rules.md, unresolved conditions for those two rows). The fixture is right; its stated intent is not. Fix: Recommended: correct the sentence (name `rms.refs.direction` alone), or - better, and what I would choose - move the null-child feature into Detail as well as keeping one in Core so the fixture actually seeds all three unresolved conditions and the golden covers `rms.detail.no_internal_references`'s unresolved branch, which nothing in the baseline currently does. Regenerating the baseline is one command and this is the moment to do it, before more tasks pin it.
- **reviewer/tests/golden/test_golden/rms-part.yml:1** (report-tools-cli-golden) - The baseline pins an interpretation of contracts/rules.md that the contract does not obviously give: `part.py::_verdict` (line 224, "A violation replaces the pass rather than joining it") means a document that fails a rule for one subject never appears in that rule's `checked` item, even when most subjects comply. In this baseline doc:3 appears in no `checked` row at all, although e.g. `rms.sketches.fully_defined` has three compliant sketches there. rules.md's preamble says a rule "produces one RuleResult per outcome it reaches on that document" and that it "may therefore appear in more than one bucket (finding, checked, skipped, unresolved) for a single document" - which reads as allowing finding+checked together, and the implementation does emit checked+unresolved together (doc:4, `rms.detail.holes_last`). Fix: This is T020's decision, but the golden is what freezes it, so it should be settled before more baselines are generated. Recommended: keep the current behaviour (a document that fails a rule is not also 'checked' for it - that is the more useful report and the coverage counts stay readable) and write the interpretation down in part.py beside `_verdict` plus a line in the golden's generator docstring, so the next reader does not read the preamble and 'fix' it. Alternative: emit the pass alongside the violation and regenerate rms-part.yml - closer to a literal reading of the preamble, but it makes every failing document also 'checked', which reads badly in the coverage summary.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/SwFeatureReader.cs:218** (csharp-dumper-probe) - `Features()` turns anything that is not an `object[]` into an empty list, and drops non-IFeature items from an array that is one, with no gap either way. The comment says 'the dumper's id mapping is what notices a feature it cannot name', but the dumper only ever sees the filtered list, so both cases are invisible. This matters because `[]` is a pass-producing value: rules.md rows 37, 38, 39 and 43 treat a non-null `child_ids`/`consumer_ids` as an answer, and 43 says outright 'a sketch with no consumer passes'. research.md R5 open question 2 ('whether GetChildren and GetParents return values for ordinary features on 2024') is exactly the case where every feature would get `[]` and three fail rules would pass on every part. Fix: At minimum, record a `feature_children`/`feature_parents` gap when the answer is a non-empty array none of whose items is an IFeature (an answer that came back and could not be read is not 'no dependents'). Keep `null -> []` only if T062 confirms 2024 returns an empty VARIANT for a childless feature, and say so in the comment instead of the current claim that the dumper notices; make T062 sign-off on question 2 a gate for US1 rather than a later calibration.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/FeatureDumperTests.cs:588** (csharp-dumper-probe) - `Dump_FeedsTheTypeNameCensusNothing` asserts `Unconsumed()` is empty, which is weaker than the T023 sentence 'TypeNameCensus receives no pass from this dumper'. A pass with `Consumed: false` would fail the test (good), but a pass with `Consumed: true` also leaves `Unconsumed()` empty - and that is the more damaging mistake, because marking every walked type consumed would suppress the unsupported-type gap that HoleDumper's and MateDumper's passes exist to raise for the same document. Fix: Assert that no pass was recorded at all rather than that no name is unconsumed: seed the census with a pass of the document's own types marked unconsumed before calling Dump and assert those names are still reported afterwards, or expose a pass count / document count on TypeNameCensus for the test to assert is zero.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:462** (csharp-dumper-probe) - ReadFillet (line 462) and ReadSketch (line 424) raise `fillet_radius` and `sketch_status` gaps for features that have neither. When `GetDefinition` fails on a folder or an extrusion the gap says the radius is unknown for a feature that never had one, and the same for `GetSpecificFeature2` on a non-sketch. Those gaps inflate the unresolved coverage the report aggregates per document (data-model section 2, coverage aggregation) with subjects no rule grades. Fix: The sketch case is genuinely unknowable (a failed GetSpecificFeature2 means we do not know whether it is a sketch) and should stay. For the fillet case, the failure is only meaningful once the feature is known to be a fillet - consider raising the `fillet_radius` gap only for the classified Simple/Variable paths and recording a `feature`-level gap for a GetDefinition that failed outright, so the fillet rule's unresolved count means fillets.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:273** (csharp-dumper-probe) - The `feature_tree_configuration` gap is written from `other.Node.ReferencedConfiguration` (the configurations the document is actually instanced under), but the Python consumer deliberately does not parse the reason and instead names `document.configurations` minus the one the tree was read in (reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/report.py:180-186). For a document that has configurations it is not instanced under, the coverage limit 'other configurations not read: <names>' will name configurations no instance uses, and the two layers disagree about what the gap means. Fix: Pick one definition and state it in both places. Either C# writes the gap for every other configuration the document owns (matching what Python prints), or Python derives the names from the instances' referenced configurations. Cross-layer, so agree it with the owner of report.py before changing either side.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Console/Program.cs:655** (csharp-dumper-probe) - RunProbe's own parsing has no test. CommandLineOptionsTests covers `--features`, the probe's option list and `--allow-start`, but nothing exercises `probe` with no subject (UsageError), `probe --doc x` (subject that starts with `--`), or `probe notrms` (unknown subject), and nothing asserts the new usage lines. Constitution III asks for the invalid-input tests alongside the implementation, and these three branches are the ones a typo hits. Fix: Extract the subject check into an internal static helper on Program (as KnownOptions already is) and add three CommandLineOptionsTests cases for it, plus one asserting the usage text lists `probe rms`.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:415** (csharp-dumper-probe) - Every feature of every resolved part gets GetChildren, GetParents, Description, GetErrorCode2, IsSuppressed2, GetSpecificFeature2 and GetDefinition - seven out-of-process COM round trips each, GetChildren and GetParents being the slow ones. research.md R7 forbids filtering by type name (that is the whole point of the design), so there is no cheap filter available, but the constitution's technical constraint that out-of-process calls stay coarse makes this the phase most likely to dominate dump time on a real assembly. Fix: Do not change the design, but make T062 measure it: have `probe rms` print elapsed time for the walk and for the per-feature reads so the workstation run produces a number, and decide there whether the feature phase needs a per-document budget or a documented expectation in cli.md.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/FeatureDumperTests.cs:299** (csharp-dumper-probe) - T023 names "description null plus gap", and FeatureDumper.cs:368 has a dedicated branch for it - Description READ successfully and answered null - which writes the feature_description gap so that blank ("") and unknown (null) stay different answers. No test exercises it: FakeFeature.Description defaults to string.Empty and no test assigns null (only DescriptionThrows, which reaches the gap through TryStep instead). Deleting the whole `if (descriptionRead && description == null)` block leaves the suite green, and a null-returning interop property is the likelier real-world shape than a throwing one. Fix: Add a case that sets `boss.Description = null` with DescriptionThrows false and asserts row.Description is null, one gap with EntityKind feature_description and EntityId feat:0001, and GapKind.NotExtracted (not ToolError) so the two routes to the gap stay distinguishable.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:200** (csharp-dumper-probe) - The "active configuration could not be read -> drop the document's rows" branch in DumpDocument is untested. FakeDocument has a WalkFailure switch (used by Dump_WalkThatFailed_...) but no ActiveConfiguration failure switch, so neither the TryStep-false path nor the `configuration == null` path is covered; the comment says a tree labelled with the wrong configuration is worse than no tree, and nothing pins that decision. Fix: Add `ActiveConfigurationFailure`/null support to FakeDocument and a test asserting no rows for that document, a `feature` gap naming the document id, and that another document still dumps - mirroring Dump_WalkThatFailed_IsAGapAndTheOtherDocumentsAreStillRead.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:228** (csharp-dumper-probe) - FeatureTreeIndexer was changed (Phase 2 file) from skipping a null node to throwing ArgumentException, and its new comment says "the caller turns this into a gap rather than a quietly shorter tree". The caller does not: Index is called bare here, outside any TryStep, so the exception escapes Dump() to PackageWriter.RunPhase, which records one generic "Failed to read the part feature trees" gap with a null entity id and discards the rows of EVERY document, including the ones already collected. probe rms (Program.cs:718) calls Index bare too and simply exits 1. The claim in the comment is only true at the FeatureTreeIndexerTests level, where the test asserts the throw, not the recovery. Fix: Wrap the Index call in scope.Gaps.TryStep("feature", documentId, ...) and return Array.Empty<Feature>() on failure - same shape as the walk failure - plus a FeatureDumperTests case with a null node in the walk asserting one document is lost and the other still dumps. Otherwise correct the indexer comment to say the caller does not localise it.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:311** (csharp-dumper-probe) - When row.Node.Handle is null, ReadFeature returns null and the caller writes the gap reason "'<name>' in <file> has no persistent reference" - which is not what happened; the walk produced a node with no live feature. An engineer chasing that gap looks for a persistent-reference problem that does not exist. The path is also untested (Node() always sets Handle). Fix: Either distinguish the two causes (return a small reason from ReadFeature, or write the handle gap at the point of detection) or drop the handle null-check and let the PersistRef read fail naturally; add a test for whichever you choose.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/SuppressTestRun.cs:28** (csharp-dumper-probe) - SuppressTestRow.Outcome's default was changed from Truncated to Aborted, in a Phase 2 file that is not in T023/T024/T031's scope. data-model.md line 74 says the opposite for the contract's own meaning of a row nobody attempted: "One per planned feature, in plan order (len(rows) == features_present; untested ones are `truncated`)", and T054 requires --limit/--timeout-seconds rows to be `truncated` (a skip) rather than `aborted` (unresolved). The change is safe only while every row's outcome is set explicitly; T055 is the task that will construct those rows and is not written yet. Fix: Confirm the deviation with the user. If it stays, say in the XML comment that T055 must set Truncated explicitly for limit/timeout rows (the default is a last-resort, not the truncation mechanism), so the next agent does not inherit `aborted` for deliberately untested features.
- **reviewer/tests/golden/test_golden/cover-blind-tap.yml:46** (csharp-dumper-probe) - The feature 001 golden baseline is NOT byte-identical any more: +10 lines adding the `modeling.resilience` checklist item to the rendered checklist. The stated constraint for this feature is that feature 001 and 002 goldens stay byte-identical. The change comes from T028's checklist_v1.yaml edit (another agent, outside this group), not from T023/T024/T031, but nothing in the suite flags it as a constraint breach - the golden was simply regenerated. Fix: Reconcile explicitly before T061: either accept that adding a checklist item necessarily re-renders every feature-001 golden and record that decision, or keep the RMS checklist item out of the rendered baseline. Do not let it pass as an unremarked regeneration.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Console/Program.cs:738** (csharp-dumper-probe) - ProbeFeatures takes `configuration` from Describe(() => reader.ActiveConfiguration(...)), so when that read fails the variable holds the error text ("!InvalidOperationException: ...") and is then passed to reader.Suppressed(handle, configuration) as a configuration NAME. IsSuppressed2 with a name that does not exist answers false for every feature, and the probe prints suppressed=False for a whole tree - a calibration run reading that would record the wrong answer for R5 item 6. Fix: Keep the configuration as string? from a non-Describe read; when it is unavailable print `suppressed=(no configuration)` and skip the IsSuppressed2 call rather than asking with a bogus name.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/FeatureDumper.cs:420** (csharp-dumper-probe) - SketchInfo.ConsumerIds is assigned the SAME List<string> instance as Feature.ChildIds (ReadSketch(..., childIds) stores the reference). Serialisation is unaffected today, but any later code that mutates one list silently mutates the other, and the aliasing is invisible at the call site. Fix: Assign a copy: `ConsumerIds = childIds == null ? null : new List<string>(childIds)`, keeping the null-means-unavailable distinction the test pins.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:223** (carry-over-python) - `_feature_row` hashes `feature.child_ids` as a raw list, so the feature-tree digest is sensitive to the order `GetChildren` happened to return dependents in. The module docstring (line ~15) promises the opposite - "It is order-independent, so the order SOLIDWORKS happened to report components and faces in cannot flip an exception" - and the geometry path keeps that promise by sorting face tokens. Every rule that reads `child_ids` (`rms.refs.direction`, `rms.refs.quarantine_has_no_children`, `rms.detail.no_internal_references`) treats it as a set, and SOLIDWORKS promises no ordering from `GetChildren` (research.md R5 leaves it to be confirmed on the workstation). A rebuild that reorders the children re-opens every RMS exception on that document as `needs_review` although nothing a rule reads has changed - churn that pushes engineers to stop trusting the mechanism. Fix: Hash `None if feature.child_ids is None else sorted(feature.child_ids)` (keeping null distinct from empty), and add a test alongside `test_the_feature_tree_fingerprint_is_independent_of_row_order` that reverses one row's `child_ids` and asserts the digest is unchanged.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:265** (carry-over-python) - The new `REQUIRED_KEYS`/`_require` guard covers only top-level keys, but `_parse` still indexes the nested assembly keys directly: `assembly["reference_entity_kinds"]`, `assembly["geometry_entity_kinds"]`, `assembly["mate_chain_depth_limit"]`. A table whose `assembly:` block is missing a key raises a bare `KeyError('reference_entity_kinds')` with no file path, and one whose `assembly:` is a list raises `TypeError`, which is exactly the failure mode the `_require` docstring says was being removed ("refused by name rather than surfacing a bare KeyError from wherever it happened to be read"). `document["version"]` has the same hole: a non-numeric version surfaces `invalid literal for int()` with no path. The parametrized test only walks `REQUIRED_KEYS`, so the gap is invisible. Fix: Either extend the guard to the nested block (an `isinstance(assembly, dict)` check plus a `_require`-style loop over the three assembly keys, with `ASSEMBLY_REQUIRED_KEYS` parametrized in the test the same way), or narrow the `REQUIRED_KEYS` docstring to say it covers the top level only and state why the nested block is left bare. The first is the honest option given the rest of this loader's error handling.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/registry.py:59** (carry-over-python) - A module-level `assert` is used to enforce a production invariant (`RuleCoverageBucket` is a subset of `report.session.CoverageBucket`). Python strips asserts under `-O`, so the guard is not reliable; and it is already asserted twice in `test_rms_registry.py::TestCoverageBuckets`, so it is a third copy of the same fact (DRY: "never add a second mechanism for something the repo already has"). Its only effect at runtime is to pull `swreview.report.session` into the rule catalogue module, coupling the one list of rules to the report layer for no behavioural reason. Fix: Drop the `assert` and the `from swreview.report.session import CoverageBucket` import (and the now-unused `get_args`), leaving the two existing tests as the guard; or, if the coupling is wanted, make it a real `raise ValueError` at import so `-O` cannot remove it. Prefer the first - the tests already run on every change.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/recording.py:52** (carry-over-python) - The carry-over fix passes `exception_id` and `tool_result_ids` by keyword at the `record_result` call site, and the new test's docstring states the guarantee as fact - "The two optional arguments are passed by keyword, so an added parameter cannot silently slide one into the other's place" - but the signatures of `result_to_finding` (line 52), `record_result` (line 97) and `record_results` still accept them positionally. The convention is enforced nowhere; the next caller (T022's `checks/rms/report.py` is already one) can still pass them positionally and the same latent swap returns. All six current call sites already use keywords, so the enforcement is free. Fix: Insert a bare `*` before `exception_id` in `result_to_finding` and `record_result` and before `tool_result_ids` in `record_results`, making the optional provenance arguments keyword-only. No call site changes.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_features.py:490** (carry-over-python) - `test_the_fixture_builder_emits_only_documented_gap_entity_kinds` does not do what its docstring claims. The docstring says it "anchors `NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS` to something that actually writes a gap" because "the parametrized test above would pass for any tuple at all", but the assertion is `emitted <= set(NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS)` over a package that emits only two kinds (`feature_tree_unavailable`, `mate_suppression`). An invented eleventh entry in the tuple, or a documented kind no producer ever writes, still passes; only `assert emitted` (non-empty) and the subset direction are actually checked. The claim in the docstring is stronger than the test, which is the kind of assertion that gets trusted later. Fix: Assert the exact set for that package (`assert emitted == {"feature_tree_unavailable", "mate_suppression"}`), or build the union over builder scenarios that exercise each documented kind and assert equality with `set(NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS)`. Failing that, reword the docstring to claim only what is checked.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:551** (carry-over-python) - The two new `SuppressTestRun` invariants (`len(rows) == features_present`, and `unrestored_feature_ids` empty when `restore_verified`) plus `limit`/`timeout_seconds` `ge=1` are enforced only on the Python side, where a violation is a `ValidationError` raised while loading `package.json`. `rms_suppress_test` is an optional, audit-only member, but a bad run makes the whole package unloadable - every check dies, not just `rms.detail.individually_suppressible`. The C# producer carries none of these: `extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/SuppressTestRun.cs` defaults `Limit` and `TimeoutSeconds` to `0` (which Python now rejects) and has no row/count guard, so `AppendSuppressTest` can write a file the reviewer refuses to open. Fix: Mirror the invariants at write time in T055's `PackageAppender.AppendSuppressTest` (throw naming the mismatch, with `PackageAppenderTests` cases for a short row list, a non-zero `unrestored_feature_ids` with `restore_verified`, and a zero limit/timeout), so a malformed run is refused where it is produced rather than bricking the package for every other check.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:218** (carry-over-python) - `_feature_row` hashes two fields beyond the list data-model.md section 3 enumerates: `sketch is not None` (line 218) and `fillet is not None` (line 221). The reasoning in the docstring is sound (absent sketch vs unreadable sketch are different facts and drive different rule outcomes), and T015's own test pins it - but section 3 spells the hashed fields out one by one and has not been updated, so the implementation and the normative contract now disagree. specs/003 is off-limits to the implementer, which is exactly why the deviation has to be surfaced rather than left in a docstring. Fix: Report the deviation explicitly to the feature owner so section 3's field list gains the two presence flags (or the flags are removed if the owner disagrees). Until then, add a one-line comment at the call site pointing at the open contract question rather than leaving the divergence implicit.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:419** (carry-over-python) - `ExceptionStore.accept` binds any check whose id starts with `rms.` to a feature-tree fingerprint without checking that the rule is `fail`-severity. `contracts/rules.md` ("Waivable rules") is normative: "Only `fail`-severity rules are acceptable as exceptions; a waiver naming a `warn`, data-gap, or out-of-scope rule is reported invalid and changes nothing", and data-model section 3 repeats it. Nothing in the module, and no test in `test_exceptions_feature_tree.py`, prevents a `warn` rule (`rms.folders.present`) or a coverage-only one (`rms.advisory.avoid_multibody`) from being accepted today. Fix: Confirm the guard lands in T045's `exceptions accept-rms` (which owns the four per-id statuses including `invalid (warn rule)`), and add a test there that a `warn` and a coverage-only rule id are both refused. If the store is ever reachable by another path, the cheapest durable guard is for `accept` to consult `RULES[check].severity` for `rms.*` ids and refuse a non-`fail` rule outright.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms_types.py:264** (carry-over-python) - `REQUIRED_KEYS`/`_require` was added so a malformed table is "refused by name rather than surfacing a bare KeyError", but the guard only covers top-level keys. Two lines later `assembly["reference_entity_kinds"]`, `assembly["geometry_entity_kinds"]` and `assembly["mate_chain_depth_limit"]` are indexed unguarded, and `assembly` is never checked to be a mapping: a table with `assembly: {}` still raises a bare `KeyError`, and `assembly: []` raises `TypeError: list indices must be integers`. `test_a_table_missing_a_required_key_is_refused` is parametrized over top-level keys only, so nothing covers this. Fix: Check the three assembly sub-keys the same way (an `_require`-style call against the `assembly` mapping, plus an `isinstance(assembly, dict)` refusal with the same `f"{path}: ..."` shape), and add the matching parametrized test beside `test_a_table_missing_a_required_key_is_refused`. Same mechanism, no second one - it is ~6 lines and closes the only remaining raw-exception path in the loader.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/registry.py:59** (carry-over-python) - A module-level `assert` enforces the `RuleCoverageBucket <= CoverageBucket` invariant in library code. `assert` is stripped under `python -O`, so the invariant it claims to enforce is not enforced in an optimized interpreter; and when it does fire it raises at import time, which surfaces as a pytest collection error rather than a named test failure. It is also duplicated verbatim by `tests/unit/test_rms_registry.py::TestCoverageBuckets::test_the_rule_buckets_are_session_buckets`, which means the same assertion is now maintained in two places - a DRY violation in exactly the direction the project instructions call out. Fix: Drop the module-level assert and keep the test, which already covers it and fails cleanly by name. If a runtime guarantee is genuinely wanted, raise explicitly (`if not set(...) <= set(...): raise ValueError(...)`) rather than asserting - the rest of this package refuses bad state with `ValueError` and a message naming the thing, and an `assert` is the one place that convention is broken.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_features.py:490** (carry-over-python) - `test_the_fixture_builder_emits_only_documented_gap_entity_kinds` does not do what its docstring claims. The docstring says "a kind it emits and data-model.md section 1 does not document fails here", but `NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS` (line 48) is a hand-written literal in the test file, not parsed from data-model.md - so the test is anchored to the test's own list, exactly the weakness it says it is fixing. The assertion is also a subset check (`emitted <= set(NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS)`) over a fixture that emits only 2 of the 10 documented kinds, so 8 kinds remain pinned by nothing but `test_the_new_gap_entity_kinds_validate`, which the docstring itself correctly describes as vacuous (`Gap.entity_kind` is a free string). Fix: Parse the ten kinds out of data-model.md section 1 the way `test_rms_registry.py` already parses `contracts/rules.md` (`tests.support.contracts.REPO_ROOT` plus a small regex), assert the parsed set equals `NEW_GAP_ENTITY_KINDS`, and make the builder test assert set *equality* against the kinds that fixture is built to produce (`{"feature_tree_unavailable", "mate_suppression"}`) rather than a subset. That makes a doc/code drift in either direction fail, and stops the builder silently dropping a gap.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:250** (carry-over-python) - The `description="Row-major 4x4 relative to the root assembly, translation in meters"` added to `ComponentInstance.transform` (and regenerated into `specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/ir.schema.json` line 209) is feature 001 surface with no bearing on feature 003 - no RMS rule, dumper or tool reads `transform`. Its test, `test_component_transform_documents_the_frame_and_the_unit` (test_ir_features.py:453), asserts the exact wording, so it is a pure change detector: any reword breaks it with no semantic change, and it cannot fail for any reason a reader would care about. Fix: Either revert both the field description and its test (surgical-change rule: every changed line should trace to feature 003), or - if documenting the frame is deliberate and wanted - keep the description and delete the test, since the schema-sync test (`test_schema_sync`) already guarantees the regenerated contract matches the model. Do not keep both.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/exceptions.py:218** (carry-over-python) - `_feature_row` now hashes two fields data-model.md section 3 does not list: `sketch is not None` (line 218) and `fillet is not None` (line 221). The behaviour is right - I confirmed `sketch_lost` and `SketchInfo(raw_status=None, consumer_ids=None)` collided without the flag, and both new tests go red if the flags are removed - but the contract enumerates the hashed fields exactly ("index, type_name, name, depth, folder_id, suppressed, description present or not, sketch.raw_status, len(sketch.consumer_ids), fillet.default_radius, child_ids"), and the code now exceeds that list with no contract change and no note. Fix: Since specs/003 is off-limits to the implementing agent, flag it for the spec owner: data-model.md section 3's field list needs "whether a sketch/fillet block is present at all" added, or the flags need removing. Leaving the code ahead of the contract is the one thing not to do - the next reader reconciling the two will not know which is authoritative. Worth recording alongside it that the digest shape is now load-bearing for `tests/golden/fixtures/rms-part/case.json`'s stored hash (I verified it still recomputes to 5bdb638b…), so any further row-shape change must regenerate that fixture.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_exceptions_feature_tree.py:165** (carry-over-python) - This file defines local `feature(index, name, type_name, ...) -> Feature` (line 75) and `rms_package(features=..., equations=...) -> EvidencePackage` (line 165) that shadow the shared T003 builders of the same names in `tests/support/features.py` (`feature(name, type_name, **kwargs) -> FeatureSpec` at line 172, `rms_package(parts=..., assembly=...)` at line 415) with different signatures and different return types. `test_ir_features.py` now imports the shared `rms_package` while its neighbour defines a different one - two functions, one name, one test suite, one feature. Fix: Rename the local helpers to something that cannot be confused with the shared builders (`tree_package(...)`/`row(...)`, say). Do not try to merge them: the local one is legitimately different - it wraps `build_package` so the geometry digests pinned at lines 59 and 66 stay computable, and the fingerprint tests need per-field `model_copy` mutation that the spec-driven builder does not offer. The collision is a naming problem, not a duplication problem.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:551** (carry-over-python) - `_rows_account_for_every_planned_feature` enforces `len(rows) == features_present` and `restore_verified => not unrestored_feature_ids` in Python only. Neither invariant is expressible in (or present in) the regenerated `ir.schema.json`, and `extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/SuppressTestRun.cs` is a plain property bag with no equivalent check. So a T055 `SuppressTest`/`PackageAppender` writer can emit a run that passes `IrSerializerTests`' contract validation on the C# side and then makes the *entire* package unloadable on the Python side - the reviewer loses every check on the package, not just the suppressibility rule. Fix: Pin the invariant on the writing side too: assert both in `PackageAppender.AppendSuppressTest` (T055 already has a `PackageAppenderTests` case to extend), so a violation fails at the point of writing with a message naming the mismatch rather than as a pydantic error on someone else's machine a day later. Worth deciding at the same time whether a violating run should really take the whole package down or whether `rms.detail.individually_suppressible` should simply report unresolved - the latter matches this project's "unknown stays unknown" rule better for a field that only the C# side can get wrong.

- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/apply.py:1072** (artifacts) - `_undo_of` catches every `ValueError` from `derive_undo` and returns `None`, which the artifact defines as a deliberate value meaning "no in-place inverse exists" (apply_log.py `derive_undo` docstring, run-artifacts.md). A `rename` whose `before.name` did not read, or an `equation.edit` with no reco Fix: Keep returning `None` (the control flow is right) but carry the refusal sentence into the terminal record's `error`, so `changes.jsonl` distinguishes "this kind has no inverse in v1" from "the reading this inverse needed was not there".

## Feature 004 offline part 3 (2026-09-16)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:247** (judgement) - `build_provider` (lines 231-262) is a near-verbatim copy of `cli.provider_factory` (cli.py:243-279). The Gemini branch is character-for-character identical (deferred `from google import genai`, `client=genai.Client(**settings.client_kwargs())`, `redact=lambda text: redact_secrets(text, settings.secrets)`, `max_output_tokens=output_ceiling(...)`). The brief called out the EventSink copy as the sin to avoid and this is the same sin one module over: the next change to the Gemini enterprise path, the output ceiling, or a new SDK kwarg lands in one of the two sites and the remodel run silently diverges. contracts/tools.md's "exactly one place" is satisfied for the remodel run, but the product now has two adapter-construction bodies.
  - Fix: Extract the shared body into agent/providers (or agent/settings) as one `build_adapter(settings, *, parallel_tool_calls=None, script=None)` and have both cli.provider_factory and remodel.build_provider call it, each keeping only its own policy (the CLI's efficiency lever, the remodel run's refusal of ProviderName.FAKE). Keep the T118 AST assertion pointed at remodel/runner.py by keeping the `providers.get` call, or retarget it at the shared function.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:422** (judgement) - `judge` hardcodes `key_source="none"` in the `ProviderInfo` it writes into session.json. The review path threads the real value (cli.py:535 and :1695 pass `key_source=settings.key_source`), and `ProviderSettings._key_source_matches_the_key` (settings.py:214) makes "none" mean specifically "no api_key is set". So a remodel run that used an env key records in its run artifact that it used no key at all. That is not "unknown stays unknown" - the enum has no unknown member, so the runner is asserting something false rather than declining to answer.
  - Fix: `run_remodel` already holds `settings`; thread `settings.key_source` through to `judge` (a keyword defaulting to "none" for the provider-injected test paths) and record it. Assert it in test_remodel_runner.py alongside test_session_json_records_every_tool_call_the_turn_made.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:734** (judgement) - `run_remodel(redact=no_redaction)` defaults to no redaction even though the same call already receives `settings`, whose `.secrets` carries the key. Every provider error text that reaches `events.jsonl` (line 450-453) and, through `JUDGEMENT_FAILED`, the plan's coverage row and report.md, is unmasked unless the not-yet-written host caller remembers to pass a redactor. FR-015 puts the key out of the run folder. `start_review` has the same default, but it does not receive the settings object, so it has no choice; `run_remodel` does.
  - Fix: Default the redactor from the settings inside `run_remodel` - when `redact` is not given and `settings is not None`, use `lambda text: redact_secrets(text, settings.secrets)` - and keep the explicit parameter for callers that install a process-wide redactor the way cli._redacting does. Add a test that a provider error carrying a fake key never appears in events.jsonl or plan.json.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:633** (judgement) - `_equation_changes` hardcodes `repairs=()`, and it is the only caller of `apply.equation_changes` in the product. `_changes` in plan.py:1060-1063 states that C5 is "only the judgement phase proposes", but no proposal tool produces a repair (`propose_global` rejects an existing name outright, remodel_plan.py:363). The result: the whole FR-029 / C5 `equation.edit` path built and tested by T089a/T089b (test_remodel_equation_edit.py, SetEquationVerified, the derive_undo inverse, the guard allowlist entries for `set_Equation` and `SetEquationAndConfigurationOption`) is unreachable from any run, and contracts/tools.md's v1 scope note ("v1 can name and repair globals that already exist") has no implementation behind it. The allowlist rationale "so no allowlist entry is without a call path" (T042) is also no longer true in practice.
  - Fix: Decide with the owner which it is: either compose the deterministic repairs in `revision_two` from whatever `remodel/intent.py` identifies as a repairable existing global and pass them as `repairs=`, or record explicitly (a `PlanCoverage` row and a line in the report) that v1 composes no repair, so the gap is visible rather than inferred from an empty tuple. Do not change contracts/tools.md here - flag it for the owner.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:601** (judgement) - The deviations merge keeps the planner's stale `fillet_default_core` deviation after the model overrides that fillet. In the T116 fixture Fillet1 is defaulted to 3-Core by the planner with `report_line="reviewed as structural; move to Quarantine if cosmetic"` (plan.py:1158), the valid script then quarantines it, `_merged_targets` correctly replaces the target - and revision 2 still carries the Core deviation. report.py prints both, so one fillet appears in report.md as reviewed-as-structural-in-Core and as a model-decided Quarantine target at the same time. FR-020's point is that a reader can tell where a group came from; two contradictory provenance rows defeat it.

- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:679** (judgement) - `_merged_targets` (line 679) and `tools/remodel_plan._targets` (line 642) both substitute model targets into `plan.targets` by feature id and silently DROP any model target whose feature is not already in `plan.targets`. Today the partition is total so nothing is lost, but the drop is silent: a future planner that leaves a feature out of `targets` (or a tool rule that is relaxed) would have `decide_fillet`/`classify_unknown` return success to the model, write a `RejectedProposal`-free accepted target, and have it vanish from revision 2 and from `get_remodel_plan("targets")`. That is an accepted proposal with no record anywhere, which is the exact failure FR-016 and the "a rejection is also written down" rule exist to prevent, only for acceptances.
  - Fix: Make the invariant explicit rather than implicit: in `_merged_targets`, raise (the way `describe_changes` already raises for an unknown feature, runner.py:534) when a model target names a feature the plan's partition does not carry, or append it. Add the assertion that len(revision-2 targets) == len(revision-1 targets) and that every model target id survives.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/remodel_plan.py:508** (judgement) - Three rejection rules exist in code that contracts/tools.md's rule tables do not list: `decide_fillet.already_decided` (line 508), `classify_unknown.already_decided` (line 566) and `decide_fillet.dependents_unreadable` (line 490). All three are defensible - two targets for one feature is not a partition, and an unreadable dependency list is not a known-empty one, which is "unknown stays unknown" - but the contract's tables are presented as exhaustive ("Every rule below is a pure function ... has its own unit test") and the report will print rule ids no reader can look up. The tasks correctly forbid editing the contract here, so this needs an owner decision rather than a silent divergence.
  - Fix: Flag to the owner for a follow-up contract edit adding the three rows (the contract already carries the equivalent `at most one proposal per feature_id` row for propose_description, so this is the same rule generalised). No code change; do not edit contracts/tools.md under T106-T119.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/tools/remodel_plan.py:374** (judgement) - `propose_global` accepts an expression identifier that is an existing document global (`proposed | existing`, line 374), while the contract row reads "every identifier in `expression` is a global declared earlier in the plan | `expression references an undeclared name`". The implementation is the more useful reading and is consistent with the sibling rule ("`evidence` is non-empty unless every identifier is a global declared earlier in this plan" only makes sense if other identifiers are permitted), and `referenced_globals` plus `seed_order` handle the case correctly, so this is a wording divergence rather than a defect - but it is undocumented and untested: no case in test_tools_remodel_plan.py proposes an expression over an existing package global.
  - Fix: Add the missing accept case to test_tools_remodel_plan.py (an expression naming an existing package global with non-empty evidence) so the intended reading is pinned by a test, and flag the contract wording to the owner.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/remodel/runner.py:667** (judgement) - DRY: `_merged_targets` (remodel/runner.py:667) and `_targets` (tools/remodel_plan.py:634) are the same four-line merge - `decided = {item.feature_id: item for item in remodel.targets}` then `tuple(decided.get(item.feature_id, item) for item in plan.targets)` - carrying near-identical comments ("two targets for one feature is not a partition"). They must agree by construction, because one is what `get_remodel_plan("targets")` shows the model and the other is what revision 2 records; the day one changes, the model sees a partition the plan does not carry. `_rows` is likewise defined twice (runner.py:659 returning a list, remodel_plan.py:159 returning a dict) over the same `row.document_id == plan.document_id` filter.

Consider the same for the document-row filter (one helper returning the list; the dict is `{row.id: row for row in ...}` at the call site).

The test would pass unchanged if `judge` grew a bridge parameter and used it, as long as the local `NoBridge` stayed unwired. - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PaneActions.cs:198** (pane) - The `IEntityResolver? resolver = _options.EntityResolver()` null check plus its `NotAttached` error is now written twice, verbatim including the message string: once in `ShowEntity` (:198-206) and once in the extracted `ShowPersistRef` (:233-241). `ShowEntity`'s copy is dead - it always falls through to `ShowPersistRef`, which repeats the check - so the two copies can drift and one of them is already redundant.

Separately, `Normalize` collapses whitespace and re-joins directives, so what is asserted is "equivalent modulo whitespace", not the "byte-identical" T132 asks for; the two strings do in fact match byte for byte today, so the weaker assertion is hiding nothing yet.
  - Fix: Lift `ContractCsp()` and the comparison into one shared helper in the test assembly (beside `ReviewPageFiles.ReadContract`) that both suites call, and have it assert ordinal string equality on the raw `content="..."` value so "byte-identical" is what is actually checked. - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelPage/remodel.js:789** (pane) - The judgement section cannot be filled after a pane restart. `renderPlan` is only called from `planRun` with `remodel.planned.plan_summary`; `renderResult` does not touch `ui.judgement`, and the `remodel.result` reply carries no plan summary. So on `applyInit` with a `latest_run`, the page calls `renderRun` + `refreshResult` and the judgement region permanently reads "No plan has been read yet" for a finished run. `contracts/pane-remodel-messages.md` region 3 requires the judgement section (accepted proposals with provider and model, and every rejected proposal with the rule that refused it, "listed rather than omitted"), and the same contract's stated reason for reading `remodel.result` from the run folder is "so the tab answers after a restart".
