This touches the contract's `remodel.result` payload, so confirm the direction with the owner before changing it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:715** (pane) - When the copy goes away mid-run, `DocumentChanged` sets the stop flag with no pending stop id, and `Execute` then sends `remodel.stopped` with `id: null` because `run.StopRequested` is true. `remodel.stopped` is a reply to `remodel.stop` in the contract's page-to-host table, not a row of the host-to-page unsolicited table, so the page's `handleUnsolicited` has no case for it and drops it silently. The engineer does get the `status {stage: "error"}` banner, so nothing is invisible, but the host is posting a message type the contract does not define in that direction - which is exactly what `EveryUnsolicitedHostToPageRowIsHandledByThePage` exists to prevent in the other direction.
  - Fix: Send `remodel.stopped` only when a `remodel.stop` is actually pending (`stopId != null`), and let the abort path report itself through the `status` it already posts. If the page should learn `changes_applied` on the abort path too, that needs a new unsolicited row in the contract.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:226** (pane) - T126 says "`document.changed` naming the **copy** aborts the run". The implementation does the opposite by design: `DocumentChanged` aborts only when `CopyExists(run)` is false, and `RemodelHostTests.ADocumentChangedNamingTheCopyItselfDoesNotAbortTheRun` locks that in. The contract row is on the implementation's side ("If the copy goes away mid-run, the run aborts with the change log intact"), and aborting on a `document.changed` that names the copy would make `remodel.open_copy` abort its own run - so the behaviour looks right and the task sentence looks like loose shorthand. It is still an unrecorded deviation from a task sentence, and tasks.md may not be edited.
  - Fix: Record the deviation explicitly for the owner (task sentence vs contract row, with the `remodel.open_copy` self-abort as the reason the contract wins), and get an explicit yes before this ships, rather than leaving the contradiction only in a test name.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:504** (pane) - `Track(runDirectory, copy.CopyPath)` runs only after `pipeline.Plan` returns. If `OpenCopy` or `Plan` throws after the copy file exists, the exception propagates to `Receive`'s catch, `_busy` is reset, and the run is never added to `_runs`. The copy `.SLDPRT` is then orphaned in the run folder and unreachable: `FindRun` answers `RunNotFound` for that `run_dir`, so `remodel.discard_copy`, `remodel.open_copy` and `folder.open` all refuse it, and the engineer has to delete it from Explorer. The copy is the one artifact this feature promises the engineer can get rid of on demand.
  - Fix: Track the run as soon as `OpenCopy` returns a copy path (before `Plan`), so a failed plan still leaves a discardable, openable run record, and let the run's `plan.json` state - or its absence - carry the fact that planning failed.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/RemodelHostTests.cs:814** (pane) - The `RunInProgress` branch of `DiscardCopy` (`RemodelHost.cs:851-855`, discard requested while that run is the active one) has no test. Every other `RunInProgress` path is covered (`ASecondPlanWhileARunIsInFlightIsRefusedAsRunInProgress`, `ASecondStartWhileTheRunIsInFlightIsRefusedAsRunInProgress`), and this is the one whose failure mode is deleting a file out from under a running executor.
  - Fix: Add a case that starts a run on the blocking scheduler, sends `remodel.discard_copy` for the active run, and asserts `error {error_class: "RunInProgress"}` and that the copy folder still exists.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:853** (pane) - The WebView2 fallback panel still says 'the Review, Ask and Model check tabs cannot be shown'. The Remodel tab now renders that same panel (LoadRemodelAsync -> BuildFallback), so on a runtime-less workstation an engineer looking at the Remodel tab reads a message that does not name the tab they are on. Three tests pin the stale wording (WebViewFallbackTests.cs:88, :127, :178) - :127 is the new Remodel case, so the new test actively locks in the inaccuracy. T120 did say 'the existing message', so this is a faithful reading of the task, but the message is now wrong.
  - Fix: Name all four WebView2 tabs: '...the Review, Ask, Model check and Remodel tabs cannot be shown. The Extract tab still works.' Update the three assertions to the new sentence (they are the only consumers).
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:718** (pane) - When a run is aborted by `DocumentChanged` (the copy went away, RemodelHost.cs:230-240) there is no `_pendingStopId`, but `Execute` still sends `remodel.stopped` because `run.StopRequested` is true - with `id: null`, i.e. as an unsolicited message. `remodel.stopped` is not a row of the contract's 'Host to page (unsolicited)' table, and remodel.js `handleUnsolicited` (remodel.js:134-160) has no case for it, so it falls through `default` and is silently dropped: the engineer never learns how many changes were applied before the abort. The contract test cannot catch this because `RemodelContract.Unsolicited` is parsed from the table, which does not list it.
  - Fix: Pick one: (a) on the abort path post `status {stage:'error', message: '... N change(s) applied'}` instead of an unsolicited `remodel.stopped`, leaving `remodel.stopped` strictly a reply; or (b) add `remodel.stopped` to the unsolicited table in contracts/pane-remodel-messages.md and a `case 'remodel.stopped'` in `handleUnsolicited` - the existing `EveryUnsolicitedHostToPageRowIsHandledByThePage` test then covers it for free.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/RemodelHostTests.cs:404** (pane) - Nothing pins the `status` stage vocabulary. `PlanPostsTheStagesThePageShows` only asserts that `copying`, `planning` and `ready` appear; there is no assertion that the host never posts a stage outside the contract's nine (`copying|dumping|planning|judging|applying|verifying|saving|ready|error`). `PostStatus` takes an arbitrary string, and the page only branches on `ready` and `error` (remodel.js:857-871), so a typo ('copy' for 'copying', or the host's `judging`/`applying` drifting) is invisible at runtime and invisible in the suite. Note the host currently never emits `dumping`, `verifying` or `saving` - those would come from the (non-existent) real pipeline, so they are untested in both directions.
  - Fix: Add a case to RemodelHostTests that reads the stage enum out of the `status` row of contracts/pane-remodel-messages.md (the RemodelPageContractTests parser already reads that file as data) and asserts every stage the host posted during plan + start + stop is a member of it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageUsageLineTests.cs:88** (pane) - 005 T016a says the test must assert 'that `GET /sessions/{chat_id}` is unchanged'. `TheNumbersComeOffTheStreamAndNotOffTheSessionRoute` only regex-scans the page's own scripts for any `<x>.usage` read that is not `state.usage`/`ui.usage`. That proves the page does not read a usage total off a reply, but it says nothing about the route: if `ChatSession.public()` later grew a `usage` member (the third copy the task exists to prevent), this test stays green. The named half of the task is therefore uncovered.
  - Fix: Add the missing half on the Python side: a test over `GET /sessions/{chat_id}` asserting the response body's key set is unchanged (no `usage`, no `round_trips`), pinned against the documented shape rather than a snapshot of the current dict.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:335** (pane) - `state.usage.push(body)` (app.js:411) accumulates with no de-duplication, and the SSE frame loop dispatches every parsed frame regardless of `seq` (app.js:330-340 updates `state.lastSeq` but never skips a frame whose seq it has already seen). The page reconnects with `Last-Event-ID` (app.js:241-242), so a server that resumes inclusively, or any redelivery, double-counts tokens and round trips in a line whose whole value is that the arithmetic is trustworthy. The other accumulators tolerate this - findings/evidence/tools are keyed maps - but `usage` is the first one that is pure arithmetic, so the existing pattern is no longer safe here.
  - Fix: Guard the dispatch on `seq > state.lastSeq` (or de-duplicate `state.usage` on `round_index` within a turn), and add a case to ReviewPageUsageLineTests that feeds the same `usage` event twice and asserts the total does not change.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/PackageWriter.cs:57** (csharp-leftovers) - PhaseOrder restates the nine phase names that are already spelled as the `entityKind` argument of each RunPhase call (lines 171, 179, 190, 208, 227, 241, 251, 257, 263) - nine string literals in two places, which is the DRY violation CLAUDE.md asks to be flagged aggressively. Worse, PhaseLog.Rows() at line 558 projects over PhaseOrder only, so a row recorded under a name PhaseOrder does not contain is timed and then silently discarded. The comment at lines 52-55 names that exact failure mode ('a phase that runs and is not named here would be timed and then dropped') and then does nothing to prevent it. Nothing in PackageWriterTests would fail: the assertions compare Rows(package) against a fixed nine-element array, which is precisely what a dropped tenth row still satisfies.
  - Fix: Make one list the source of truth: declare a constant per phase (or build a HashSet from PhaseOrder), pass those constants to RunPhase instead of inline literals, and have PhaseLog.Record throw InvalidOperationException when the name is not one PhaseOrder knows - explicit over clever, and it turns a silent data loss into a visible test failure. Add one PackageWriterTests case that records an unnamed phase and asserts it throws.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/PackageWriter.cs:145** (csharp-leftovers) - `ComponentTreeResult tree = _components.Traverse(gaps, options);` runs before any phase and is not timed, and neither is ScopeFor() at line 158 nor the Finish() tail (AddComponentInstances, the reuse-key digest). On an assembly the component-tree walk is one of the most expensive parts of a dump - it is the COM traversal 003 US6 extended with the part-root node - so the sum of the nine recorded rows is not the dump. PhaseOrder's own doc comment claims it lists 'The phases of a dump, in the order Build runs them', which is not true of the first thing Build runs. T033's stated payoff is that 'which phase actually costs the time' becomes answerable rather than guessed; with the traversal missing it is still guessed for the largest slice, and lever 9 (package reuse skips the entire dump, traversal included) will under-report its own saving.
  - Fix: Either (recommended) wrap the traversal in RunPhase as a first phase named e.g. "component_tree" at the head of PhaseOrder - one call site, its gaps already flow through GapCollector, and it makes the row set actually equal the dump - or, if the owner prefers RunPhase's abort semantics untouched, time it with its own Stopwatch and record it via phases.Record. Either way state in Manifest.cs's XML doc and the pydantic description which parts of Build the rows do and do not cover.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:672** (csharp-leftovers) - `DumpPhase.name` is a bare `str` with no constraint, and the C# `DumpPhase.Name` (extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/Manifest.cs:55) is a bare string defaulting to "". The nine legal names exist only in prose - in this field description, in the XML doc at Manifest.cs:53-54, and in PhaseOrder. That is inconsistent with the closed-set discipline used everywhere else in the same object: `status` right below is a Literal of four, `profile` is an enum, GapKind is an enum. A typo'd or renamed phase validates cleanly on both sides and against the contract, and an empty name validates too (no minLength). Combined with the silent drop in PhaseLog.Rows(), a renamed phase yields a package that says nothing is wrong.
  - Fix: Make the name a closed set the way `status` is: `name: Literal["document", "manifest", "mate", "feature", "equation", "hole", "fastener", "face", "body"]` in models.py, a DumpPhaseName enum (or a validated lookup against PhaseOrder) in Manifest.cs, regenerate the contract, and add the negative cases to test_ir_phases.py and IrSerializerTests beside the existing UnknownPhaseStatus case. If the owner would rather keep the name open so a future phase needs no schema change, say so explicitly in the description - but then the PhaseLog guard above becomes mandatory, not optional.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/Manifest.cs:42** (csharp-leftovers) - `public List<DumpPhase> Phases { get; set; } = new List<DumpPhase>();` has no omit-when-empty treatment, and PackageSerializer sets DefaultIgnoreCondition = JsonIgnoreCondition.Never, so C# always writes `"phases": []`. The implementer's own test had to strip it with a regex (Regex.Replace(json, ",\\s*\"phases\": \\[\\]", ...) in IrSerializerTests) to build the pre-T033 case, which is the proof. That contradicts the guarantee stated at reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:724-726 - 'a package written before the member existed round-trips to the bytes that build wrote' - because two C# paths do exactly that round trip: Dump/PackageAppender.cs:50 deserializes a package and :63 re-serializes it (the 003 suppress-test append), and Dump/PackageReuse.cs's Copy() deserializes a candidate and re-writes it. A pre-T033 package.json passed through either now gains a member it did not have. No checked-in golden actually moves (they are files, and the suite is green), so this is a latent byte shift, not a break today.
  - Fix: Pick one and say so in both docs. Option A (recommended): make the C# omit it too - back Phases with a nullable list or add a small JsonConverter so an empty list is not written - and add an IrSerializerTests case asserting a package with no rows serializes without the member, which also removes the regex from the existing test. Option B: narrow the Python claim at models.py:724 to 'the Python writer' and add a line to Manifest.cs:33-42 saying the C# always writes the member (empty when nothing was timed), so the asymmetry is stated rather than discovered. A recommends itself for the same reason ReuseKey is kept byte-identical across the two languages.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/Manifest.cs:33** (csharp-leftovers) - The XML doc says `phases` is 'Empty in a package written by a build that timed nothing, and in one assembled from exported files rather than dumped', and the pydantic description at reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:713-719 says the same two cases. Neither mentions the third and most consequential case: Dump/PackageReuse.cs's Copy() re-serializes the earlier candidate verbatim, so a reused run's package.json carries the ORIGINAL dump's nine rows - real millisecond numbers, all `ok` - for a run in which no dump happened. Every other field in a reused package is likewise the earlier dump's and reused_from/reused_at say so, which makes it defensible; but `phases` is the one member whose whole purpose is measurement, and 005 T085a plans to read 'the per-phase dump wall clock from T033' into the lever-9 scorecard. A harness that reads phases without first reading reused_from scores the reuse arm as costing what the cold arm cost - the opposite of the lever's finding.
  - Fix: Add one sentence to both descriptions naming the case - e.g. 'In a reused package (reused_from is set) these rows are the dump it was copied from, not this run; a reader measuring this run must check reused_from first' - regenerate the contract, and add a C# test asserting a reused package's rows are the candidate's. If the owner would rather the file never state a timing this run did not make, the alternative is to clear candidate.Extractor.Phases in PackageReuse's Copy() beside the ReusedFrom/ReusedAt assignment; that is stricter against Principle I but discards a fact the earlier run legitimately recorded, so my recommendation is the documented option plus the test.
- **specs/005-llm-efficiency/tasks.md:251** (csharp-leftovers) - T093's pass/fail precondition reads: 'the reused package must be byte-identical to a fresh dump modulo `package_id`, `created_at`, `reuse_key` and the new reuse fields'. T033 has just added extractor.phases[].elapsed_ms, a wall-clock measurement that differs between any two dumps of the same document, so that precondition can never pass again as written - and T093 is explicitly blocked on T033, so the two tasks now contradict each other. Nothing in the diff notes this. (I am not proposing an edit to tasks.md - the implementer is forbidden from touching it - only that the collision be raised before T093 is attempted, since it is a gate that would otherwise fail for a reason unrelated to lever 9.)
  - Fix: Raise it with the owner now and record the answer where T093's implementer will find it (the T033 field descriptions are the natural place). Two workable directions: (A) add extractor.phases to the modulo set, i.e. the harness strips it from both packages before comparing - simplest, keeps the timing honest, and matches how package_id and created_at are already handled; or (B) have the harness compare [{name, status}] for equality and treat the elapsed values only as a reported delta, which is stronger because it still proves the reused package ran the same phases. A is the smaller change and the one I would take; B is worth it only if the owner wants the phase set itself under the gate.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Dump/PackageWriter.cs:558** (csharp-leftovers) - `PhaseLog.Rows()` projects over the fixed `PhaseOrder` array and `PhaseLog.Record` accepts any name, so a phase whose `entityKind` is not in `PhaseOrder` is timed and then silently dropped. The doc-comment names the hazard ("a phase that runs and is not named here would be timed and then dropped") but nothing enforces it: `Build_TimesEveryPhaseItRan_InTheOrderItRanThem` asserts the nine expected rows, and a tenth phase added to `Build` with a new entityKind would still produce exactly those nine, so the test stays green while its timing is lost. Every current `RunPhase` call site is in the list, so this is a latent, not a live, defect.
  - Fix: Make `Record` refuse a name that is not in `PhaseOrder` (throw, or record it and let `Rows()` emit it at the end), and add a PackageWriterTests case that a phase name outside the list is not silently dropped. Cheapest explicit form: `if (Array.IndexOf(PhaseOrder, name) < 0) throw new InvalidOperationException(...)`, which turns the hazard into a build-time-visible test failure.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_phases.py:78** (csharp-leftovers) - 005 T033 says to implement "with the schema bump and a `test_schema_sync` regeneration". The regeneration was done (specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/ir.schema.json gained `$defs/DumpPhase` and the optional `ExtractorInfo.phases`), but no bump happened: SCHEMA_VERSION and EvidencePackage.CurrentSchemaVersion both stay "1.3.0", and `test_the_phases_member_needs_no_new_schema_version` pins that. The argument in the docstring is sound (1.3.0 landed in the immediately preceding commit f1c2bd2 as part of this same feature, the member is optional and additive, and a bump would move the version string inside the 001/002/003 goldens and break SC-007 byte-identity) — but it is a deviation from binding task text and should be the owner's call, not the implementer's.
  - Fix: Get explicit owner sign-off that 1.3.0 absorbs `extractor.phases` because it is unreleased and additive, and record that decision where a later reader will find it (a line in specs/005-llm-efficiency/probe-log.md or the T033 task note). Do not bump to 1.4.0 without first checking what it does to the goldens.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Ir/Manifest.cs:42** (csharp-leftovers) - The omission-when-empty rule is implemented on the Python side only. `PackageSerializer` sets `DefaultIgnoreCondition = JsonIgnoreCondition.Never`, so C# always writes `"phases": []` for an untimed `ExtractorInfo`, while `ExtractorInfo._omit_empty_phases` (models.py:723) drops the member entirely. `IrSerializerTests.PackageWithoutThePhasesMember_StillLoadsAndStillValidates` documents the asymmetry by regex-stripping `,\s*"phases": \[\]` from C# output rather than by producing it. Impact today is nil — `PackageWriter.Finish` always adds nine rows, so C# never emits an empty one, and `remodel/artifacts.write_package` only re-serializes C#-produced packages that already carry rows — but a C#-written package read and rewritten by Python is no longer byte-identical to the C# original, which is the property `save_package`/`load_package` round-trips elsewhere rely on.
  - Fix: Either state the asymmetry in the `Phases` doc-comment ("C# writes `[]`; the Python writer omits the member when empty, and both read either form"), or put `[JsonIgnore(Condition = JsonIgnoreCondition.WhenWritingDefault)]`-equivalent behaviour on this one property via a small converter so both sides omit. Stating it is the cheaper and more honest option; silently differing writers is the thing that bites later.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor.Tests/RemodelFinishHandlerTests.cs:282** (csharp-leftovers) - `Close_RemovesTheSessionTagThroughTheGateBeforeItClosesTheDocument` pins removal-after-verification (`removed > verified` over `Copy.Members`) and that the gate saw `ICustomPropertyManager.Delete2` with no refusals, but nothing pins removal-BEFORE-`CloseDoc`. The close goes to `_harness.Seat.Closed` and the untag to `_harness.Copy.Members`, two separate fakes, so swapping lines 1704 and 1705 of BridgeDispatcher.cs leaves every test green — while on a real seat writing a custom property to an already-closed document is the failure the ordering comment at BridgeDispatcher.cs:1697-1701 says the design depends on avoiding.
  - Fix: Give the harness one shared ordered log both fakes append to (or have `FakeRemodelSeat.CloseDocument` record into `Copy.Members`), then assert `IndexOf(RemoveSessionTag) < IndexOf(CloseDocument)` alongside the existing verify-then-remove assertion. One extra assertion in the test that already exists.
- **extractor/SwReview.Extractor/Rms/RemodelCopy.cs:381** (csharp-leftovers) - Both `Tag` and `Untag` document their return value as "handed back for `remodel.log`, so the run report can still say what the seat did", but neither call site keeps it: BridgeDispatcher.cs:737 is `_remodelGate.Call(TagMember, () => RemodelCopy.Tag(tagged, parameters.RunId));` with the int discarded (the reply's `tag` field at line 801 is `parameters.RunId`, not Add3's answer), and line 1704 discards Untag's the same way. `SwGate.Call<T>` returns the value but records only the member name through the observer, so the Add3/Delete2 answers reach no artifact. Pre-existing (committed in f1c2bd2), not introduced by this working tree.
  - Fix: Either record the answer where the comment claims it goes (add it to the `remodel.log` row or to the close/open result) or correct both doc-comments to say the answer is deliberately not used because the read-back (Tag) and the imminent close (Untag) are the evidence. The second is less code and matches what the design actually relies on.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/ir/models.py:723** (csharp-leftovers) - DRY: `_omit_empty_phases` re-implements inline (`data = handler(self); if not data.get("phases"): data.pop(...)`) what the sibling wrap serializer at line 240 does through the shared `omit_when_null` helper (line 92). Two wrap serializers in one module, one using a helper and one open-coding the same pattern, and the next additive-optional member makes it three.
  - Fix: Add an `omit_when_empty(handler, self, *names)` beside `omit_when_null` and call it, so the two omission rules read as one family and the next one has an obvious home.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_ir_phases.py:78** (csharp-leftovers) - Two near-tautological cases in an otherwise strong file. `test_the_phases_member_needs_no_new_schema_version` asserts `SCHEMA_VERSION == "1.3.0"`, which restates the constant it imports and would need editing by the same hand that bumped it; `test_every_status_the_writer_can_record_is_accepted` parametrizes over the same four strings the `Literal` declares and asserts `row.status == status`. Neither can fail for a reason anyone cares about. The rest of the file (the negative cases, the golden-fixture sweep, the committed-contract validation) is genuinely load-bearing.
  - Fix: Keep them if they are wanted as executable documentation, but consider pointing the first at what it actually guards — e.g. assert the shipped golden fixtures' `schema_version` still equals `SCHEMA_VERSION`, which fails if someone bumps without regenerating the goldens.

## Feature 004 wiring round (2026-09-16)

- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:905** (python-routes) - POST /remodel/runs/{job_id}/package-after is accepted whether or not the run has reached the rendezvous. _deliver validates only the path and the profile, then calls job.deliver(...), which sets job.package and job.arrived. A package-after posted before apply_changes finishes is returned by wait_for_package_after immediately and graded as the state after the last change — the exact outcome the wait_for_package_after docstring says must not happen ("returning a stale or empty one would grade the wrong tree"). backend-remodel.md calls this route "Hands the run the dump the add-in just wrote, ending the dump_after rendezvous". The pipeline only dumps on awaiting == "package_after", so this is not reachable in the normal flow, but the route is the enforcement point and nothing enforces it. The author appears to have considered this: test_stop_is_idempotent_and_sets_the_runs_flag asserts `status_code in {200, 409}`, a status the code never returns.
  - Fix: Refuse (409, or PackageAfterRefused) when job.state != JOB_AWAITING, and tighten the test's `in {200, 409}` to the single status that is actually contracted.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:117** (python-routes) - DRY: PACKAGE_AFTER = "package-after.json" is redefined here while swreview.remodel.artifacts already exports the identical constant (artifacts.py:94) — and this module already imports NOT_MODEL_CHECK and carry_forward_exceptions from that very module. Both new test files import PACKAGE_AFTER from artifacts, so the product and its tests now read the name from two places. The stated constraint is "DRY ... move rather than copy". Same for MODEL_CHECK_PROFILE = "model_check" (line 120), a fourth spelling of a literal that artifacts.py:235, agent/runner.py:220 and plan.py:888 each spell for themselves.
  - Fix: Import PACKAGE_AFTER from swreview.remodel.artifacts alongside the two names already imported from it, and drop the local definition. Optionally hoist the "model_check" literal into one exported constant in artifacts.py and import it in all four places.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:803** (python-routes) - Two issues in _plan. (a) DRY: plan_path(run_dir).write_text(plan.model_dump_json(indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8") is a third copy of the plan-writing lines that already exist in remodel/runner.py:290-292 (plan_run) and remodel/plan.py:1416-1418 (record_state); plan_run then writes plan.json once and this immediately overwrites it. (b) POST /remodel/plan has no guard at all: re-planning a folder whose run is live rewrites plan.json back to `planned` with a fresh change list while apply_changes is running, so the poll's plan_state and changes_total flip mid-run and the run's own record_state then writes over the re-plan. Nothing in the routes refuses it.
  - Fix: (a) Give plan_run the run_id, copy_path and source arguments (or add one exported writer in plan.py) so the plan is written once by one function. (b) Refuse POST /remodel/plan when a live job holds the folder, and when changes.jsonl already exists, reusing the same RunInProgress / ResumeRefused classes; add the two route tests.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:384** (python-routes) - RemodelJob.abandon() (called from shutdown(), line 986) sets `arrived` without a package, so wait_for_package_after falls into its timeout branch and the run dies with "package-after.json never arrived: the add-in had 600s to dump the copy after the last change and the run cannot be graded without it". At shutdown the add-in had whatever fraction of 600 s had elapsed, and the reason was the backend going away, not an expiry. That sentence reaches job.error, the pane and the log, and test_a_waiting_job_is_released_when_the_backend_shuts_down asserts only `state == "failed"`, so the wrong sentence is untested.
  - Fix: Have abandon() set a flag (or deliver a sentinel) that makes wait_for_package_after raise a distinct message naming the shutdown, and assert that sentence in the shutdown test.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:695** (python-routes) - _probe coerces a missing probe_id to the empty string: "probe_id": str(_field(reply, "probe_id") or ""). The stated constraint is "unknown stays unknown", and this module already has _optional_text for exactly that (line 1006, "Unknown is not None, never ''"). An absent probe_id then travels to POST /remodel/open, where _text refuses it with a generic "probe_id is required" 400 instead of the route that actually saw the bridge answer nothing saying so.
  - Fix: Use _optional_text and refuse at the probe route (RunFolderFailed) when the bridge answered no probe_id, the way _signals already refuses a missing scope_signals block.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:849** (python-routes) - Neither self.jobs nor self._workers is ever pruned. Every run leaves its RemodelJob and a live ThreadPoolExecutor (one OS thread each until it is shut down) in the dicts for the process's lifetime; _submit adds to _workers (line 976) and only shutdown() clears it. The backend is long-lived beside the add-in, so a session of many runs accumulates both. It also means the RunInProgress scan (once reordered per must-fix 1) walks a list that only grows.
  - Fix: In _play's finally, shut down and drop that job's executor from _workers; keep the job record (the pane still polls a finished run) but consider capping or evicting finished jobs, e.g. keeping the last N.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_remodel_routes.py:1306** (python-routes) - test_the_run_route_builds_its_provider_only_through_the_runner proves only the negative half of its name. It patches swreview.cli.provider_factory to raise and asserts the run finished — which is the FR-045 "a run whose adapter cannot be built applies the plan anyway" property, not "the run reaches a model through remodel/runner.py::build_provider". Because the test bodies always pass provider: "fake" and build_provider deliberately refuses ProviderName.FAKE (runner.py:318), no test in either new file ever exercises the route actually constructing an adapter, so a regression that routed /remodel/runs through some other factory would still pass as long as the run finished.
  - Fix: Add one test that monkeypatches swreview.remodel.runner.build_provider with a recorder returning a scripted adapter, starts a run, and asserts the recorder was called exactly once with the settings the body named — the positive half the task text asks for.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_remodel_routes.py:1259** (python-routes) - TestTheDoor asserts the missing-token 401 and the foreign-origin 403 for POST /remodel/probe only. The Guard is app-wide middleware so the property holds structurally today, but the feature 002 pane rule is that every route is behind the token, and the contract's own Tests section asks this file to hold the route table as data. test_every_route_of_the_contract_answers_something already iterates DOCUMENTED_ROUTES with a token; the unauthenticated case is not iterated.
  - Fix: Parametrize the 401 (and ideally the 403) over DOCUMENTED_ROUTES the way the smoke test already iterates them, so a route later mounted outside the Guard fails here.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:741** (python-routes) - _open records only the copy path this module derived; it never compares it against the `document_path` the bridge answered. bridge-remodel.md:77 lists document_path in remodel.open's reply and step 11 of that contract has the host assert GetPathName() == copy_path, so the check exists one layer down — but open.json, plan.copy_path and every later VerifyTarget comparison are all built from the string this route composed rather than from the path the seat reports it actually opened. If the two ever diverge (normalization, a long-path form), nothing on the Python side notices.
  - Fix: Read document_path out of the reply and refuse with RunFolderFailed when it does not resolve equal to copy_path, the way _deliver compares resolved paths for the after-dump; it is two lines and it closes the loop the constitution's "re-verifies the target document's path" rule is about.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:967** (python-routes) - `_submit` creates a `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1)` per job and stores it in `self._workers`, which is only ever cleared in `shutdown()`. `_play` never shuts its executor down, so every completed remodel run leaves an idle non-daemon worker thread and its executor alive for the life of the backend process. The `ChatServer._submit` pattern this reuses is not the same case: a chat session's executor is looked up and reused across many turns, whereas a remodel job is one-shot, so the remodel copy leaks one thread per run rather than per session.
  - Fix: In `_play`, wrap the body in try/finally and in the finally pop the job's executor out of `self._workers` and call `worker.shutdown(wait=False)` - the run is over by definition at that point. Keep `shutdown()` as it is for jobs still live at process exit.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:371** (python-routes) - `shutdown()` calls `job.abandon()`, which sets `arrived` with `package` still `None`. `wait_for_package_after` then falls into the same branch as a real expiry and raises `PackageAfterTimeout` saying "package-after.json never arrived: the add-in had 600s to dump the copy after the last change" - which is false: the add-in had whatever fraction of a second elapsed before the backend was told to stop. That sentence is redacted onto `job.error` and read by the pane, so a shutdown is reported to the engineer as an add-in that was too slow. This is exactly the "unknown stays unknown" rule pointed the wrong way.
  - Fix: Give the job an `abandoned` flag (or have `abandon()` set a sentinel) and branch in `wait_for_package_after`: if the wait ended because the backend is shutting down, raise with a sentence that says so ("the backend shut down while this run was waiting for package-after.json"); keep the 600 s sentence for a genuine `arrived.wait()` expiry. `test_a_waiting_job_is_released_when_the_backend_shuts_down` already drives this path and can assert the new sentence.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_remodel_routes.py:1306** (python-routes) - `test_the_run_route_builds_its_provider_only_through_the_runner` cannot fail. It monkeypatches `swreview.cli.provider_factory` (which no route touches), calls `run_to_completion` and asserts `state["state"] == "finished"`. Even if the route built a provider through some other path and it raised, `run_remodel` catches provider-construction exceptions (runner.py:888) and finishes the run anyway, so the assertion holds either way; it is a duplicate of `test_the_run_reaches_its_terminal_state_with_every_artifact` wearing a stronger docstring. The property it claims is only actually asserted in the integration file (`scripted == []`).
  - Fix: Spy rather than assert an outcome: monkeypatch `swreview.remodel.runner.build_provider` (and/or `swreview.agent.providers.get`) with a recorder, run to completion, and assert it was called exactly once with the settings the body named and that no other construction site fired. My review used precisely this spy and it is cheap: a plugin that wraps `providers.get` showed 15 calls, all `ProviderName.FAKE`.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_chat_remodel_routes.py:1284** (python-routes) - `test_the_eight_other_routes_run_with_every_provider_factory_raising` is named for eight routes and drives four (probe, open, plan, close). The four job-scoped routes - `GET /remodel/runs/{id}`, `GET .../events`, `POST .../package-after`, `POST .../stop` - are never exercised under `providers.get = refuse_provider`, so the task's "no provider is constructed on any route but /remodel/runs" is asserted for half the surface.
  - Fix: Either rename it to what it does, or extend it: start a run with the patched registry in place, then hit the four job routes and assert each answers 200 without the factory firing. Cheap, and it closes the enumeration the task text asks for.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_remodel_apply.py:801** (python-routes) - The wiring brief specifies the stop is read "after the change in flight has landed **or been inverted** and recorded", and `pane-remodel-messages.md` says `remodel.stop` "finishes the change in flight, inverts it if it failed". Every stop test drives the landed case only: `run(tmp_path, stop_after=N)` over the default script, where each change applies cleanly. There is no test where the change in flight fails, is rolled back, and only then does the stop end the loop - so the interaction between the rollback path and the new `stop_requested()` check at apply.py:1012 is unverified.
  - Fix: Add one case to `test_remodel_apply.py`: a script whose change N fails and inverts, with `stop_requested` raised during N. Assert the run is `truncated` with `stop.reason == "stopped"`, that change N's rollback line is on disk and terminal, that `in_flight is None`, and that nothing after N was attempted.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:604** (python-routes) - `configuration` is documented in the body of both `POST /remodel/probe` and `POST /remodel/open` (backend-remodel.md, Routes table) and is dead on both. `probe` (line 592) never reads it at all. `open` reads it at 604 and writes it into `open.json` at 756, but nothing ever reads that key back: `_plan` pulls `scope_signals` and `probe_id`, `_start` pulls `rebuild_error_count`, `document_length_unit`, `which_configs`, `geometry_before` and `copy_path`, and the plan's own `configuration` comes from the package. A documented field that is accepted and discarded is surface nobody can rely on and drift the contract tests cannot see (they parse route paths and error classes, not body keys).
  - Fix: Pick one and be explicit: either pass it where it belongs (probe's configuration to `client.probe_scope`, open.json's to `plan_run`, if the planner should honour a named configuration), or drop it from the two body columns in `backend-remodel.md` and from `_open`'s signature. Given the owner decision that the plan's configuration is the copy's as measured, I'd drop it - and say in the contract that the active configuration is read from the copy, never posted.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py:283** (python-routes) - `_refusal` builds every bridge refusal's message from `str(exc)`, the host's own sentence, and the door's handler redacts it with `server.redact` - which masks only the secrets `create_app` was given (the process environment / `secrets=`). The per-request bridge secret arrives in the body and is in no redaction list, so a bridge error sentence that quoted it would be echoed verbatim in a `RunFolderFailed`/`ScopeRefused` body. The three secret tests all cover happy paths and artifacts; none covers an error body. The likelihood is low (the host composes those sentences and has no reason to quote the secret), but "the secret is never echoed" is currently a property of the host's good manners rather than of this module.
  - Fix: Redact the request's own bridge secret on the way out: capture it in `_client` and mask it in the message of any `ChatError` raised while serving that request (a small context-local redactor composed with `self.redact` is enough). Add one test: a `RemodelError` whose sentence contains `SECRET`, asserting `SECRET not in response.text`.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelBackendClient.cs:273** (csharp-pipeline) - Call, Failed, Read, Parse, Text and Flag (lines 273-403) are a byte-for-byte copy of Review/BackendClient.cs:277-398, including the 30 s timeout pair, `request.Proxy = null`, the bearer header, the BackendUnavailable/HttpError/BadResponse vocabulary and the comment wording. That is ~130 duplicated lines of the transport that carries the token, in a task whose constraints say "DRY ... move rather than copy". The brief's "exactly the way Review/BackendClient.cs does it" names the conventions to keep, not a mandate to paste them. Two copies means the next change to loopback auth or timeout handling has to be made twice and a divergence is invisible to both test suites.
  - Fix: Extract the six members into one internal helper (e.g. AddIn/Review/BackendTransport.cs: `JsonElement Call(Func<BackendEndpoint?>, method, path, body)` plus the Text/Flag/Count readers) and have both BackendClient and RemodelBackendClient call it. BackendClient keeps its supervisor, log-folder and job-object duties; its existing tests are the regression proof that the extraction changed nothing. If the owner prefers not to touch feature 002 code, the honest alternative is to leave the copy and say so in a comment - but flag it now rather than after a third client appears.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/SwRemodelSeat.cs:83** (csharp-pipeline) - The brief's IRemodelSeat row ends "Offline test over a fake" and there is no test of SwRemodelSeat at all - the class documents itself as untested like SwReviewDump. The consequence is that the very property this review is asked to verify (OpenDoc7 with Silent|LoadModel = 17, never ReadOnly/ViewOnly) is asserted only by a comment. It is also stated twice in the codebase now: as booleans here and as the integer RemodelCopy.OpenOptions in the extractor, with nothing tying them together, so a future edit that sets ReadOnly or LightWeight here would be caught by nothing.
  - Fix: Extract `internal static void Configure(IDocumentSpecification spec)` (DocumentType, Silent, LoadModel, ReadOnly=false, ViewOnly=false) and test it over a small fake IDocumentSpecification - the interface is ~27 plain properties, so a fake is a few lines: assert Silent && LoadModel && !ReadOnly && !ViewOnly, and assert the equivalence to RemodelCopy.OpenOptions by composing the flags back into an int and comparing to 17. The ActivateDoc3-vs-open branch can be covered the same way over a tiny seam if the owner wants it; the options are the part that matters.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/BackendRemodelPipeline.cs:185** (csharp-pipeline) - OpenCopy drops reply.CopyPresent on the floor: RemodelCopyReading carries only the path and the count, so the host decides the PreexistingRebuildErrors path from the count alone. backend-remodel.md makes copy_present the field that says whether a copy exists on disk. If the bridge refusal carries a detail count of 0 (or any build answers copy_present:false with a zero count), the host sees "0 rebuild errors" and proceeds to plan and run against a run folder with no copy in it - the dump then reads whatever is active, which is the source (see must_fix 1). Parsing a contract field and discarding it is also the kind of silent drift the rest of this class is careful to avoid.
  - Fix: Carry copy_present into RemodelCopyReading (or refuse in the pipeline when copy_present is false: throw RemodelRefusal("PreexistingRebuildErrors", ...) naming the count, unknown when the count is null), and test that copy_present:false with rebuild_error_count:0 refuses rather than planning.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelBackendClient.cs:156** (csharp-pipeline) - changes_total and changes_applied use `Count(...) ?? 0`, so a field that is absent, null or not an int becomes 0, while rebuild_error_count on the very same client is carefully kept null-as-unknown with a comment explaining why. After a stop, that 0 is what reaches remodel.stopped {changes_applied} through RemodelRunOutcome, so a malformed or truncated status body tells the engineer that nothing was applied to a copy that may hold a dozen applied changes. The task's "unknown stays unknown" constraint is the same rule the rebuild count follows.
  - Fix: Either make the counts nullable through RemodelRunStatus and report unknown (the host can say "the run applied an unknown number of changes; the change log says which"), or treat a status body missing either count as BadResponse the way a missing copy_path and a missing job_id already are. Add the test beside StatusGetsTheJobAndReadsEveryFieldOfIt.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/BackendRemodelPipeline.cs:78** (csharp-pipeline) - StageForPlanState maps the terminal plan state `truncated` to the stage `saving`, so a run the engineer just stopped posts "Saving the copy..." - and `saved` maps to `saving` too, so a run that has already saved says it is saving. Extend EveryStageTheRunPostsIsOnTheContractsClosedList with a truncated poll so the choice is pinned.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/BackendRemodelPipeline.cs:173** (csharp-pipeline) - Two different "copying" statuses are posted for one moment: RemodelHost.cs:467-469 posts `Copying {RunFolders.DocumentName(document.Path)} into the run folder...` and then OpenCopy posts `Copying {Path.GetFileName(request.SourcePath)} into the run folder...`. - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/BackendRemodelPipeline.cs:470** (csharp-pipeline) - The comment says the profile is read back "out of the file that was written", but the code calls PackageIndex.HeadOf(Path.GetDirectoryName(written)), which re-derives <dir>/package.json by name and ignores `written` entirely. Today they are the same file only because PackageWriter happens to name it package.json; if a dump ever returns a differently named or nested package, this silently validates the profile of a stale neighbour file and then File.Move's the real one into place unchecked.

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/BackendRemodelPipeline.cs:105** (csharp-pipeline) - _probeId is set by ProbeScope and never cleared. If OpenCopy is ever reached without a fresh probe in this process - a host path added later, or a probe that threw after an earlier run succeeded - a probe id minted for a different source travels in the open body. The bridge refuses it (scope_not_probed), so the part is safe, but the refusal the engineer reads is a scope error rather than the real cause, and the property is currently guaranteed by the host's call order rather than by this class.
  - Fix: Clear _probeId at the start of ProbeScope and again after a successful OpenCopy, and add a test that OpenCopy with no preceding probe sends probe_id: null (which the bridge refuses by name) rather than a stale id.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/RemodelBackendClientTests.cs:62** (csharp-pipeline) - T134d asks for 'method, path, token header and body per route'. Failure scenario: a future edit that builds one of those six requests without `request.Headers["Authorization"]` (or that puts the token in the query string for the GET routes, which is exactly the mistake the Guard middleware and chat-api.md warn about) passes the whole suite green, and the regression only shows up as a 401 against the live backend.

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/IRemodelBackend.cs:266** (csharp-pipeline) - `RemodelEventPage Events(string jobId, int after)` is declared on the interface, implemented in RemodelBackendClient.cs:162, faked in BackendRemodelPipelineTests.cs:784 and covered by RemodelBackendClientTests.cs:322 — but `grep -rn '\.Events(' extractor/SwReview.AddIn --include=*.cs` returns nothing. The wiring brief and backend-remodel.md are explicit that the page reads the event stream itself with the token and origin from `init`, so there is no caller by design. This is speculative surface (CLAUDE.md section 2: no abstractions for single-use code, nothing beyond what was asked) carrying its own tests, which makes it look load-bearing to the next reader.
  - Fix: Either delete the member, its implementation, its fake and its test (the page owns the stream), or name the one concrete add-in caller that needs it and wire that caller now. If it is kept deliberately as the add-in's escape hatch, say so in the XML doc in one sentence that states no production caller exists yet — but the DRY/YAGNI-consistent choice here is to remove it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/SwRemodelSeat.cs:94** (csharp-pipeline) - The open options are hard-coded as four independent booleans (`Silent = true; LoadModel = true; ReadOnly = false; ViewOnly = false`) with a comment asserting they are 'the two halves of RemodelCopy.OpenOptions (Silent | LoadModel = 17)'. The extractor pins 17 and the absence of the ReadOnly(2)/ViewOnly(4) bits (RemodelCopyTests.cs:462-466, RemodelOpenHandlerTests.cs:284), but the add-in's own OpenDoc7 path is outside that guard. Failure scenario: someone changes RemodelCopy.OpenOptions (say, adds RapidDraft) or flips `ReadOnly = true` while chasing a file-lock bug; the extractor tests stay green, the add-in tests stay green, and the engineer gets a copy they cannot Save As — the one thing the run exists to let them do. I recommend the latter given the brief's constraint, but flag it rather than assume.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:1143** (wiring) - `DeleteCopyFolder` tolerates a missing folder but not an undeletable one, and the `PreexistingRebuildErrors` callers do not wrap it. `Directory.Delete(folder, recursive: true)` is unguarded; the two Plan callers (lines 497 and 510) call it outside any try, so an `IOException`/`UnauthorizedAccessException` escapes `Plan`, is caught by `Receive`'s generic handler (line 229) and answers `HostError` with a Win32 sentence. The contract-named refusal the engineer needs never arrives. This is reachable: the preceding `pipeline.CloseCopy` failure is deliberately swallowed (lines 1134-1139), so a tool service that is not listening leaves SOLIDWORKS holding `copy\<part>-RMS.SLDPRT` open and the delete fails. `DiscardCopy` (line 906) does wrap it and answers `DiscardFailed`, so the two callers of one method behave differently on the same failure - the asymmetry is the tell.
  - Fix: Wrap the delete (or the call at 497/510) so the named refusal still goes out: catch the exception in `DeleteCopyFolder`, return whether the folder went, and have the `PreexistingRebuildErrors` handlers still send `PreexistingRebuildErrors` with a sentence that adds "the copy could not be deleted: <reason>". - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:280** (wiring) - Dispose symmetry with the Model check host is claimed but not achieved for a run in flight. The Model check host does its work on the pump thread, so `StopModelCheckPump`'s 2 s join really does bound it. The remodel run is handed to `RemodelHostOptions.Schedule` (`Task.Run`, RemodelHost.cs:75) precisely so it is off the pump - so `StopRemodelPump` bounds nothing, and `RemodelHost.Dispose` (RemodelHost.cs:267-272) only clears `_runs`, `LatestRun` and `_active`; it never calls `run.RequestStop()`. `DisconnectFromSW` then continues to `_applicationThread = null`, `_pane.Dispose()`, `Marshal.ReleaseComObject(_taskPane)`, `_swApp = null` and `GC.Collect()` while the worker is still inside `pipeline.Run` - still polling the backend, still calling `SwReviewDump.Run` on the application thread it just dropped, and still able to drive a save of the copy through the bridge. Failure scenario: engineer disables the add-in (or SOLIDWORKS unloads it) during a twenty-minute run; the run keeps touching a session whose pointers have been released.
  - Fix: Have `RemodelHost.Dispose` request the stop on `_active` before it clears it (the flag already exists and the executor already finishes the change in flight, inverts it if it failed and finalizes - exactly the shutdown behaviour wanted), and give the add-in a bounded wait for the executor to finish, the way `StopRemodelPump` bounds the pump, before `_applicationThread`/`_swApp` are nulled. Pin it with a test: dispose while a run is in flight, assert the run saw `StopRequested`.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/RemodelHostTests.cs:1647** (wiring) - The `CloseCopy` half of `DeleteCopyFolder`'s tolerance is asserted only by a comment. `FakePipeline.CloseCopy` can never throw (it just records "close"), so the swallow at RemodelHost.cs:1134-1139 - whose comment says "A copy that is not open, or a SOLIDWORKS that will not answer, must not stop the folder being deleted" - is untested in both callers. - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:626** (wiring) - The new secret in the wiring is never pinned by a test. `Secrets` now returns the API key and `_toolService?.RemodelBridge?.Secret`, which is the one genuinely new redaction input in T134f (the remodel bridge secret authorizes every write to the copy). `RemodelWorld.Open` (RemodelHostTests.cs:1420) configures `Secrets = () => new[] { Endpoint?.Token }` only, so `TheTokenNeverReachesThePageInAStatusOrAnError` (line 143) proves one secret is masked and nothing proves a second one in the list is, nor that a null entry (the ordinary state before the tool service listens) does not break redaction. `SwReviewAddIn` itself is untestable headless by the project's own precedent (ToolServiceWiringTests.cs:22), so the host-side test is the only place this can be held.

- **specs/004-resilient-remodeler/contracts/backend-remodel.md:129** (wiring) - Two refusal classes the Remodel page can receive are in no contract. `backend-remodel.md` line 129 enumerates the host's own classes as `NoDocument`, `NotAttached`, `DocumentReadOnly`, `RunNotFound`, `CopyDiscarded` plus `PreexistingRebuildErrors`, and `pane-remodel-messages.md`'s refusal table lists eleven - but `RemodelHost` also sends `OpenFailed` (line 875) and `DiscardFailed` (lines 908, 924), and `RemodelHostTests` asserts `OpenFailed` at line 552. No code change.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:760** (wiring) - The new `_remodelHost?.PostStatus(stage, message)` line puts the Remodel page on a fan-out that emits an off-contract stage. `PostStatusToPages("backend_starting", ...)` at line 731 sends `status {stage: "backend_starting"}`, but `specs/004-resilient-remodeler/contracts/pane-remodel-messages.md:67` defines a CLOSED list of nine stages (`copying|dumping|planning|judging|applying|verifying|saving|ready|error`) that does not contain `backend_starting` — unlike `specs/003-resilient-modeling/contracts/model-check.md:122` and `specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/pane-host-messages.md:71`, which both list it explicitly. `BackendRemodelPipelineTests.EveryStageTheRunPostsIsOnTheContractsClosedList` (RemodelHostTests' sibling, line 374) enforces that list for the pipeline and asserts `stages.Count == 9`, so the project already treats the list as closed; the wiring is the one place that breaks it. - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:267** (wiring) - `Dispose()` clears `_runs`, `LatestRun` and `_active` but never calls `run.RequestStop()` on the run in flight, and this is the one place the Model check symmetry does not hold. `ModelCheckHost.Dispose` (ModelCheck.cs:228) is equally minimal, but a check runs synchronously on its pump thread, so stopping the pump stops the work. Untested: no test in RemodelHostTests.cs exercises Dispose with a run in flight.

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/RemodelHostTests.cs:536** (wiring) - Test-strength gap on T134g's first named case, the `RemodelRefusal` mapping. `SendRefusal` (RemodelHost.cs:1292) passes three fields through — class, message and `Retryable` — but only the class and message are pinned in both directions. `retryable` is asserted exactly once for a refusal, at line 447, and there the expected value is `true`. The two tests that construct `RemodelRefusal(..., retryable: false)` (line 514 for the executor, line 543 for `open_copy`) assert only `ErrorClass`. So a `SendRefusal` that hardcoded `retryable: true` — or dropped the parameter and let PaneActions default it to true — passes the entire suite. The `retryable` flag is what the page uses to decide whether the button is pressable again, so a stuck-true value is a user-visible wrong answer, not cosmetic.
  - Fix: Add `Assert.False(world.Reply("error", "o1").GetProperty("retryable").GetBoolean());` to `AnRefusalRaisedWhileOpeningTheCopyIsAnsweredWithItsOwnClass` (line 536) — one line, no new fixture, and it makes the pair at 447/536 pin the field in both directions.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/BackendRemodelPipeline.cs:464** (wiring) - Out of my scope (T134e), but it is the one place the 'nothing reaches the source document' property is enforced by assumption rather than by check, so reporting it rather than letting it pass. `DumpInto` calls `_dump.Run(runDirectory, ..., DumpProfile.ModelCheck)`, which dumps whatever SOLIDWORKS says is the ACTIVE document, and then verifies only the PROFILE of the file that came back (`PackageIndex.HeadOf(...).Profile != DumpProfile.ModelCheck`). It never verifies the dumped document's path is the copy. If the active document is the source at that moment — the engineer clicks the source's window between `remodel.open` and the dump, or the bridge's open did not activate — `package-before.json` is a reading of the SOURCE, the plan is built against the source's feature tree, and it is then applied to the copy. No write reaches the source, so the constitution's hard rule holds, but the run is silently wrong and the artifacts claim otherwise. `OpenCopy` already knows the copy's path (`RemodelCopyReading.CopyPath`), so the check is cheap.
  - Fix: Hand `DumpInto` the expected copy path and assert the written package's document path (or `DumpSummary`'s own document path) matches it, throwing the same shaped `InvalidOperationException` the profile mismatch throws. Add the case to BackendRemodelPipelineTests beside the existing profile-mismatch test.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:585** (wiring) - T134f itself has no test. `StartRemodelHost`, `OnRemodelPageMessage`, `StartRemodelPump`, `StopRemodelPump`, the `-=` at line 248, the `Dispose` at line 280 and the fan-out at line 760 are referenced by nothing in SwReview.AddIn.Tests (grep confirms: the only test-project references to `SwReviewAddIn` are the COM-attribute and assembly-redirect tests). Every behaviour the task names is therefore verified only by reading. This is precedent-consistent — `StartModelCheckHost` and the `ModelCheckPageMessageReceived` subscription are equally untested, and ToolServiceWiringTests.cs:22 states the reason ('None of this can be tested through SwReviewAddIn itself') — so I am not calling it a defect of this task. But it means the subscribe/unsubscribe/dispose symmetry I verified by inspection has no regression guard, and the `backend_starting` finding above is exactly the kind of thing such a guard would have caught.
  - Fix: Either accept it explicitly as the established pattern, or do what ToolServiceWiringTests did for the tool-service delegates: extract the three or four delegates `StartRemodelHost` composes (the endpoint func, the bridge func, the `RegisterLatestRun` action, the `Secrets` func) into a small testable factory and assert their behaviour there. I would not do this now — it is scope the task did not ask for — but it should be an owner decision rather than an omission.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs** (wiring, fixer note) - `Execute`'s `catch (RemodelRefusal)` synthesizes `RemodelRunOutcome("failed", 0)`, so `remodel.stopped {changes_applied}` reports 0 after a refusal late in a run even though changes landed; the pipeline cannot hand the applied count out through an exception.
  - Fix: carry the applied count on `RemodelRefusal` (or read it from `changes.jsonl` in the catch) so the closing message states what landed.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/remodel.py** (python-routes, author note) - `copy_path_for` restates the C# `RemodelCopy.CopyPathFor` convention (`<run_dir>/copy/<stem>-RMS.SLDPRT`) in a second language; the two can drift silently.
  - Fix: have `remodel.open`'s reply carry the copy path the bridge actually created and drop the Python derivation, or pin both with one shared fixture.

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/BackendProxyPageTests.cs:118** (proxy) - None of the page tests the brief names for item 1 exist, and the wired path has no test at all. What landed is the opposite assertion - `ThePagesOwnResourcesStillLoadWithTheProxyFilterInstalled` ends `Assert.Empty(fake.Requests)`. So `ServeProxied` (reading headers and `Content` before the deferral goes async, `TryServe` off the UI thread, `CreateWebResourceResponse` and `Complete()` back on it) is exercised by nothing: the claims my review was asked to verify are true by inspection only.

Add a test-only fixture that maps a second virtual host with no folder mapping, points `TaskPaneOptions.Backend`/`ProxySend` at the fake, and asserts the brief's four properties through the real `ServeProxied`. That test survives whichever way out the owner picks, and it is the only way the ArgumentException in must_fix 2 would have been caught.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:683** (proxy) - `ConfigureAwait(true)` makes the UI-thread hop implicit: it resumes on whatever `SynchronizationContext` was current when the handler ran. In the pane that is the WinForms context, so it is correct today, but if the event ever fires with no ambient context the continuation sets `args.Response` and completes the deferral from a thread-pool thread - a COM wrong-thread failure that the blanket `catch (Exception)` at line 693 swallows silently, leaving the request unanswered with no trace. "Explicit over clever" argues against leaving the most load-bearing thread hop in the method to an ambient.
  - Fix: Marshal explicitly - capture the control and resume with `BeginInvoke`, or assert `SynchronizationContext.Current != null` at registration time with a comment naming the WinForms context as the requirement. Either way the reader sees which thread each of the three steps runs on.

The test would pass with `TryServe` hard-coded to return a 500 for every URL. It reads as coverage of "a null answer leaves page resources alone" and is not.
  - Fix: Either narrow the docstring to what it actually proves (installing the filter and the handler does not break page loading), or move the real property to the non-mapped-host fixture above, where a request outside the prefix can actually reach `TryServe` and be seen to be left alone.

Only the event stream moved off loopback. The deviation is documented and the byte-identity constraint is intact (Remodel page CSP == contract CSP, both unchanged, RemodelPageContractTests green), and the brief-named tests for these items - the page-level fake status/body, Authorization-and-no-Origin at page level, the 501 through the page, and the `fetch('http://127.0.0.1:1/')`-refused-by-CSP test - are correspondingly absent. If interception still cannot be had, put doc section 4's two ways out to the owner: (A) a second, non-folder-mapped virtual host - smallest change, but re-opens CORS and adds OPTIONS handling to a class that currently strips every Access-Control-* header; (B) serve the pages themselves through WebResourceRequested and drop the folder mapping - this is the design section 2 chose, `connect-src 'self'` holds and no CORS anywhere, but it changes how every page loads and the offscreen page tests map the host today. - **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:713** (proxy) - DRY: `ReadRequestContent` is a byte-for-byte duplicate of the private `Read` in BackendProxyHandler.cs (copy to MemoryStream, ToArray, null when empty) - the same rule stated twice, in two files, with two docstrings that must stay in agreement. It also costs two extra copies of every request body: byte[] on the UI thread, wrapped in a new MemoryStream at line 683, re-read into another byte[] by the handler.

A regression here would put `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` back on a same-origin response - re-opening precisely the question the proxy closes - with nothing to catch it.

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:696** (proxy) - ServeProxied swallows every exception with no record anywhere. The two catch blocks are silent by design, but the pane already owns a redacting report channel, and the failure mode this whole class exists for was diagnosed on a pilot workstation by reading logs. A proxy call that throws leaves the engineer with the same three words (`Failed to fetch`) and nothing to distinguish cause from cause - which is exactly the confusion docs/pane-backend-proxy.md section 4 warns about.
  - Fix: Report one redacted line per failure through the existing choke point (URL path only, never headers - the Authorization header is in scope on that stack), rate-limited if need be. The token must not appear; today it does not reach any log, which is correct and should stay pinned by a test once ServeProxied is testable.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:258** (events) - `Redact(frame)` runs per SSE frame, and `Redact` resolves the secret every call: `PaneActions.Redact` -> `_options.Secrets()` -> `_settings.ResolveApiKey(...)` -> `Convert.FromBase64String` + `ProtectedData.Unprotect` (UserSettings.cs:322) + a fresh `Encoding.UTF8.GetString` of the plaintext key. The event stream is the highest-volume path in the product - `text.delta` is one frame per token - so a review now performs one DPAPI decryption per token and materialises the plaintext API key as a new, uncleared managed string per token. `Array.Clear(plain, ...)` clears the byte array but not the string, so the key is duplicated across the managed heap thousands of times per review, which is exactly the crash-dump exposure chat-api.md's token rule exists to prevent, and it is on the reader thread that is supposed to keep up with the stream.
  - Fix: Memoise the resolved secret in `ReviewHost` - resolve once per settings load and on every successful `settings.save`, hand `PaneActions` a `Secrets` lambda that returns the cached value - so `Redact` stays the single choke point but stops decrypting. Add a test that N frames cause one key resolution.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/EventStreamPump.cs:197** (events) - DRY: the loopback URL is rebuilt by hand as `string.Format("http://127.0.0.1:{0}/sessions/{1}/events", endpoint.Port, ...)` even though `BackendEndpoint.Origin` (BackendProcess.cs:52) is that exact string and is what `BackendClient.Send` uses (BackendClient.cs:286). Two places now encode "the backend is at 127.0.0.1 on this port"; if the endpoint ever gains a host or a scheme, this one will not follow.
  - Fix: Build the URI from `endpoint.Origin + "/sessions/" + Uri.EscapeDataString(chatId) + "/events"`. The `Uri.EscapeDataString` on the chat id is right and should stay.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/EventStreamPump.cs:210** (events) - Connection starvation on net48. The project targets net48 (extractor/Directory.Build.props), where `ServicePointManager.DefaultConnectionLimit` is 2 per ServicePoint, and nothing in the tree raises it (no `ServicePoint` reference anywhere under extractor/). This request holds a connection to 127.0.0.1:<port> open for the whole life of a review, leaving exactly one for every other `HttpWebRequest` to the same endpoint - `BackendClient` (`IsTurnRunning`, `ListModels`, `CreateSession`), `BackendProxyHandler.Send` on the WebView2 thread, and `RemodelBackendClient`. Two of those overlapping while the stream is open means the third blocks until its `Timeout` and surfaces as a spurious backend failure. Nothing in the current tests can see this: they open one stream and make no other call.
  - Fix: Set `request.ServicePoint.ConnectionLimit` (8 is ample) in `Build`, with a comment saying the stream pins one connection for the life of a review and net48 hands out two by default. One explicit line beats a process-wide `ServicePointManager` mutation from inside an add-in.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/EventStreamPumpTests.cs:48** (events) - `FramesArriveInOrderAndVerbatim` and `AMultiLineDataFrameIsNotSplit` only ever feed `\n`-terminated frames (`SseConnection.Pump` writes `frame + "\n\n"`). The real backend is sse-starlette, whose `DEFAULT_SEPARATOR` is `\r\n` (reviewer/.venv/Lib/site-packages/sse_starlette/event.py:13), so every frame in production takes the CRLF path through `Drain` - the one path with no coverage at all. A regression that mishandled a bare `\r`, or that stopped normalising and shipped `\r` into the page, would pass the whole suite.
  - Fix: Give `SseConnection.Send` an optional separator and add one case that writes a multi-line `\r\n` frame plus a `\r\n\r\n` terminator, asserting the delivered frame is the `\n`-joined form. While there, add a frame containing a blank `data:` line to pin what the blank-line split does to it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/EventStreamPumpTests.cs:234** (events) - `EventsOpenStreamsFramesToThePageAndNeverPostsTheToken` does not actually exercise the redaction it names in its docstring. `PumpWorld` builds `UserSettings.Defaults()` with no `ApiKeyProtected` and no environment override, so `Secrets()` yields a single null and `Redaction.Redact` is a no-op; and the bearer token was never in a frame in the first place, it is a request header. `AssertNothingPostedContains(Token)` therefore passes byte-for-byte with `Redact(frame)` deleted - which is the FR-015 rule this test is the only guard for.
  - Fix: Configure a key on the settings the world writes (`SetApiKey`), have the fake server send a frame whose `data:` embeds that key the way a provider error message would, and assert the posted `events.frame` carries the mask and not the key. Do the same for one `events.closed` reason.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/EventStreamPumpTests.cs:271** (events) - The brief names four stoppers - "`events.close`, a new `events.open`, a `ready` from a reloaded page, and `Dispose` all stop the pump". Three are covered (`AReadyFromAReloadedPageAndAnEventsCloseBothStopThePump`, `ASecondOpenReplacesTheFirstAndDropsItsLateFrames`). `Dispose` is only ever called in `PumpWorld.Dispose` with nothing asserted after it, so `ReviewHost.Dispose() -> _events.Dispose()` - the line that keeps a reader thread and a socket from outliving the pane - has no test. Deleting `_events.Dispose()` from ReviewHost.cs:405 leaves the suite green.
  - Fix: Add a case that opens a stream, waits for a frame, disposes the host, then sends another frame from the still-open connection and asserts nothing further is posted and the server sees the connection drop.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:524** (events) - `LastEventId` accepts a JSON number as well as a string ("A number as well as a string, because the page's own `seq` is a number"). The page does not send a number: app.js:274 sends `state.lastSeq > 0 ? String(state.lastSeq) : null`, and the contract row says `{chat_id, last_event_id | null}`. The numeric branch is unrequested flexibility for a caller that does not exist, and no test reaches it - every `events.open` in the suite passes `last_event_id = (string?)null`. Per the repo's "no configurability that wasn't requested", it is either a branch to delete or a branch to test.
  - Fix: Preferred: drop the `JsonValueKind.Number` branch and keep string-or-null, which is what the contract and the only caller say. If it is kept deliberately, add the two tests it needs - a numeric `last_event_id` arriving as the `Last-Event-ID` header, and `0` / a negative arriving as no header at all - since `Blank`, the `> 0` guard and the invariant-culture format are all untested today.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/EventStreamPump.cs:277** (events) - A narrow race lets a replaced pump still post `events.closed`. `Closed` checks and bumps `_generation` atomically, but calls `_onClosed` after releasing the lock. If a reader is ending naturally at the moment the page sends a new `events.open`, `Closed` can pass its generation check first, then `Open`'s `Close()`/`++_generation` runs and starts the replacement, and only then does the old thread post `events.closed`. The page's `payload.chat_id === state.chatId` guard does not help on the common case, which is a reconnect to the SAME chat: `scheduleReconnect` fires against a stream that was just opened, sets `state.stream = null` and schedules a duplicate `openStream()`. Combined with the backoff regression above this becomes a self-sustaining open/close loop. Frames are immune - `Deliver` re-checks under the lock on every frame - it is only the terminal message that escapes.
  - Fix: Capture the decision and the callback invocation under one guard: have `Closed` return the reason (or null) from inside the lock and invoke `_onClosed` only when the caller is still the current generation at invocation time, e.g. re-check `generation == _generation - 1` from the lock exit, or serialise `Open`/`Close`/`Closed` on the same gate with the callback dispatched by whoever won. A test that ends a connection and calls `Open` from another thread in a tight loop pins it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:278** (events) - `showStreamState('Streaming.')` now fires the instant `events.open` is posted, before anything has connected. With the backend down the engineer sees "Streaming." for up to a second, then "the review backend is not running. Reconnecting...", then "Streaming." again - a status line that alternates between a claim and its contradiction. The old code showed it only after `response.ok`.
  - Fix: Same fix as the backoff: move `showStreamState('Streaming.')` into the first `events.frame` for the current chat. Until then the honest text is "Connecting...".
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:405** (events) - `Dispose` -> `_events.Dispose()` -> `Close()` joins the reader for up to `StopTimeout` (5s). `SwReviewAddIn.DisconnectFromSW` runs on the SOLIDWORKS UI thread and disposes the review host there (SwReviewAddIn.cs:~277). The join is bounded, which is what the brief asked for, but the bound is a 5-second SOLIDWORKS freeze on add-in unload in the case where the abort does not unblock the socket read promptly. `events.close` and `events.open` do not have this problem - they are handled on the dedicated page-message pump thread.
  - Fix: Either shorten the bound on the dispose path (the reader is a background thread; 500ms is enough to observe an abort that worked, and a wedged one dies with the process), or dispose the pump off the UI thread before the UI-thread teardown. Say which in the comment, because "bounded" alone does not tell the next reader that this bound lands on the application thread.
