The test's own docstring says the second half "is the point of the round", so it should name the route.
  - Fix: Add `"/events"` to the forbidden list (after confirming no other page names it), or assert on the stripped source that no `fetch(` argument expression contains `events`. Cheap, and it fails the exact regression the test was written for.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:538** (events) - The numeric branch of `LastEventId` (`JsonValueKind.Number` -> `seq.ToString(InvariantCulture)`) is unreachable from the only caller: app.js line 274 sends `state.lastSeq > 0 ? String(state.lastSeq) : null`, always a string or null. The doc comment justifies it as "the page's own seq is a number and asking it to stringify one before sending it would be a rule to forget" - but the page does stringify, so this is flexibility that was not requested and that nothing tests.
  - Fix: Either delete the numeric branch (explicit over clever: the contract row says `{chat_id, last_event_id | null}` and the page sends a string), or keep it and pin it with the numeric test case named in must_fix #2. Do not leave it untested and unreachable.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/EventStreamPumpTests.cs:193** (events) - `ASecondOpenReplacesTheFirstAndDropsItsLateFrames` is weaker than it reads, in two ways. (1) `Open` calls `Close` first, which aborts the first request, so the first connection's writer thread throws on the next write and the late frame usually never reaches the reader at all - the generation guard in `Deliver` is not really the thing under test, and the test would likely still pass with that guard removed. (2) The only closure assertion is `Assert.DoesNotContain("chat-8", ...)`, but the spurious close the abort would produce is for `ChatId`, not `chat-8`, so a regression that let the replaced reader post `events.closed` for the old chat passes here. (The guard is separately covered by `CloseStopsTheReaderAndSaysNothingFurther`, so this is about this test's strength, not a hole in the implementation.)
  - Fix: Add `Assert.Empty(recorded.Closed);` to this test, and rename or re-scope it to what it actually proves (a second open opens a second connection and the recorder only ever sees the new chat's frames). To exercise the guard properly, drive `Deliver` past a generation bump without an abort - e.g. a second `Open` for the same chat while the first server is mid-frame - or state plainly in the docstring that the abort is the primary mechanism and the generation counter is the backstop.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/EventStreamPumpTests.cs:146** (events) - No test covers a non-200 answer on the event route. `GetResponse()` throws `WebException` for 401/404/500, which lands in the catch at EventStreamPump.cs:184 and posts `events.closed` with a raw .NET string ("The remote server returned an error: (404) Not Found."). That is the single most likely real-world failure of this route (unknown chat id, rotated token, backend restarted) and it is the path that feeds the reconnect loop in must_fix #1.
  - Fix: Add a case where the `SseBackend` answers the request with 404 (and one with 401) and assert `events.closed` arrives once, carries the chat id, and that no frame was delivered.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewHost.cs:405** (events) - `_events.Dispose()` in `ReviewHost.Dispose` is untested. `PumpWorld.Dispose` calls `_host.Dispose()` but asserts nothing about it, so deleting line 405 leaves the whole suite green while every pane close leaks a reader thread and an open socket against a backend that is being shut down. The brief lists `Dispose` alongside `events.close`, a new `events.open` and `ready` as one of the four things that must stop the pump; the other three are tested and this one is not.
  - Fix: Extend `AReadyFromAReloadedPageAndAnEventsCloseBothStopThePump` to a third stopper, or add a sibling test: open a stream, dispose the host, send another frame from the connection, sleep, and assert no further `events.frame` and no `events.closed` were posted.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/EventStreamPumpTests.cs:81** (events) - `AMultiLineDataFrameIsNotSplit` writes the whole frame in a single `Write` + `Flush` (SseConnection.Pump), so it does not test what its name claims - a chunk-based reader that split on `\r?\n\r?\n` in a buffer would pass it too. What it genuinely proves is that the `ReadLine` lines are rejoined with `\n`, which is worth having but is not the brief's case ("a frame arrives in two reads as often as in one", per the Drain docstring).
  - Fix: Add a case that sends one frame in two writes with a flush between them, splitting mid-line (e.g. `"id: 9\ndata: {\"se"` then `"q\":9}\n\n"`), and assert one frame arrives, verbatim. That requires a small `SendRaw(string)` on `SseConnection` that writes without appending the frame terminator.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageContractTests.cs:161** (events) - The "page no longer reads the stream itself" assertion bans `text/event-stream`, `getReader` and `ReadableStream`, but not the route. A regression that reintroduced `fetch(backendUrl(sessionPath('/events')))` - with `EventSource`, or reading `response.text()` in a loop - would pass all three. The route string appears nowhere in page code today (only in comments, which `Strip` removes), so the check is free.
  - Fix: Add `"/events"` to the `gone` array, with the message naming the route rather than the API.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:167** (events) - The `events.frame` guard tests only `payload.chat_id === state.chatId`, while `events.closed` and `scheduleReconnect` correctly test `state.stream`. After `endSession` calls `closeStream()` (line 564), `state.stream` is null but `state.chatId` is unchanged, so any frame already in flight from the host still appends a card after "The session ended." It is a small, benign window, but it is the one place where the page's two stream guards disagree with each other.

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:278** (events) - `showStreamState('Streaming.')` is written the moment `events.open` is posted, before the host has opened a socket - the page says it is streaming when all it knows is that it asked. With must_fix #1 unfixed it also repaints "Streaming." once a second against a backend that is refusing, so the banner alternates between a failure reason and a claim that is false. This is the same 'unknown stays unknown' rule the round holds elsewhere.
  - Fix: Fold this into the must_fix #1 change: leave `showStreamState('Connecting...')` (or nothing) at the open, and set `Streaming.` together with the backoff reset when the first `events.frame` arrives.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceGate.cs:251** (tool-service) - A document change that arrives while a start is in flight is dropped permanently, and the comment that justifies dropping it is only conditionally true. The guard at line 251 returns when `_starting`, and line 253 explains 'A start already in flight will attach to whatever is active when it runs'. `StartCore` attaches on the application thread (`ToolServiceHost.Start` -> `OnApplicationThread(options.Invoker, ...)`, ToolServiceHost.cs:712) while `_starting` stays true until after the attach returns and the pipe is listening (ToolServiceGate.cs:364). `ActiveDocChangeNotify` is delivered on that same application thread, so if it lands *after* the attach delegate captured document A but *before* the worker reaches line 364 — the window covering `server.Start()` and the first log write — `FollowDocument` returns at 251 and nothing ever re-checks. The service stays attached to A until the engineer happens to switch documents again. Narrow, but it is the original bug re-created, and no test covers `FollowDocument` during `_starting` at all.
  - Fix: Record the dropped path instead of discarding it: add a `private string? _pendingFollow;` set under the lock at line 251 when the guard fires because of `_starting`, and at the end of `StartCore` (after `_started(service)` succeeds) read and clear it under the lock and, if set, call `FollowDocument(pending)` again. Add a test with the injected `schedule` seam: `EnsureStarted`, then `FollowDocument(other)` while the start is still pending, then run the pending action — assert a second start happens and the gate ends attached to `other`. Correct the comment at line 253 while you are there.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceGate.cs:279** (tool-service) - No de-duplication of the busy log line. SOLIDWORKS raises `ActiveDocChangeNotify` on every activation, including simply clicking between two already-open document windows, so during one long review turn the engineer flipping windows gets the identical 'not re-attaching to X: a turn is running against Y' line appended over and over. Each line is a separate `File.AppendAllText` open/write/close (ToolServiceLog.Write, ToolServiceHost.cs:470-483) and — until must_fix #1 lands — each one is preceded by a fresh round of HTTP busy questions on the application thread. The log the finding points the engineer at (`tool-service-*.log`) becomes harder to read, not easier, which is the opposite of the point.
  - Fix: Keep the last logged pair on the gate (`private string? _lastBusyLog;`) and write only when `(activePath, attached.DocumentPath)` differs from it, clearing it whenever a restart actually happens or the service is replaced. Assert in `ABusyBridgeIsNotRestartedAndTheLogSaysWhyNamingBothDocuments` that three `FollowDocument` calls for the same active path while busy still produce exactly one line (the test already uses `Assert.Single`, so it only needs the extra calls).
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceGate.cs:273** (tool-service) - When the busy question throws, the engineer is told in the wrong place. The docstring at line 239 says 'A busy question that throws counts as busy', and the behaviour is right, but the reason goes only to `_report` — the add-in log and the pane — while the *tool-service* log, which is the file finding 1 sends the engineer to and the file that carries the 'attached to ...' line, gets nothing. The engineer who switches documents, sees stale-document errors and opens `tool-service-*.log` finds no explanation at all in the one case where the gate genuinely could not decide. The test `ABusyPredicateThatThrowsIsTreatedAsBusyAndReported` (ToolServiceWiringTests.cs) asserts only `world.Reports` and does not notice.
  - Fix: Call the same `Log(attached, ...)` on the throw path with a reason of its own — e.g. `"not re-attaching to " + activePath + ": could not tell whether a turn is running against " + attached.DocumentPath` — before returning, and extend the test to assert the line lands in `world.Services[0].LogLines` as well as in `Reports`.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:736** (tool-service) - The busy predicate does not cover everything that holds the bridge, and nothing re-points the consumers that cached the old one. (a) A live Terminal ('Ask') session is not counted: `CliProfileWriter` bakes `{pipe, secret}` from `GeneralChatBridge` into the generated CLI profile at terminal start (Terminal/CliProfileWriter.cs:378), so a restart mid-session leaves the running CLI pointed at a disposed pipe with a retired secret — its MCP tool calls fail with a connect/unauthorized error rather than the documented `document no longer open`. (b) The same is true of any backend chat created earlier: `POST /sessions` carries the bridge once, at creation (ReviewHost.cs:882), and only new sessions see the re-pointed `reviewOptions.Bridge`; an idle-but-open chat's next turn uses the dead pipe. (c) Secret hygiene: `TerminalHost.Secrets` reads the *current* general-chat secret late (`() => new[] { toolService.GeneralChatBridge?.Secret }`, Terminal/TerminalHost.cs:283), so after a restart the retired secret drops out of the redaction set used at TerminalHost.cs:1136 while a live CLI process still holds it in its profile — an error message that echoes the profile or env would now pass through unredacted. Before this change secrets never rotated inside one SOLIDWORKS session, so none of this could arise.
  - Fix: Three separable steps, in priority order: (1) add 'a terminal session is attached' to the `busy` lambda so the common case simply does not restart under a live Ask session (and log it, which the existing wording already covers); (2) have `TerminalHost` keep retired secrets in its redaction list — change `Secrets` to return an accumulating set rather than only the current bridge's secret — so a rotation can never un-redact something; (3) decide explicitly, with the owner, what should happen to an already-created chat's stale bridge: either re-write the profile / restart the terminal on the `_started` callback, or accept it and say so in the doc. (3) is a design call, not a code fix I would make unilaterally.
- **docs/pane-findings-2026-09-16.md:48** (tool-service) - Finding 1 has no closing '**Fixed.**' line, so the doc still reads as an open defect while the code that fixes it is in the tree. The brief's item 5 asks for one on each of the two findings; finding 2 has one at line 90, finding 1 ends at 'Worth considering.' (line 48) with nothing after it. The doc's own intro (line 7-8, 'Both are real and reproducible, and neither is fixed.') is now stale for both findings.
  - Fix: Append a '**Fixed.**' paragraph under finding 1 naming what landed — `ToolServiceGate.FollowDocument`, called from `SwReviewAddIn.OnActiveDocumentChanged` after `EnsureStarted`, skipped while a review turn or remodel run holds the bridge and logged into the tool-service log when it is, plus `DocumentPresenceDispatcher.DocumentClosedError(attachedDocumentPath)` now naming the attached document — and amend the intro sentence at line 7-8 so it no longer says neither is fixed.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceGate.cs:401** (tool-service) - `Canonical` is under-tested for the cases it exists to handle, and one of its behaviours is surprising. `Path.GetFullPath` resolves a relative path against the *process* working directory, which for an add-in hosted in SLDWORKS.exe is whatever SOLIDWORKS last set it to — so two relative spellings could canonicalise to two different absolute paths, or to the same wrong one. It also does not normalise 8.3 short names, `subst` drives, or a UNC path versus the mapped drive that points at the same share, all of which are ordinary in a PDM/vault setup and all of which would read as 'different document' and trigger a restart. The documented fallback (line 407-410: `GetFullPath` throws -> compare the trimmed raw strings, because 'differ' is the answer that restarts) is the right call, but no test drives it: `TheSameDocumentSpeltDifferentlyDoesNotRestartAnything` covers only case and a `..` segment.
  - Fix: Add two cases to `TheSameDocumentSpeltDifferentlyDoesNotRestartAnything`'s neighbourhood: a path containing a character `GetFullPath` rejects on both sides (asserting the raw-string fallback still says 'same', so no restart), and a trailing-separator pair (`C:\models\deck.SLDASM` vs `C:\models\deck.SLDASM\` is already handled by the `TrimEnd`, but nothing pins it). Then state the known limits in the `SameDocument` docstring — short names, substituted drives and UNC-vs-mapped spellings restart once and then settle, which is acceptable because the restart is idempotent — so the next reader does not have to rediscover them.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceGate.cs:251** (tool-service) - A document switch that arrives while the FIRST start is in flight is dropped permanently. FollowDocument returns at line 251 when _starting is true, and the comment at 252-253 ('A start already in flight will attach to whatever is active when it runs') is only true up to the moment SwSession.Attach reads the active document; SwScope.Open then walks the component tree, which docs/review-backlog.md:422 records as tens of seconds on a large assembly. A switch during that walk leaves the gate attached to the old document with no event left to correct it, which is exactly finding 1's symptom, until the engineer switches documents again.
  - Fix: Record the last requested active path when FollowDocument bails on _starting, and re-evaluate it at the end of StartCore (after _service is published): if the newly attached DocumentPath differs from the recorded request, call FollowDocument again. Add a test: schedule captured manually, EnsureStarted, FollowDocument('other') while the start is pending, run the pending start (which attaches to the old path), and assert a second start follows and the gate ends on 'other'.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ToolServiceWiringTests.cs:385** (tool-service) - FollowDocumentStartsNothingBeforeTheFirstService is near-tautological: it sets DocumentOpen = false, so Starts stays 0 even for an implementation that wrongly schedules StartCore from FollowDocument - StartCore would bail on _documentAvailable() and the assertions would still pass. The test's own docstring says the property under test is 'starting is EnsureStarted's job', which it does not actually pin.
  - Fix: Set DocumentOpen = true (leave the gate with no service) and assert Starts == 0 and gate.GeneralChatBridge is null. That version fails if FollowDocument ever schedules a start of its own.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/ToolService/ToolServiceGate.cs:279** (tool-service) - The busy branch logs and probes on every call. SOLIDWORKS raises ActiveDocChangeNotify on each window/tab activation, so while one long review turn runs against document A and the engineer works in B, each activation writes another identical 'not re-attaching to B: a turn is running against A' line into the tool-service log and re-runs the HTTP busy probe per open chat. The test only exercises a single call (Assert.Single(LogLines)), so the repetition is unpinned.
  - Fix: Remember the last (attached path, active path) pair the line was written for and skip the log - and, if cheap, the probe - when it is unchanged; extend ABusyBridgeIsNotRestartedAndTheLogSaysWhyNamingBothDocuments to call FollowDocument three times with the same active path and still assert Assert.Single(LogLines).
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:173** (tool-service) - RunInProgress is a new public member with no test of its own; every gate test uses GateWorld's Busy flag, so nothing asserts that the half of the add-in wiring that reads a remodel run actually reports one. If _busy's meaning drifts (it is also the plan/start refusal flag, set at 471/752 and cleared at 612/800), the tool service would restart underneath a running remodel and nothing would fail.
  - Fix: Add ~5 lines to RemodelHostTests beside ASecondStartWhileTheRunIsInFlightIsRefusedAsRunInProgress: assert host.RunInProgress is false before the start, true while the run is in flight, and false once it completes. Same for ToolServiceHost.WriteLog if it is reachable from a test seam.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/SwReviewAddIn.cs:862** (tool-service) - Nothing pins the add-in-side wiring itself: that OnActiveDocumentChanged calls FollowDocument(CurrentDocument()?.Path) after EnsureStarted(), and that busy is the OR of the two hosts. ToolServiceWiringTests' header explains why SwReviewAddIn cannot be tested directly, so this matches existing precedent - but the busy lambda at 735-736 is new logic, and a null-guard or an inverted OR there would silently disable the whole feature with a green suite.
  - Fix: Either accept it explicitly (precedent), or lift the busy expression into a small static helper on the gate side (e.g. ToolServiceGate.Busy(Func<bool> review, Func<bool> remodel)) that a test can drive with each combination of null/true/false.
- **docs/pane-findings-2026-09-16.md:29** (tool-service) - The brief (item A5) requires both findings to gain a 'Fixed' line; only finding 2 has one (line 90). Finding 1 - the finding Part B implements - still reads as open, and lines 16 and 29 still quote the superseded error wording with no note that the message now names the attached document.
  - Fix: Add a 'Fixed.' paragraph under finding 1 naming ToolServiceGate.FollowDocument, the SwReviewAddIn.OnActiveDocumentChanged call site and DocumentPresenceDispatcher.DocumentClosedError(string), and quoting the new sentence. If the docs update is another agent's task in this stage, hand it the wording rather than editing the file.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:925** (check-rms) - The command still says nothing about which exception store it graded, and after this change that omission can hide a real substitution: with `--out`, a carried-forward exceptions.json in the run folder wins over exceptions.json beside the package (checks/rms/run.py:583), so waivers an engineer accepted against the package can silently stop applying because an unrelated sibling run of the same design_id was copied in first. Neither the JSON payload nor the human lines carry run.exceptions_carried_forward, although check.json and the route's CheckResult both do. Before this change the two paths were always the same file, so the precedence had no observable consequence.
  - Fix: Add "exceptions_carried_forward": {"from_run": ..., "count": ..., "reason": ...} to the payload and one human line next to `session:`/`report:` naming the store that was graded ("carried forward N exception(s) from <run>", or the reason when nothing was carried). Two lines, matching what check.json already records, and it makes the new precedence visible instead of inferred.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/run.py:583** (check-rms) - _store_to_grade_against introduces a precedence rule (run folder first, package second) but no test exercises the case where both files exist. test_rms_run.py:657 covers carried-only and test_rms_run.py:680 covers package-only. The conflicting case - the one where an engineer's package waivers are shadowed by a carried copy - is the only case where the rule is a decision rather than a tautology, and it is unpinned.
  - Fix: Add a case in TestCarryForward: the package holds a store waiving rule A, an earlier sibling run holds a store waiving rule B, run with out_dir under that run root, then assert B is silenced, A is not, and the package's file is still byte-for-byte what it was. That is the sentence the docstring claims; pin it.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:1925** (check-rms) - The move of the run root from the package's parent to `--out`'s parent is tested only in the positive direction. Nothing asserts what the finding actually asked for: that a folder sitting beside the PACKAGE, holding the same design_id and an exceptions.json, is no longer read as an earlier run. That is the hazard docs/pane-findings-2026-09-16.md and docs/review-backlog.md:773 named (a foreign store copied in from an arbitrary sibling of an arbitrary --package directory), and it is now closed by construction with no test holding it closed.
  - Fix: Add a case: build the package under tmp_path/pkgroot/pkg with a same-design sibling tmp_path/pkgroot/other holding an exceptions.json, run with --out tmp_path/runs/r1, and assert nothing was carried (check.json's exceptions_carried_forward.from_run is None, no exceptions.json in the run folder) and the finding is not waived.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/chat/server.py:639** (check-rms) - The CLI can now create folders that is_check_folder (checks/rms/run.py:777) accepts but check_result cannot read: read_rms_check needs only check.json plus the session it names, while check_result does load_package(check_dir), and a `--out` run folder holds no package.json. Since the new guidance encourages putting run folders under a run root whose siblings are earlier checks, an engineer who points --out under the pane server's --run-root makes GET /checks/{check_id} answer that folder with InvalidPackage instead of the check it holds, and POST /sessions treats it as a claimable check folder. POST /checks/rms itself is unaffected (verified).
  - Fix: Either record the package directory in check.json and have read_rms_check carry it on RmsCheckRun.package_dir so check_result loads the package the check actually graded (one field plus a back-compatible read), or state in specs/003-resilient-modeling/contracts/cli.md that --out must not name a folder under a running pane's run root. The first is the explicit fix; the second is free but leaves a trap.
- **benchmarks/README.md:67** (check-rms) - Two more documented invocations are now usage errors: benchmarks/README.md:67 (`swreview check rms --package ../benchmarks/packages/<name>/native --scope part`) and benchmarks/native/rms-part/RECIPE.md:257 and :262. These are the workstation recipes an engineer follows with SOLIDWORKS open, so they fail at the least convenient moment.
  - Fix: Add `--out <run dir>` to all three lines, matching the form specs/003-resilient-modeling/quickstart.md now uses for the native package (`--out $runs\native\run-1`). Outside the brief's named files, so raise it with the owner before editing.
- **README.md:80** (check-rms) - The quick-reference line still reads `swreview check rms --package <package dir>           # findings, coverage, session.json, report.md`. That invocation now exits 2 (missing required option), and the comment still describes files landing beside the package. The brief did not name README.md, so the implementer was right not to touch it, but it is now the most visible wrong instruction in the repo.
  - Fix: Owner call: either add README.md to this round's scope and make it `swreview check rms --package <package dir> --out <run dir>   # findings, coverage; run folder holds session.json, report.md, check.json`, or leave it and carry it as a known-stale line. Recommend fixing it - one line, zero risk, and it is the line an engineer copies.
- **specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/cli.md:42** (check-rms) - Feature 001's CLI contract still documents `swreview check rms` with no `--out` and says "it writes `session.json` and `report.md` into the package directory". It now directly contradicts specs/003-resilient-modeling/contracts/cli.md:7. The brief named only the 003 contract, so this is a scope question rather than an implementer error, but two contracts in the tree now describe the same command incompatibly.
  - Fix: Ask the owner: (A) update the 001 row to match 003 - one row, no code, keeps the contracts consistent; (B) leave it, on the rule that a feature's contract is frozen at its feature, and accept that 001 is superseded by 003 for this command; (C) replace the 001 row with a pointer to 003's. Recommend A - the contracts are read as current documentation here, and a frozen-but-wrong row costs more than the edit.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:669** (check-rms) - `_context_for(package_dir, *, exceptions: bool = False)`: the `exceptions=True` branch now has no caller anywhere. All four in-tree call sites (cli.py:756, 779, 805, 826) use the default, and the only `exceptions=True` caller in the repo was `rms_run` in test_cli_exceptions_accept_rms.py, which this change deleted. Ten lines of docstring and two lines of body are now dead. CLAUDE.md section 3 asks for orphans created by a change to be removed.
  - Fix: Drop the parameter and the `load_exceptions`/`refresh` lines, simplifying to `return build_context(load_package(package_dir))`, and drop the now-unused `load_exceptions` import if nothing else in cli.py uses it. Alternative (do nothing): harmless but leaves a documented option nothing exercises, which is exactly the kind of thing that later gets wired up wrongly. Recommend removing it, but flagging it to the owner first since it is a src edit slightly outside the brief's letter.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:914** (check-rms) - Nothing refuses `--out` pointing at the package directory itself. `swreview check rms --package pkg --out pkg` writes `session.json`, `report.md` and `check.json` into `pkg` and sets `run_root` to the package's parent - exactly the pre-fix behaviour, including against a golden fixture. The brief's guarantee ("the package directory is never written") is therefore conditional on the operator, and no test covers the case.
  - Fix: Three options. (A) Do nothing: it is an explicit request, and `--out` being required already removes the accidental path that caused the defect. (B) Refuse when `out_dir == Path(package).resolve()` with a one-line message, plus one test - three lines, explicit, and makes the contract sentence unconditional. (C) Refuse only when the package is under `tests/golden/fixtures` - narrow and magic, I would not. Recommend B if you want the contract to read as an absolute, A if you would rather not grow the command; either way add a test pinning the chosen behaviour, because right now it is unstated.
- **reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli_exceptions_accept_rms.py:366** (check-rms) - Two assertions were weakened from `assert not (run_dir / REPORT_FILE_NAME).exists()` to a byte-comparison against the report the check itself wrote (also at line 396). The change is forced - `rms_run` now goes through `run_rms_check`, which writes a report - but a byte-compare no longer detects a re-render that happens to produce identical bytes, which is the likely shape of "accept-rms accepted nothing and re-rendered anyway".
  - Fix: Keep the byte-compare and add `st_mtime_ns` (captured before the call) to the assertion, or assert on the report file's stat only. Both tests already assert `body["report_file"] is None`, which carries most of the weight, so this is a small tightening rather than a hole - but the lens asked whether each assertion would still fail for the behaviour it guards, and this pair would not.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/checks/rms/run.py:370** (check-rms) - The comment above `out.mkdir` claims the placement means "a run that is refused for its arguments leaves no folder behind". True for the two refusals above it, but `UnreadableExceptionsError` from the carry-forward and `RmsRunError` from `_dispatch` both fire after the mkdir, so a refused run can leave an empty `out` (and, for the dispatch failure, a carried-forward `exceptions.json`). Nothing asserts what `out` holds after a refusal; `test_check_rms_reports_a_rule_that_cannot_grade_as_an_error_not_a_traceback` now creates one and checks nothing about it.
  - Fix: Add one assertion to that existing test - after the exit-1, `out` either does not exist or holds no `session.json`/`report.md`/`check.json` - pinning whichever is the intended answer, and reword the comment to "after the two argument refusals" so it stops implying more than it delivers. No implementation change needed if a bare empty folder is acceptable; if it is not, move the mkdir down to just before `_carry_forward` - but the carry-forward needs it, so an empty folder after a dispatch failure is unavoidable and should simply be stated.
- **reviewer/src/swreview/cli.py:949** (check-rms) - The human output gained `session: <path>` and `report: <path>` but not `check: <path>`, while the JSON payload names all three (`session_file`, `report_file`, `check_file`). An engineer reading the terminal is told about two of the three files the run just wrote, and `check.json` is the one `read_rms_check`/`GET /checks/{id}` answers from.
  - Fix: Add `lines.append(f"check: {run.check_file}")` and extend `test_check_rms_human_output_names_the_run_folder` to assert it - two lines, keeps the two output shapes saying the same thing. Alternative: drop to a single `run folder: <out_dir>` line and let the payload carry the filenames, which is arguably cleaner but changes the line the quickstart now describes. Recommend adding the third line.

## Proxy option B round (2026-09-16)

- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:428** (serving) - `ServeThePagesTheWayTheAddInDoes` is named for serving "the way the add-in does", and the constraint was that the helpers "serve the same way so the page tests exercise the real path". It shares the `PageFileServer` class and the `TaskPaneControl.PageResourceFilter` constant, but its handler body is a second, independent implementation: it does not call `BackendProxyHandler.TryServe` first, it does not take a deferral, it does not guard the empty-reason phrase, and it has no try/catch. So every page test in the assembly exercises the file server but not the handler ordering, the deferral discipline, or the UI-thread/off-thread split that `TaskPaneControl.ServePageResource` actually implements. A regression in the pane's ordering (proxy-first) would leave all ten page-test files green; only `BackendProxyPageTests.TheShippedPaneServesItsOwnPagesAndItsBackendCallsThroughOneHandler` would catch it. Two handler bodies for one documented rule is also a DRY violation of exactly the kind the decision text called out.
  - Fix: Extract the wiring from `TaskPaneControl.AttachPageAsync` + `ServePageResource` into one internal static entry point - e.g. `TaskPaneControl.ServeOrigin(CoreWebView2 core, CoreWebView2Environment environment, BackendProxyHandler? proxy, PageFileServer files)` that registers the filter and installs the real handler - and have both the pane and `ServeThePagesTheWayTheAddInDoes` call it, the helper passing a proxy over a null endpoint (which already returns null outside the prefix). One body, one filter, one ordering, and the page tests then genuinely exercise the shipped path. If that is judged too large a change now, at minimum wrap the helper's handler body in a try/catch so an exception cannot escape a WebView2 event on the STA loop and take the test host with it, and rename the method to say what it really does.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Remodel/RemodelHost.cs:386** (serving) - `RemodelHost.SendInit` still sends `{ origin: endpoint.Origin }` - i.e. the literal `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` (BackendProcess.cs:52) - together with `token`, while `ReviewHost.cs:533` and `ModelCheckHost.cs:284` now send `BackendProxy.PageOrigin`. The Remodel page cannot in fact reach loopback (its CSP is now `connect-src 'self'`, and `remodel.js` contains no fetch at all, both verified), so this is not a live escape. But it hands a renderer a loopback URL and a bearer token it has no use for, and it leaves one of the four hosts on the pre-proxy contract: the day someone adds a fetch to the Remodel page, or relaxes that one CSP, the failure is the exact web-filter interstitial this whole change exists to remove. docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:223 states the rule as "`init.backend.origin` is now `BackendProxy.PageOrigin`" and its Where column silently omits RemodelHost; section 2 does qualify it, so the two halves of the doc disagree about whether the rule is universal.
  - Fix: Send `BackendProxy.PageOrigin` from `RemodelHost.SendInit` as the other two hosts do, so all four pages are given the same, uniform, unusable-off-origin value, and add the assertion to `RemodelPageContractTests` alongside the ones in `ModelCheckHostTests`/`PageMessageTests`. If the Remodel page genuinely needs neither field, dropping `origin` (and `token`) from its init payload is the stronger version - explicit over clever - but that is a contract change, so confirm before doing it. Either way, make docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:223 say "all four hosts" or name the exception.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:146** (serving) - The XML doc on this class (lines 30-34) says "every segment is decoded first and then refused if it is empty, `.`, `..`, or carries a separator", but the code decodes the entire `AbsolutePath` and only then splits on '/'. The behaviour is actually safe - and arguably safer than the documented order, because `%2f` becomes a real boundary and the resulting `..` segment is then refused - but the consequence is that the "carries a separator" clause can only ever fire for a backslash, never for a forward slash, since a decoded '/' has already become a split point. A reader auditing this class against its own comment will conclude the forward-slash case is covered by the segment check when it is covered by the split. On the one line in the product that decides whether the pane can read the rest of the disk, the comment and the code should describe the same mechanism.
  - Fix: Reword the comment to state what the code does: the whole path is unescaped once, then split, so a decoded '/' becomes a segment boundary (and a '..' it produces is caught by the segment check), while a decoded '\\', ':' or wildcard is caught by `Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars()`. No code change; `TraversalOutOfTheWebFolderIsRefused` already pins both forms. Optionally add the one case the theory is missing - a double-encoded `%252e%252e%252f` - so the "decode exactly once" property is pinned rather than assumed.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:86** (serving) - `_root = Path.GetFullPath(webFolder).TrimEnd(Path.DirectorySeparatorChar)` degrades badly for a drive-root web folder. `PageFileServer("C:\\")` yields `_root == "C:"`, after which `Path.Combine("C:", "Windows", "win.ini")` produces the drive-relative path `C:Windows\win.ini`, `Path.GetFullPath` resolves it against the process's current directory on C:, and the containment check `candidate.StartsWith("C:" + '\\')` passes for every file on the volume. `TaskPaneOptions.WebFolder` defaults to `<assembly dir>\\web` so this is unreachable in the product today, but it is the one input that turns the containment gate into a no-op, and `WebFolder` is a public settable property. Separately, `TrimEnd` only strips `Path.DirectorySeparatorChar`, not `AltDirectorySeparatorChar`.
  - Fix: Guard the root in the constructor: after `Path.GetFullPath`, reject a path equal to its own `Path.GetPathRoot` (and a path that does not exist as a directory) with an `ArgumentException` naming the parameter, rather than trimming into a drive-relative spelling. Add two constructor tests - a drive root and a null/whitespace folder - since neither is covered today. Cheap, no effect on the shipping path, and it closes the only input that disarms the second traversal gate.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:761** (serving) - `TaskPaneControl.ReadRequestContent` and `BackendProxyHandler.Read` (BackendProxyHandler.cs, the private `Read(Stream?)`) are the same nine lines implementing the same rule - copy the stream into a MemoryStream, and return null rather than an empty array when there are no bytes - and the second is applied to a MemoryStream that the first just built from the same bytes. So every proxied request with a body is copied twice and the "empty means null" rule is stated in two places that must agree for `ProxiedRequest.Body == null` to keep meaning "no body". `BackendProxyHandlerTests` pins the rule on one of them only.
  - Fix: Give `BackendProxyHandler.TryServe` a `byte[]?` body overload (the pane already has the bytes on the UI thread and must read them there), keep the `Stream?` overload delegating to it for the tests that exercise it that way, and delete `TaskPaneControl.ReadRequestContent`'s duplicate rule by making it the single definition. That removes one full copy of every POST body per request and leaves one place where "empty means no body" is decided.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:323** (serving) - `_files = new PageFileServer(_options.WebFolder)` snapshots the web folder at construction, while `_proxy` is built over lazy callbacks (`() => _options.Backend()`, `request => _options.ProxySend(request)`) precisely so the options can change after the pane is built. `WebFolder` is a public `get; set;` on `TaskPaneOptions`, so a caller that sets it after constructing the control gets a file server pointed at the old folder with no error - the pane simply serves 404s and the tab never finishes loading. `BackendProxyPageTests.WithPane` happens to set it in the object initializer, so nothing catches this.
  - Fix: Pick one discipline and make it explicit. Either read the folder lazily like the other two (`new PageFileServer(() => _options.WebFolder)` or construct the server inside `ServePageResource`), or - better, since the folder genuinely never changes at runtime - move `WebFolder` to a constructor parameter of `TaskPaneOptions` / make it `{ get; }` with an init-time value, so "set after construction" stops being expressible. The second is smaller and matches how the folder is actually used.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:69** (serving) - Responses carry only `Content-Type` and `Cache-Control: no-store`. There is no `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. The closed extension list makes MIME confusion unlikely today, but the pane now serves `.svg` (which renderers will treat as a document) and `.json`, and the folder-mapping sandbox that used to decide content types is gone - this class is the only thing that types a response now. One header is a smaller cost than reasoning about sniffing every time a new extension is proposed.
  - Fix: Append `\r\nX-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` to both the 200 and the 404 header strings, and extend `APageFileIsServedWithItsContentTypeAndNoStore` and `AssertNotFound` to assert it. Two lines, no behavioural risk. Do nothing is defensible given the closed list - flagging it because the contract in pane-host-messages.md now enumerates the response rules and this is the one it does not mention.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:181** (serving) - `Path.GetFullPath` canonicalizes `..`, `.` and short names but does not resolve NTFS reparse points, so a junction or symlink placed inside the web folder that points outside it passes the `StartsWith(_root + separator)` check and the file outside is served. The class doc calls the containment check "the line that decides whether the pane can read the rest of the disk", which slightly overstates what `GetFullPath` guarantees. Exploiting it requires write access to the add-in's install folder, at which point the attacker can replace the DLL - so the practical risk is near zero and "do nothing" is a reasonable answer.
  - Fix: Either (a) accept it and soften the class comment to say the check is textual containment of the canonical path, not reparse-point resolution, so the next reader is not misled about the guarantee; or (b) resolve the final path with `new FileInfo(candidate).Directory.FullName` plus a `FileAttributes.ReparsePoint` check on each resolved component before serving. My recommendation is (a): (b) adds a syscall per resource on the tab-load path to defend against an attacker who already owns the install directory, which is over-engineering for this threat model.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/BackendProxy.cs:19** (serving) - The class remarks still say WebView2 "raises `WebResourceRequested` for <see cref="ResourceFilter"/>", but `ResourceFilter` was deleted in this change - the filter is now `TaskPaneControl.PageResourceFilter`. The cref resolves to nothing (the build is at 0 warnings only because XML doc generation is off, so the compiler never checks it). This is the same failure mode the design doc gave as the reason for deleting the property: "a property documented as 'the filter WebView2 is given' that is not the filter WebView2 is given is a lie the next reader would act on". The property went; the sentence pointing at it stayed.
  - Fix: Change the cref to `<see cref="TaskPaneControl.PageResourceFilter"/>` and adjust the sentence: the filter is now the whole origin, not the prefix, and the same handler serves the page files. While there, consider turning on `<GenerateDocumentationFile>` for SwReview.AddIn so CS1574 catches the next one - the codebase leans heavily on these comments.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:107** (serving) - The `catch (IOException)` and `catch (UnauthorizedAccessException)` -> 404 branches have no test. The comment names a reachable scenario ("a build copying over it is the realistic one"), and this is the only place where a file that passed `File.Exists` still fails to be served - i.e. the one path where a live pane shows a blank tab for a file that is genuinely there. Deleting both catches would fail no test; the exception would instead escape into `ServePageResource`'s blanket catch and leave the request unanswered, which is a materially different outcome from a 404.
  - Fix: Two tests, both cheap and deterministic. (1) Hold `web/Review/ReviewPage/app.js` open with `new FileStream(path, FileMode.Open, FileAccess.Read, FileShare.None)`, then assert `Serve(Origin + "/Review/ReviewPage/app.js")` is the same 404 as every other refusal (reuse `AssertNotFound` plus the `EveryRefusalLooksTheSame` comparison). (2) While you are there, consider widening the catch - `File.ReadAllBytes` can also throw `SecurityException` and `OutOfMemoryException`, neither of which is currently answered, and the class's stated contract is that it "always answers".
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:323** (serving) - `TaskPaneOptions.WebFolder` is a public settable property (line 88) but `TaskPaneControl` captures it once in the constructor (`_files = new PageFileServer(_options.WebFolder)`), while the two options beside it, `Backend` and `ProxySend`, are read late through callbacks on line 322. Setting `WebFolder` after the control is constructed therefore has no effect at all, silently. That matters more than the usual mutable-option smell because the property is now documented as "the boundary a page request may not escape" (lines 85-87): a caller who set it would believe they had moved the security boundary. `BackendProxyPageTests.WithPane` happens to set it in the initializer, which is why nothing catches this.
  - Fix: Pick one and be explicit. Cheapest: make `WebFolder` get-only (constructor or initializer-only setter) so the compiler enforces what the code already assumes. Alternative, consistent with `Backend`/`ProxySend`: construct the `PageFileServer` lazily in `AttachPageAsync` from `_options.WebFolder`. If you keep it as is, at minimum document that it is read once, in the constructor. My recommendation is get-only: it is the explicit option and it costs one keyword.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:441** (serving) - `ServeThePagesTheWayTheAddInDoes` claims in its own doc comment to serve "exactly as `TaskPaneControl.AttachPageAsync` does", and it diverges in two ways. It drops production's empty-reason normalization (`string.IsNullOrEmpty(answer.Reason) ? "OK" : answer.Reason`, TaskPaneControl.cs:722-723), and it omits `BackendProxyHandler.TryServe` entirely - so a page test that ever fetched `/__backend/...` would get `PageFileServer`'s plain-text 404 rather than the proxy's `{error_class, message, retryable}` 503, which is a different shape from the one the pages parse. Nothing currently does (ModelCheckPageTests stubs `window.fetch`), so this is a latent trap for the next page test rather than a live failure.
  - Fix: Either narrow the claim - say it serves the page FILES the way the add-in does, and that `/__backend` is not wired here because no page test fetches - or close the gap by giving the helper a `BackendProxyHandler` over a fake transport and the same `TryServe(...) ?? files.Serve(...)` chain. I would narrow the claim: the helper is a test harness and adding a second server to it buys nothing until a page test needs one. Also use the same empty-reason normalization, so the two sites cannot disagree about what an empty reason means.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/PageFileServerTests.cs:167** (serving) - Two of the seven `TraversalOutOfTheWebFolderIsRefused` cases carry no signal. I confirmed on this machine's .NET Framework 4.8 that `Uri` collapses `/../secret.html` and `/Review/../../secret.html` to `/secret.html` before `PageFileServer` sees them, so those two pass because `web\secret.html` does not exist - they would still pass with the entire segment rule at lines 153-161 deleted. The docstring acknowledges the clamping, but the cases sit in a theory named "traversal ... is refused" and are counted toward the 58. The `Assert.DoesNotContain("outside", ...)` is likewise decorative: the 404 body is the constant "Not found.", so it can only fire if the 200 path is taken, which the status assertion already caught. (The other five cases - `%2e%2e`, `..%2f`, `%5C` - are genuinely load-bearing; I verified `Uri` leaves all three escaped.)
  - Fix: Keep the two clamped cases as regression guards but move them into their own small test named for what they actually assert - that a browser-normalized `..` lands on a path that is simply not there - so the traversal theory contains only cases that exercise the rule. And drop or strengthen the `DoesNotContain("outside")` assertion, which cannot fail as written.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:193** (serving) - The containment check and the segment rule are each individually redundant: I traced every refusal case and each one is caught by both. Delete `candidate.StartsWith(_root + Path.DirectorySeparatorChar, ...)` and the suite stays green (the `..` segment rule catches it); delete the `..` segment rule and the suite stays green (containment catches it). That is deliberate belt-and-braces and I would keep both - but it means a refactor that removes either one silently loses half the defence, and the `+ Path.DirectorySeparatorChar` in particular is the classic prefix off-by-one (a sibling folder `web2` next to `web`) that nothing pins.
  - Fix: One test that reaches the containment check with the segment rule intact: construct a `PageFileServer` over `<root>/web` with a real `<root>/web2/evil.html` beside it, and assert `Serve(Origin + "/%2e%2e/web2/evil.html")` is a 404 that does not contain the file's bytes. That case survives `Uri` canonicalization (verified), is refused by the segment rule today, and would be the test that fails if someone drops the separator from line 193. Cheap, and it converts "defence in depth" from a comment into something the suite enforces.
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/pane-host-messages.md:79** (origin-csp) - The contract never states what `init.backend.origin` now is. The Review row (line 79) and the Model check row (line 166) both still read `{backend: {port, origin}, token, ...}` with no value, and line 97 says only "using the token and origin from `init`". The single most consequential change of this round - `origin` stopped being `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` and became `https://swreview.invalid/__backend` - is stated only in docs/pane-backend-proxy.md and in two C# comments. A page author working from the contract alone (which is what the contract is for, and what ReviewPageContractTests reads as data) would rebuild a loopback URL and land back on the failure in section 1.
  - Fix: Spell the value out in both `ready` rows - `backend: {port, origin: "https://swreview.invalid/__backend"}` - and add one sentence under the new "Every page resource is served by the host" section saying the port is carried for the pane's own display and diagnostics only and that no page builds a URL from it. Then have ReviewPageContractTests assert the literal `https://swreview.invalid/__backend` appears in the contract and in the host's init, so the two cannot drift again.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageEventStreamTests.cs:264** (origin-csp) - The Review page's event-stream tests still boot the page from `backend = new { port = 51999, origin = "http://127.0.0.1:51999" }` - an `init` ReviewHost can no longer produce. PageMessageTests.cs and ModelCheckPageTests.cs were both converted in this round; this fixture was missed. It is the largest remaining page-test corpus running against a shape that does not exist, so it would keep passing if `backendUrl()` regressed to building loopback URLs, and it is a copy-paste source for the next page test.
  - Fix: Change it to `origin = "https://swreview.invalid/__backend"` to match the host, and consider asserting once (as PageMessageTests now does) that no page fixture's init origin contains `127.0.0.1`.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/TaskPaneControl.cs:730** (origin-csp) - `ServePageResource` leaves the request unanswered on an unexpected exception, and the comment's justification ("WebView2 then tries the request itself and fails") is weaker now than it was under the folder mapping. With no mapping behind the handler, "tries it itself" means WebView2 issues a real request for `https://swreview.invalid/...`: on the pilot workstation - the one whose filter answers browser HTTP with its own content, which is the premise of this entire document - anything that resolver or filter returns would be loaded on the page's own origin, under `script-src 'self'`. `.invalid` is reserved and will not resolve on a correct resolver, so this is narrow, but it is the one path where the round's "every resource on this origin is served by the host" claim does not hold.
  - Fix: In the catch, make one best-effort attempt to assign a plain 404 (the same body/headers PageFileServer.NotFound produces) inside its own try/catch, so a request that reaches this handler is never handed back to the network stack. Add a test that forces `Serve`/`TryServe` to throw and asserts a response was assigned.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:29** (origin-csp) - The stated path rule is not the implemented one. The XML summary (line 29) and docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:240-243 both say "every segment is decoded first and then refused if it ... carries a separator". The code at line 141 does the reverse: it decodes the whole `AbsolutePath` with `Uri.UnescapeDataString` and then splits on '/', so an encoded `%2f` becomes a real separator rather than being refused as one. The outcome is still safe - the segments a decoded traversal produces are `..` or empty and are refused, and PageFileServerTests pins `/..%2f..%2fsecret.html` - but the rule a future maintainer will reason from is not the rule the code enforces, and this is the line that decides whether the pane can read the rest of the disk.
  - Fix: Either make the code match the doc (split on '/' first, then `Uri.UnescapeDataString` each segment and refuse any that still contains a separator), which is the stricter and more explicit reading, or reword both the summary and the doc to describe what happens: the path is decoded whole, split, and each resulting segment refused if empty, `.`, `..`, or carrying an invalid file-name character. The first is preferable - it makes `%2f` a refusal rather than a separator - and PageFileServerTests already has the cases to prove it either way.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/PageFileServerTests.cs:283** (origin-csp) - The "every file the four shipped pages load" theory is a hand-kept list of 13 InlineData rows and it is already incomplete: `Terminal/TerminalPage/term.css`, `Terminal/TerminalPage/vendor/addon-fit.js`, `Model/ModelCheckPage/check.css` and `Remodel/RemodelPage/remodel.css` are all loaded by their pages and none is on it. docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:220 describes this test as covering "every file the four shipped pages load", which it does not. The risk the closed extension list created is exactly this: a page that adds an asset with an extension outside the list (`.woff`, `.gif`, `.webmanifest`, a `.map`) gets a silent 404 and a half-loaded tab, which under the old folder mapping could not happen.
  - Fix: Replace the InlineData list with an enumeration of the built `web/` folder: every file on disk must either serve 200 with the expected content type or appear on a short, named 404 allow-list (`vendor/LICENSES.md` today). That makes adding an unserveable asset a red test instead of a broken tab, and removes a list that has to be kept by hand.
- **docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:143** (origin-csp) - Two stale claims in the doc that is the handoff's source of truth. Line 143 says `BackendProxyTests.cs` has "13 cases"; it now has 18 (6 `[Fact]` plus 12 `[InlineData]`), because this round removed the `ResourceFilter` cases and added `TheOneResourceFilterCoversThePrefixAndThePagesThemselves`. Line 224 says the four CSPs are "byte-identical (the page contract tests assert that)"; the four tests that check it (ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:179, ModelCheckPageInjectionTests.cs:66, RemodelPageContractTests.cs:204, TerminalPageContractTests.cs:148) all compare `Normalize(expected)` to `Normalize(actual)`, which collapses whitespace and re-joins directives - so they assert equivalence, not byte-identity. (I verified independently that all four page CSPs are in fact byte-for-byte equal to the contract's line 69 today; the doc's claim is true, the test cited as proving it does not prove it.) Line 146's "58 cases" for PageFileServerTests is correct (7 + 51).
  - Fix: Correct the count on line 143, and either soften line 224 to "the page contract tests assert it up to whitespace" or - better, and what the decision's "byte-identical" constraint actually asks for - drop `Normalize` from the four assertions so they compare the raw strings.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/RemodelPageContractTests.cs:939** (origin-csp) - `ContractCsp()` and `Normalize()` are duplicated verbatim in four test files - RemodelPageContractTests.cs:914/939, ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:232/246, ModelCheckPageInjectionTests.cs:136 and TerminalPageContractTests.cs:604-609 - each re-parsing the same CSP out of the same contract file with its own regex (and the regexes already differ: some allow `/?>`, some do not). This round is what made that duplication load-bearing, because the CSP was changed in all four pages at once and one page silently keeping the old policy is precisely the failure these tests exist to catch. Pre-existing, so it is a cleanup rather than a regression.
  - Fix: Move `ContractCsp()` and the comparison into one shared helper next to `ReviewPageFiles.ReadContract` (e.g. `PageContracts.AssertShipsContractCsp(html, which)`), and have all four call it. One regex, one normalization decision, one place to change when the policy changes again.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:104** (origin-csp) - Page responses carry `Content-Type` and `Cache-Control: no-store` but no `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. The folder mapping used to be the thing deciding content types; now the pane does, from a closed extension list, and a served file whose bytes disagree with its extension can still be sniffed by the renderer. Low severity - the extension list is closed, everything under `web/` is shipped by the add-in, and `default-src 'none'` constrains what a sniffed type could become - but it is one header, it is free, and it is the kind of thing the mapping used to make moot.
  - Fix: Append `\r\nX-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` to the 200 and the 404 header strings, and add it to the contract's file-server rules list (pane-host-messages.md, the paragraph that already names `Cache-Control: no-store`) so the two stay together. One assertion in PageFileServerTests pins it.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/PageFileServerTests.cs:297** (origin-csp) - `EveryFileTheShippedPagesLoadIsServed` does not cover every file the shipped pages load, though its own docstring (line 277-280) and docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:220 both say it does. The four index.html files reference 17 resources; the theory has 13. Missing: `/Terminal/TerminalPage/term.css` (Terminal index.html:8), `/Terminal/TerminalPage/vendor/addon-fit.js` (:67), `/Model/ModelCheckPage/check.css` (:7), `/Remodel/RemodelPage/remodel.css` (:7). All four are .css/.js in directories the theory already reaches, so they pass today — but the folder mapping that used to answer them is gone and the test that is supposed to be the safety net for 'a single 404 here is a blank tab' has holes in exactly the two pages (Terminal, Remodel) that get the least end-to-end coverage.
  - Fix: Add the four `[InlineData]` rows. Cheap, and it makes the docstring and the doc true. Optionally derive the list by regexing `src=`/`href=` out of the four index.html files instead of hand-listing, which would make the test self-maintaining — but that is the more-engineered option and hand-listing 17 rows is defensible.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/BackendProxyPageTests.cs:120** (origin-csp) - The confound that already bit this test once is removed by construction but never asserted. `TheFolderMappedVirtualHostRaisesNoWebResourceRequestedForAFetchTheCspAllows` exists because the original version fetched from the shipped Review page, whose CSP then lacked `'self'`, so the renderer refused the call before a request existed and the zero-event result proved the policy rather than the platform (docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:191-195). The fix was to serve a scratch page built with `WriteScratchPage("connect-src 'self'")` — but the test asserts only `Failed to fetch` and `seen.Count == 0`, both of which a CSP refusal produces just as happily. Nothing in the test body fails if `ScratchPageHtml` (line 395) is later edited and the confound returns; it would go on passing while proving nothing. This is the one test in the file whose conclusion the suite cannot re-derive.
  - Fix: Install the `securitypolicyviolation` listener before the fetch — the pattern is already written 180 lines down in `ThePagesCspRefusesLoopbackBeforeAnyNetworkCallIsMade` (line 285-300) — and assert `window.__violation === null` alongside the existing two assertions. Then the test states its own precondition instead of inheriting it from a helper.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/BackendProxyPageTests.cs:309** (origin-csp) - Tautological assertion. `Assert.Empty(fake.Requests)` in `ThePagesCspRefusesLoopbackBeforeAnyNetworkCallIsMade` cannot fail for the reason its placement implies. `http://127.0.0.1:1/health` is off the page origin, so it falls outside `TaskPaneControl.PageResourceFilter` (`https://swreview.invalid/*`) and can never reach `ProxySend` — with `connect-src 'self'`, with `connect-src *`, or with no CSP at all. It reads as corroboration of the CSP claim and is independent of it. The real claim is carried entirely by the two `Assert.Contains` on the violation event above it.
  - Fix: Either drop the line, or keep it and say in the comment what it actually pins — that a loopback URL is outside the filter as well as outside the policy, which is a second independent fact and worth one sentence rather than an assertion that looks like the conclusion.
- **specs/002-task-pane-assistant/contracts/pane-host-messages.md:79** (origin-csp) - The contract never says what `init.backend.origin` holds — the single payload field this decision changed. Both `ready` rows (line 79 Review, line 166 Model check) and specs/004-resilient-remodeler/contracts/pane-remodel-messages.md:29 still print `{backend: {port, origin}, ...}` with no definition. The new 'Every page resource is served by the host' section documents the serving side thoroughly but stops short of the field the pages read. A reader coming to the contract fresh gets `origin` from its history — the loopback origin it used to be — and nothing catches that: ModelCheckHostTests.cs:62 and PageMessageTests.cs:57 assert a hardcoded literal, not a value read out of the contract, which is how RemodelHost.cs:386 came to disagree with the other two hosts without any test noticing.
  - Fix: One sentence in the new section: `init.backend.origin` is `https://swreview.invalid/__backend` — the page's own origin under the proxy prefix — and `port` is carried for the pane's display and for diagnostics only, with no page building a URL from it. Then decide whether the remodel contract row says the same thing (see must_fix #1).
- **docs/pane-backend-proxy.md:143** (origin-csp) - Two accuracy slips in a document that is otherwise exact. (a) Line 143 says `BackendProxyTests.cs` is '13 cases'; it is 18 — 6 `[Fact]` plus 12 `[InlineData]` across 3 `[Theory]`. The neighbouring counts are both exact (BackendProxyHandlerTests 9, PageFileServerTests 58, and I counted all three), so this is the one stale number and it is in the table describing the file this change edited. (b) Line 224 says the CSP is 'byte-identical (the page contract tests assert that)'. The tests assert normalized equality: ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:180, ModelCheckPageInjectionTests.cs:78 and RemodelPageContractTests.cs:203 all compare through a `Normalize()` that trims each directive and collapses internal whitespace. Byte identity does hold today — I compared the raw bytes of the contract's tag and all four index.html tags and they match exactly — but the suite would not catch a page that drifted to `connect-src  'self'`.
  - Fix: Correct the count to 18, and either say 'identical directive-for-directive (the page contract tests normalize whitespace before comparing)' or tighten the three tests to `Assert.Equal(expected, actual)` on the raw capture. I'd take the doc edit: normalization is the right call for a policy string spread across markdown and HTML, and the doc is what is wrong.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:233** (origin-csp) - DRY, pre-existing, reporting not recommending. `ContractCsp()` and `Normalize()` are copied verbatim into three files — ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs:233-244, ModelCheckPageInjectionTests.cs:132-148, RemodelPageContractTests.cs:915-944 — and TerminalPageContractTests.cs:604-648 carries a fourth spelling of the same idea (`CspMeta`/`CspContent`/`CspTag`), which is also why the Terminal page is checked against the Review page rather than against the contract. One rule, four implementations. The chain does close (Review/Model/Remodel each compare to the contract, Terminal compares to Review), so the CSP is correctly pinned everywhere — this is maintenance burden, not a hole. Only ReviewPageInjectionTests.cs is touched by this change, so under the surgical-changes rule I am flagging it rather than folding it into this task.
  - Fix: Separate change: one `PageCspContract` helper next to ReviewPageFiles, used by all four contract test classes, with Terminal comparing to the contract like the others instead of to the Review page. Worth doing when something next touches CSP; not worth widening this diff for.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/PageFileServer.cs:64** (origin-csp) - `.woff2` is provisioned for a policy that would refuse it. The owner's decision names woff2 in the closed content-type list, so it is there correctly — but no page ships or references a font (`@font-face` and `url(` return nothing across app.css, check.css, remodel.css, term.css and vendor/xterm.css), and the contract CSP is `default-src 'none'` with no `font-src`, so a woff2 served by this list is blocked by the page's own policy before it can be used. Same shape, less sharply, for `.json`, `.svg`, `.png` and `.ico`: all named by the decision, none currently referenced — though `img-src 'self' data:` at least permits three of them, which is what makes woff2 the odd one out.
  - Fix: Keep the entry (the decision is binding and a font is a plausible next asset), and add half a sentence to the list's doc comment or the contract's file-server paragraph saying woff2 is stocked ahead of a `font-src` the CSP does not yet grant — so the first person to ship a font finds the CSP change waiting for them instead of debugging a silent block. No code change.
- **extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/BackendProxyPageTests.cs:392** (origin-csp) - Stale instruction in a failure message that is meant to be read years from now. `AssertNoEventsWereRaised` tells whoever trips it to 'finish the proxy as that document's section 2 describes and delete these two tests'. The proxy is finished — option B shipped, and section 4 is what a reader would need. The message is the only guidance a future engineer gets at the moment a WebView2 update starts raising the event for folder-mapped hosts, which is precisely when it matters that the message is current. ('These two tests' is correct: two tests call this helper.)
  - Fix: Reword to: the mapping no longer swallows the event, so `SetVirtualHostNameToFolderMapping` is a choice again rather than a trap — delete these two tests and read section 4 before reintroducing it, since the file server also pays for the sandbox the mapping provided.

## Feature 006 spec review should-fix (2026-09-17)

- **C:\Users\Cole\source\repos\smart_SW\specs\006-standards-check\spec.md** (Differences from the Macro, row (v) (line 265)) - Row (v) lists the data-card check among the checks this feature makes document-scoped and explains it by the macro's sub-assembly sequence dropping checks - but the macro's sub-assembly sequence does run the data-card check. The row states a difference that does not exist and thereby weakens the table SC-015 is judged against.
  - Fix: Remove "and the data-card check" from row (v). The real data-card differences are already rows (l) and (m) plus FR-005's library-skip rule.
- **C:\Users\Cole\source\repos\smart_SW\specs\006-standards-check\spec.md** (FR-011 (line 193); Differences from the Macro table (lines 242-267)) - The transparency check is the one assembly check the macro deliberately does NOT skip suppressed components for, so under FR-004 a suppressed component carrying a transparency override becomes an unresolved row where the macro produced an error finding. FR-011 says nothing about suppression and no Differences row covers it (row (c) covers only the hidden check, in the opposite direction).
  - Fix: State the suppression rule in FR-011 explicitly and name check 6 in whichever Differences row records the suppressed-component decision taken for finding 4 above.
- **C:\Users\Cole\source\repos\smart_SW\specs\006-standards-check\spec.md** (Edge case (line 130), FR-005 (line 184)) - "The longest matching prefix wins" is stated globally over "profile prefixes", but the profile carries four independent lists (skip, one-mate, two-mate, sketch-exempt). A single path routinely matches prefixes in two different lists - the macro's own constants do exactly this, with an electrical library folder on the skip list and an electrical wires sub-folder on the two-mate list - and a global longest-wins rule silently discards one of the two classifications.

- **specs/006-standards-check/spec.md** (FR-032 (line 223), FR-034 (line 225)) - Both requirements describe a transport that no longer exists in this repository. The pages no longer reach the loopback backend and are no longer served from a folder-mapped virtual host: the mapping was removed and every page file and every backend call is served by the host through one `WebResourceRequested` handler at the page's own origin. A Standards page built to "call the backend itself" with a loopback origin, under a `connect-src http://127.0.0.1:*` policy, is the exact failure the handoff round fixed - every call answered by the web filter.
  - Fix: Say the page calls the backend at its own origin under the `/__backend` prefix through the host's proxy, with the token from `init`, and that its files are served by the host's page file server under `connect-src 'self'` - and note that the `/events` route is not proxyable, which this tab does not need.
- **specs/006-standards-check/spec.md** (FR-032 (line 223)) - The `standards.start` message has no payload specified, while the row it mirrors carries one, and the verb is renamed from the sibling's (`check.start`/`check.extracted`) without saying why. A host implementing this cannot tell whether the message carries a scope, a profile path, or nothing.
  - Fix: Give `standards.start` an explicit payload (`{}` if it needs none) and state whether the family keeps the sibling's verb with a family field or its own verb, and why.
- **specs/006-standards-check/spec.md** (FR-030 (line 221), Edge Cases (line 166)) - The run-folder name is left to "the run-folder helper's existing naming behaviour", but that name is the check id an engineer sees, types and reads back, and both families will now produce identically shaped ids under one run root.   - Fix: Decide and state the suffix (recommended `-standards`, so the run root is readable by eye and the family filter of the other tab is trivial), and say that the family field remains authoritative for the result shape.
- **specs/006-standards-check/spec.md** (FR-033 (line 224), FR-001 (line 177)) - The route set never says how the backend learns which profile to grade against. The command line has `--profile`; the route has nothing, and FR-001 makes the path a setting without naming which process reads it. The tab could therefore grade against a different profile than the command line does on the same package.
  - Fix: State that the start route's body carries the profile path (or that the backend resolves the same setting the command line's default resolves), and require the check record to store the profile path and content hash so a reader can tell which profile a result was graded against - Key Entities already promises that.
- **specs/006-standards-check/spec.md** (FR-001 (line 177), US3 AS-15 (line 91)) - The profile path "MUST be a setting", but no surface is named for editing it. An engineer whose profile lives elsewhere has no way to point the tab at it.
  - Fix: Say where the setting is edited - the settings file and the quickstart only (recommended, so no existing page changes), or the Review settings form, in which case AS-15's "unchanged" must be qualified - and say what the refusal tells the engineer to do (the quickstart path).
- **specs/006-standards-check/spec.md** (FR-011 (line 193)) - FR-011 contains two MUSTs that collide for the same component: a component with no appearance override "MUST be checked coverage", and the check "MUST be unresolved... for every component until the workstation probe... has settled" the polarity.   - Fix: Scope the probe clause: "unresolved for every component that carries an override, until the probe has settled the polarity", leaving components with no override in checked coverage.
